<a id="demouijsinternalcomponentschatjs"></a>
<details>
<summary><strong><code>demo/ui/js/internal/components/chat.js</code></strong> — 470 lines, 13 functions</summary>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/chat.js`

**470 lines** | 13 functions/classes

This module implements the `Chat` component, which is responsible for all client-side rendering of messages within a chat interface stream.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to take structured message data (content, sender role, metadata) and transform it into correctly formatted, styled HTML elements that appear in the chat window (`#chat-stream`). It manages the visual presentation logic for various types of conversational turns.

**What It Does:**
1.  **Message Rendering:** It provides dedicated functions to render different message roles (e.g., `user`, `assistant`, `system`, `ephemeral`), each with unique styling, sender labels, and metadata headers (like node IDs or status indicators).
2.  **Content Formatting:** It integrates markdown parsing (`marked`) for general text formatting. Crucially, it extends this functionality to support advanced LaTeX math rendering using KaTeX, allowing the display of both inline ($\text{math}$) and block ($\$\$ \text{math} \$\$$) equations.
3.  **Streaming Support:** It includes logic to create placeholder elements for messages that arrive incrementally (streaming), allowing the content to update live as tokens are received.
4.  **User Experience:** It implements an `autoScroll` function, ensuring the chat view automatically scrolls down only when the user is already near the bottom of the conversation, improving UX during active chatting.

**Role in the Project:**
The component acts as the presentation layer for the entire chat history and real-time interaction stream. By handling complex rendering tasks—such as markdown parsing, mathematical equation display, and dynamic scrolling—it ensures that the user interface accurately and robustly displays conversational data regardless of its source or complexity.

### Functions & Methods

#### `autoScroll()` — function, line 11

* **Purpose:** Automatically scrolls a designated chat container element (`streamEl`) to its bottom if the user's current view is already near the bottom, ensuring new content is visible.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** void
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function relies on an external variable `streamEl` (expected to be a scrollable DOM element). It uses a fixed threshold of 80 pixels; if the user is within this distance of the bottom, the scroll position is forced to the maximum height. If `streamEl` is falsy, the function exits immediately.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function autoScroll() {
    if (!streamEl) return;
    var threshold = 80;
    var atBottom = (streamEl.scrollHeight - streamEl.scrollTop - streamEl.clientHeight) < threshold;
    if (atBottom) {
      streamEl.scrollTop = streamEl.scrollHeight;
    }
  }
```

</details>

#### `init()` — function, line 20

* **Purpose:** Initializes the chat component by setting up a reference to the message stream element and registering custom markdown extensions to correctly render LaTeX math using KaTeX.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** void
* **Behavioral Notes:** Requires `marked` and `katex` libraries to be globally available in the scope; registers two custom marked extensions (`mathBlock` for display math and `mathInline` for inline math) that intercept markdown tokens and render them using KaTeX. If KaTeX rendering fails, the raw LaTeX code is displayed instead of throwing an error.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init() {
    streamEl = document.getElementById('chat-stream');

    // === KaTeX + marked integration ===
    // Registers a marked extension that renders LaTeX math blocks.
    // $$...$$ → display math, $...$ → inline math.
    if (typeof marked !== 'undefined' && typeof katex !== 'undefined') {
      var mathExtension = {
        extensions: [
          {
            name: 'mathBlock',
            level: 'block',
            start: function (src) { var m = src.match(/\$\$/); return m ? m.index : -1; },
            tokenizer: function (src) {
              var match = src.match(/^\$\$([\s\S]+?)\$\$/);
              if (match) {
                return { type: 'mathBlock', raw: match[0], text: match[1].trim() };
              }
            },
            renderer: function (token) {
              try {
                return '<div class="math-block">' + katex.renderToString(token.text, { displayMode: true, throwOnError: false }) + '</div>';
              } catch (e) {
                return '<div class="math-block"><code>' + token.text + '</code></div>';
              }
            }
          },
          {
            name: 'mathInline',
            level: 'inline',
            start: function (src) { var m = src.match(/\$/); return m ? m.index : -1; },
            tokenizer: function (src) {
              var match = src.match(/^\$([^\$\n]+?)\$/);
              if (match) {
                return { type: 'mathInline', raw: match[0], text: match[1].trim() };
              }
            },
            renderer: function (token) {
              try {
                return katex.renderToString(token.text, { displayMode: false, throwOnError: false });
  // ... (truncated)
```

#### `renderMessage(role, content, nodeId, animate)` — function, line 72

* **Purpose:** Renders a single message bubble into the chat stream container, constructing complex HTML elements based on the sender's role and content.
* **Inputs:**
    * `role` (string): The identity of the sender ('user', 'assistant', 'ephemeral', etc.). This determines the sender label text and specific UI styling/headers.
    * `content` (string): The textual or HTML content that should be displayed within the message body.
    * `nodeId` (string | null): An optional unique identifier for the message node, used to populate data attributes and specialized headers.
    * `animate` (boolean): If false, prevents CSS animations from being applied to the newly rendered message element.
* **Outputs:** None (void). The function modifies the DOM directly by appending the constructed message elements to the chat stream container (`streamEl`).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Requires a globally or locally scoped variable `streamEl` (the target container element) to be defined; if it is falsy, the function exits immediately.
    * The rendering logic is highly conditional based on the `role`. For example, 'assistant' and 'ephemeral' roles trigger specific header structures containing "wire-pill" elements that are not rendered for other roles.
    * This function causes a side effect by mutating the Document Object Model (DOM).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function renderMessage(role, content, nodeId, animate) {
    if (!streamEl) return;

    const wrapper = document.createElement('div');
    wrapper.className = 'message message--' + role;
    if (animate === false) wrapper.style.animation = 'none';
    if (nodeId) wrapper.dataset.nodeId = nodeId;

    // Sender label
    const sender = document.createElement('div');
    sender.className = 'message__sender';
```

#### `renderSystem(content, opts)` — function, line 144

* **Purpose:** Renders a system message into the chat stream element (`streamEl`), handling markdown formatting and displaying truncation indicators if necessary.
* **Inputs:**
    * `content` (string): The text content of the system message to be displayed.
    * `opts` (object, optional): Configuration options for rendering the message. Expected properties include:
        * `truncated` (boolean): If true, displays a truncation indicator below the message body.
        * `nodeId` (string, optional): The ID of the node that generated the truncated content, used in the indicator text.
* **Outputs:** None (It performs DOM manipulation).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Requires the global variable `streamEl` to be defined and available for appending the rendered message.
    * If markdown rendering library (`marked`) is available and the content contains common formatting characters (`**`, `$$`, `` ` ``, `# `), the content will be processed as markdown; otherwise, it is treated as plain text.
    * Appends a truncation indicator if `opts.truncated` is true, including the provided `nodeId`.
    * Calls an external function `autoScroll()` after successfully appending the message to ensure the chat view scrolls to the bottom.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function renderSystem(content, opts) {
    if (!streamEl) return;
    opts = opts || {};
    const wrapper = document.createElement('div');
    wrapper.className = 'message message--system';
    const body = document.createElement('div');
    body.className = 'message__body';

    // Render markdown if content looks like it has formatting
    if (typeof marked !== 'undefined' && (content.includes('**') || content.includes('$$') || content.includes('`') || content.includes('# '))) {
      body.className = 'message__body markdown';
      body.innerHTML = marked.marked(content);
    } else {
      body.textContent = content;
    }

    // Show truncation indicator if content was cut
    if (opts.truncated) {
      const indicator = document.createElement('div');
      indicator.style.cssText = 'font-family:var(--font-mono);font-size:9px;color:var(--color-accent);margin-top:6px;';
      indicator.textContent = '\u2026 truncated — full content in memory (Node ' + (opts.nodeId || '?') + ')';
      body.appendChild(indicator);
    }

    wrapper.appendChild(body);
    streamEl.appendChild(wrapper);
    autoScroll();
  }
```

</details>

#### `removeArchived(opts)` — function, line 304

* **Purpose:** Removes archived messages from the chat stream element based on provided node ID criteria while animating the removal process.
* **Inputs:**
    * `opts` (`object`): Configuration options for determining which nodes to keep or remove. Can contain one of the following properties:
        * `keepStart` (`number`, optional): The starting node ID (inclusive) that should be kept in the stream.
        * `keepEnd` (`number`, optional): The ending node ID (inclusive) that should be kept in the stream.
        * `fromNodeId` (`number`, optional): A specific node ID used to determine which nodes preceding it should be removed.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This function performs direct DOM manipulation and relies heavily on CSS transitions for visual effects.
    * Messages marked for removal are faded out and translated using `setTimeout` (300ms delay) before being physically removed from the DOM (`el.remove()`).
    * The window boundary divider (`.window-boundary`) is also scheduled for removal after the animation completes.
    * If `keepStart` and `keepEnd` are provided, all nodes with IDs less than `keepStart` or greater than `keepEnd` are removed.
    * If only `fromNodeId` is provided, all nodes with IDs less than `fromNodeId` are removed.
    * The function uses a final `setTimeout` block to handle post-animation cleanup and insertion of informational markers (though the marker logic is truncated in the source).

```javascript
  function renderSources(results) {
    if (!streamEl || !results || results.length === 0) return;
    var el = document.createElement('div');
    el.className = 'message message--system';
    el.style.animation = 'fadeSlideIn 0.3s ease both';
    var body = document.createElement('div');
    body.className = 'message__body sources-list';
    body.style.fontFamily = 'var(--font-mono)';
    body.style.fontSize = '11px';
    body.style.lineHeight = '1.8';

    var html = '<strong style="color:var(--color-accent);">Sources</strong><br>';

      html += '<a href="' + r.url + '" target="_blank" rel="noopener" style="color:var(--color-accent); text-decoration:none; display:block;">';
      html += '<span style="color:var(--color-accent);">[' + (i + 1) + ']</span> ' + r.title;
      html += '</a>';
    });
    body.innerHTML = html;
    el.appendChild(body);
    streamEl.appendChild(el);
    autoScroll();
  }
```

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/docgen.js`

**66 lines** | 2 functions/classes

This module provides the client-side interface for initiating and monitoring automated documentation generation (DocGen).

**Purpose:** Its primary purpose is to allow users to select a specific project folder and trigger a background process that generates comprehensive documentation files.

**What it Does:**
1.  It initializes by attaching an event listener to a dedicated "DocGen" button.
2.  When triggered, it prompts the user for a relative folder path.
3.  It sends this path via a streaming POST request to the `/api/docgen` endpoint.
4.  Crucially, it processes Server-Sent Events (SSE) from the response stream in real time. It interprets these events to provide live feedback: updating the top bar with progress status, displaying success messages upon completion (including file counts), or reporting any errors encountered during the process.

**Role in the Project:** This component acts as a user-facing utility wrapper. It manages the entire lifecycle of the documentation generation feature—from user input and API communication to real-time UI feedback—ensuring a smooth, observable experience for the developer.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init()` — function, line 7

* **Purpose:** Initializes the document generation feature by attaching a click event listener to the element with the ID 'btn-docgen'.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function must be called after the DOM is fully loaded. It attaches a persistent event listener, meaning if `init` is called multiple times without cleanup, multiple listeners will be attached to the button.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init() {
    document.getElementById('btn-docgen').addEventListener('click', startDocGen);
  }
```

</details>

#### `startDocGen()` — function, line 11

* **Purpose:** Initiates an asynchronous documentation generation process by sending a specified folder path to a backend API endpoint and streaming progress updates to the user interface.
* **Inputs:** None (It prompts the user for input via `prompt('Enter the folder path...')`).
* **Outputs:** None (Returns implicitly when the process completes or is cancelled).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function requires the user to manually enter a relative folder path using a browser prompt. If no path is provided, the function exits immediately.
    * It performs multiple side effects by updating the UI: `Chat.renderSystem` and `Topbar.setStatus`.
    * It uses `fetch` with streaming capabilities (`response.body.getReader()`) to consume Server-Sent Events (SSE) from `/api/docgen`.
    * The function processes incoming SSE events, specifically looking for `progress` updates (to update the top bar status) and a final `done` event (to display a completion message in the chat).
    * The process is asynchronous (`async`) and handles streaming data chunk by chunk.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async function startDocGen() {
    var folderPath = prompt('Enter the folder path to document:\n(relative to project root, e.g. "demo" or "src/core")');
    if (!folderPath) return;

    Chat.renderSystem('📝 DocGen starting: "' + folderPath + '"');
    Topbar.setStatus('active', 'DOCGEN');

    try {
      // Use fetch with streaming to read SSE events
      var response = await fetch('/api/docgen', {
        method: 'POST',
        headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
        body: JSON.stringify({ rootDir: folderPath })
      });

      var reader = response.body.getReader();
      var decoder = new TextDecoder();
      var buffer = '';

      while (true) {
        var chunk = await reader.read();
        if (chunk.done) break;

        buffer += decoder.decode(chunk.value, { stream: true });

        // Process complete SSE events
        var lines = buffer.split('\n\n');
        buffer = lines.pop(); // Keep incomplete event in buffer

        for (var i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
          var line = lines[i].trim();
          if (!line.startsWith('data: ')) continue;

          try {
            var event = JSON.parse(line.slice(6));

            if (event.type === 'progress') {
              Topbar.setStatus('active', 'DOCGEN: ' + event.data);
            } else if (event.type === 'done') {
              Chat.renderSystem('✓ DocGen complete: ' + event.data.filesDocumented + ' files, ' + event.data.totalFunctions + ' functions → DOCUMENTATION.md');
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

</details>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/ingest-modal.js`

**285 lines** | 8 functions/classes

This module, `IngestModal`, is responsible for handling the process of ingesting external data—either single files or entire code repositories—into the application's active memory store.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to provide a user interface and logic layer that allows users to upload content (files/folders) and convert that raw data into structured, searchable nodes within the system's in-memory knowledge base (`window._luminalMemory`).

**What It Does:**
1.  **Input Handling:** It manages two distinct ingestion modes:
    *   **File Upload:** Supports drag-and-drop or file selection for single files.
    *   **Repository Ingestion:** Utilizes the File System Access API to recursively traverse and select entire directories, filtering out common system folders (like `.git` or `node_modules`).
2.  **Processing:** It communicates with a backend API endpoint (`/api/ingest/file`) to upload file contents. The server processes this content and returns structured data chunks (nodes).
3.  **Memory Storage:** Upon successful retrieval, the component takes these returned nodes and stores them directly into the global in-memory structure, making the ingested information available for subsequent application use (e.g., querying or chat context).

**Role in the Project:**
The `IngestModal` acts as the critical data pipeline entry point. It bridges the gap between external file systems/uploads and the internal state management of the application. By reliably ingesting content into memory, it populates the knowledge base that powers the core functionality of the system.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init()` — function, line 15

* **Purpose:** Initializes all necessary event listeners to make the ingestion modal interactive, handling tab switching between file and repository sources, and setting up drag-and-drop functionality for file uploads.
* **Inputs:** None (Relies on specific DOM elements identified by IDs like `ingest-tab-file`, `ingest-panel-repo`, etc.)
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    *   **Tab Switching:** Clicking a tab updates the visual state (`.active` class) and controls the visibility of the corresponding content panel (setting `display: none` or `display: ''`).
    *   **File Drop Zone Interaction:** The dropzone area is configured to visually change its border color on `dragover` (indicating acceptance) and revert on `dragleave`.
    *   **File Input Trigger:** Clicking the dropzone element programmatically triggers the hidden file input field (`ingest-file-input`).
    *   **Drop Handling:** When files are dropped, the default browser behavior is prevented, the border color resets, and the function prepares to process the `dataTransfer.files` array (processing logic is truncated).

#### `walkDirectory(dirHandle, relPath, files)` — function, line 95

* **Purpose:** Recursively traverses a directory handle, collecting all files that match specified extension criteria into an array while skipping hidden or ignored directories/files.
* **Inputs:**
    * `dirHandle` (DirectoryHandle): The file system handle of the directory to start traversing from.
    * `relPath` (string): The relative path leading up to the current directory level.
    * `files` (Array<File>): An array that will be mutated and used to collect all found File objects.
* **Outputs:** Promise<void>
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This function mutates the `files` array passed as an argument, adding processed file objects to it.
    * Traversal is asynchronous (`async/await`).
    * Files are filtered based on three criteria: they must not start with a dot (`.`), their name must not be in `ignoreNames`, and their extension must be present in `validExts`.
    * Each collected file object has an additional property, `_relPath`, set to its full relative path within the directory structure.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async function walkDirectory(dirHandle, relPath, files) {
    for await (var entry of dirHandle.values()) {
      var name = entry.name;
      if (name.startsWith('.') || ignoreNames.indexOf(name) !== -1) continue;

      if (entry.kind === 'directory') {
        var subPath = relPath ? relPath + '/' + name : name;
        await walkDirectory(entry, subPath, files);
      } else if (entry.kind === 'file') {
        var ext = '.' + name.split('.').pop().toLowerCase();
        if (validExts.indexOf(ext) === -1) continue;
        var file = await entry.getFile();
        file._relPath = relPath ? relPath + '/' + name : name;
        files.push(file);
      }
    }
  }
```

</details>

#### `runIngest()` — function, line 113

* **Purpose:** Executes the data ingestion process by calling either `ingestFile` or `ingestRepo` based on the currently active tab, managing UI state throughout the operation.
* **Inputs:** None (Relies on global scope variables: `activeTab`, and external functions: `ingestFile`, `ingestRepo`).
* **Outputs:** Promise<void> - Resolves when the ingestion process completes or rejects if an unhandled error occurs during the cleanup phase.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This function heavily mutates the DOM by disabling/enabling the 'Ingest' button and updating the content and color of the element with ID `ingest-result`.
    * It uses a `try...catch...finally` block to ensure that regardless of success or failure, the ingest button is re-enabled and reset.
    * If an error occurs during ingestion, the result area (`ingest-result`) is explicitly styled red (`#ff6b6b`) and displays the error message.
    * The function assumes `activeTab` determines which specialized ingestion handler (`ingestFile` or `ingestRepo`) must be called.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async function runIngest() {
    var resultEl = document.getElementById('ingest-result');
    var btn = document.getElementById('ingest-run-btn');
    btn.disabled = true;
    btn.textContent = '⏳ Ingesting...';
    resultEl.textContent = '';

    try {
      if (activeTab === 'file') {
        await ingestFile(resultEl);
      } else {
        await ingestRepo(resultEl);
      }
    } catch (err) {
      resultEl.style.color = '#ff6b6b';
      resultEl.textContent = '✗ Error: ' + err.message;
    } finally {
      btn.disabled = false;
      btn.textContent = '▶ Ingest';
    }
  }
```

</details>

#### `ingestFile(resultEl)` — function, line 135

* **Purpose:** Handles the asynchronous process of uploading a selected file to an API endpoint and storing the resulting data nodes into global memory.
* **Inputs:**
    * `resultEl` (`HTMLElement`): The DOM element used to display status messages (success, failure, or loading).
* **Outputs:** Promise<void> - Does not return a value but resolves when the ingestion process is complete and the UI has been updated.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * **Side Effects:** Mutates the `resultEl` element's text content and style color throughout its execution to provide real-time feedback. It also mutates global state by calling `storeNodes(memory, data.nodes, selectedFile.name)`, which relies on `window._luminalMemory`.
    * **Error Handling:** Handles multiple failure states: no file selected, API errors (`data.error`), and uninitialized memory (`!memory`).
    * **File Type Detection:** Automatically adjusts the content type header for PDF files regardless of the file extension check.
    * **Dependencies:** Relies on external variables/functions `selectedFile` (the File object), `storeNodes`, and global state `window._luminalMemory`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async function ingestFile(resultEl) {
    if (!selectedFile) {
      resultEl.style.color = '#ff6b6b';
      resultEl.textContent = 'No file selected.';
      return;
    }

    resultEl.style.color = '#888';
    resultEl.textContent = 'Uploading and parsing...';

    var contentType = selectedFile.type || 'text/plain';
    if (selectedFile.name.endsWith('.pdf')) contentType = 'application/pdf';

    var buffer = await selectedFile.arrayBuffer();

    var res = await fetch('/api/ingest/file?filename=' + encodeURIComponent(selectedFile.name), {
      method: 'POST',
      headers: { 'Content-Type': contentType },
      body: buffer
    });

    var data = await res.json();
    if (data.error) {
      resultEl.style.color = '#ff6b6b';
      resultEl.textContent = '✗ ' + data.error;
      return;
    }

    // Store chunks as nodes in memory
    var memory = window._luminalMemory;
    if (!memory) {
      resultEl.style.color = '#ff6b6b';
      resultEl.textContent = '✗ Memory not initialized.';
      return;
    }

    var count = storeNodes(memory, data.nodes, selectedFile.name);
    resultEl.style.color = '#7fdbca';
    resultEl.textContent = '✓ Ingested ' + count + ' nodes from "' + selectedFile.name + '" (' + data.totalChars.toLocaleString() + ' chars)';

  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `ingestRepo(resultEl)` — function, line 187

* **Purpose:** Processes a list of selected files within a folder, reading their content and sending them sequentially to an ingestion API endpoint to extract data nodes.
* **Inputs:**
    * `resultEl` (HTMLElement): The DOM element used to display status messages during the ingestion process.
* **Outputs:** Promise<void> - Does not return a value but handles asynchronous operations for file processing and API calls.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * **Dependencies:** Requires the global variable `selectedFolder` (an array of File objects) and `window._luminalMemory` to be initialized and available in scope.
    * **Validation:** Checks if `selectedFolder` is empty; if so, it displays an error message on `resultEl`.
    * **Memory Check:** Checks for the existence of `window._luminalMemory`; if missing, it aborts and reports an error.
    * **File Filtering:** Files are skipped if their text length is less than 30 characters or greater than 50,000 characters.
    * **API Interaction:** For each valid file, it reads the content as text, sends a POST request to `/api/ingest/file`, and expects JSON data containing an array of `nodes`.
    * **State Mutation:** Updates the `resultEl`'s text content and color throughout the process (e.g., 'No folder selected.', 'Ingesting X files...', or status updates).
    * **Error Handling:** Uses a `try...catch` block around file processing, allowing it to silently skip unreadable files without crashing the entire ingestion process.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async function ingestRepo(resultEl) {
    if (!selectedFolder || selectedFolder.length === 0) {
      resultEl.style.color = '#ff6b6b';
      resultEl.textContent = 'No folder selected.';
      return;
    }

    resultEl.style.color = '#888';
    resultEl.textContent = 'Ingesting ' + selectedFolder.length + ' files...';

    // Read all files from the selected folder via File System Access API
    var memory = window._luminalMemory;
    if (!memory) {
      resultEl.style.color = '#ff6b6b';
      resultEl.textContent = '✗ Memory not initialized.';
      return;
    }

    var allChunks = [];
    var filesProcessed = 0;

    for (var i = 0; i < selectedFolder.length; i++) {
      var file = selectedFolder[i];
      try {
        var text = await file.text();
        if (text.length < 30 || text.length > 50000) continue;

        var res = await fetch('/api/ingest/file?filename=' + encodeURIComponent(file._relPath || file.name), {
          method: 'POST',
          headers: { 'Content-Type': 'text/plain' },
          body: text
        });
        var data = await res.json();
        if (data.nodes && data.nodes.length > 0) {
          allChunks.push(...data.nodes);
          filesProcessed++;
        }
      } catch (e) { /* skip unreadable */ }
    }

  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `storeNodes(memory, nodes, source)` — function, line 253

* **Purpose:** Processes an array of content chunks, formats them with source metadata, and stores them into a memory object for indexing and retrieval.
* **Inputs:**
    * `memory` (Object): The knowledge base or state management object containing the `chain` and `bm25` index structures.
    * `nodes` (Array<Object>): An array of content chunks, where each chunk must contain at least a `heading` and a `content` property.
    * `source` (String): A string identifying the source from which the nodes are being ingested.
* **Outputs:** Number: The total count of nodes that were successfully processed and stored in memory.
* **Behavioral Notes:** Filters out any node chunk where the `content` is null, undefined, or less than 20 characters long. Mutates the provided `memory` object by appending content to its internal chain and adding it to the BM25 index. Logs a summary message to the console detailing the number of stored nodes.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function storeNodes(memory, nodes, source) {
    var count = 0;
    for (var i = 0; i < nodes.length; i++) {
      var chunk = nodes[i];
      if (!chunk.content || chunk.content.length < 20) continue;
      var nodeContent = '[Ingested: ' + source + '] ' + chunk.heading + '\n' + chunk.content;
      var node = memory.chain.append('system', nodeContent);
      memory.bm25.add(node);
      count++;
    }
    console.log('[Ingest] Stored ' + count + ' nodes from "' + source + '"');
    return count;
  }
```

</details>

#### `saveState(memory)` — function, line 267

* **Purpose:** Saves the current application state contained within the provided memory object to a backend API endpoint (`/api/state/save`).
* **Inputs:**
    * `memory` (Object): The state container instance that must implement an `export()` method returning serializable data.
* **Outputs:** None (The operation is asynchronous and fire-and-forget).
* **Behavioral Notes:** Network requests are made using `fetch`, but any errors during the process are caught and silently ignored, making the save operation non-critical to the application flow.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function saveState(memory) {
    try {
      fetch('/api/state/save', {
        method: 'POST',
        headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
        body: JSON.stringify(memory.export())
      });
    } catch (e) { /* non-critical */ }
  }
```

</details>

#### `formatSize(bytes)` — function, line 277

* **Purpose:** Converts a raw byte count into a human-readable string format using appropriate units (B, KB, or MB).
* **Inputs:**
    * `bytes` (number): The size value in bytes to be formatted.
* **Outputs:** string: The formatted size representation (e.g., "1.5 MB").
* **Behavioral Notes:** Uses fixed precision (`toFixed(1)`) for KB and MB outputs, rounding the displayed value to one decimal place.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function formatSize(bytes) {
    if (bytes < 1024) return bytes + ' B';
    if (bytes < 1024 * 1024) return (bytes / 1024).toFixed(1) + ' KB';
    return (bytes / (1024 * 1024)).toFixed(1) + ' MB';
  }
```

</details>

</details>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/input.js`

**48 lines** | 4 functions/classes

This module is an **Input Component** responsible for managing user interaction within a messaging or chat interface.

**Purpose:** Its primary goal is to provide controlled access to the text input area and handle the logic for sending messages.

**What It Does:**
1.  **Initialization:** It sets up event listeners on the dedicated text area and send button when initialized.
2.  **Input Handling:** It automatically resizes the textarea as the user types, ensuring optimal display up to a certain limit.
3.  **Sending Logic:** It captures messages when the user clicks the send button or presses 'Enter' (unless Shift is held). Upon sending, it clears the input field and executes a provided callback function with the message content.
4.  **State Management:** It provides methods to programmatically disable or enable the entire component, controlling both the send button state and focusing the text area.

**Role in the Project:** It acts as the single source of truth for user-generated input, abstracting away the complexities of DOM manipulation, event listening, and message submission logic from other parts of the application.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init(onSend)` — function, line 7

* **Purpose:** Initializes the input component by attaching necessary event listeners to handle sending messages and automatically resizing the text area based on content.
* **Inputs:**
    * `onSend` (`function`): A callback function executed when the user attempts to send a message (e.g., clicking the send button or pressing Enter).
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Attaches a click listener to `#send-btn` calling `handleSend`.
    * Attaches a keydown listener to `#user-input`: If 'Enter' is pressed without Shift, and the component is not in pocket mode (`.input-box--pocket-mode`), it prevents default behavior and calls `handleSend`.
    * Attaches an input listener to `#user-input` that automatically resizes the textarea height to fit content, capped at a maximum of 200px.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init(onSend) {
    textareaEl = document.getElementById('user-input');
    sendBtnEl = document.getElementById('send-btn');
    onSendCallback = onSend;

    sendBtnEl.addEventListener('click', handleSend);
    textareaEl.addEventListener('keydown', function (e) {
      if (e.key === 'Enter' && !e.shiftKey) {
        // Skip if pocket mode is active (handled by pocket note system)
        if (document.querySelector('.input-box--pocket-mode')) return;
        e.preventDefault();
        handleSend();
      }
    });

    // Auto-resize textarea
    textareaEl.addEventListener('input', function () {
      textareaEl.style.height = 'auto';
      textareaEl.style.height = Math.min(textareaEl.scrollHeight, 200) + 'px';
    });
  }
```

</details>

#### `handleSend()` — function, line 29

* **Purpose:** Processes and sends the trimmed content of a designated textarea element, clearing the input field afterward.
* **Inputs:** None explicit, but relies on `textareaEl` (HTMLElement) for the input source and `onSendCallback` (Function) for handling the successful send event.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function will immediately exit if the content of the textarea is empty or only contains whitespace. Upon a successful send, it clears the textarea's value and resets its height to 'auto'.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function handleSend() {
    var msg = textareaEl.value.trim();
    if (!msg) return;
    textareaEl.value = '';
    textareaEl.style.height = 'auto';
    if (onSendCallback) onSendCallback(msg);
  }
```

</details>

#### `disable()` — function, line 37

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function disable() {
    if (sendBtnEl) sendBtnEl.disabled = true;
  }
```

</details>

#### `enable()` — function, line 41

* **Purpose:** Activates a UI component by enabling the send button and setting focus on the associated text area.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function safely checks if `sendBtnEl` and `textareaEl` exist before attempting to modify their properties, preventing runtime errors if one or both elements are not present in the DOM.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function enable() {
    if (sendBtnEl) sendBtnEl.disabled = false;
    if (textareaEl) textareaEl.focus();
  }
```

</details>

</details>

<a id="demouijsinternalcomponentsinspectmodaljs"></a>
<details>
<summary><strong><code>demo/ui/js/internal/components/inspect-modal.js</code></strong> — 81 lines, 3 functions</summary>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/inspect-modal.js`

**81 lines** | 3 functions/classes

This module implements an **Inspect Modal Component**, designed to provide a comprehensive, real-time visualization of the internal state and memory structure used by the system.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is debugging and introspection. It allows developers or users to visually inspect exactly what information the AI model "sees" at any given moment, including its context, retrieved knowledge, and underlying operational statistics.

**What it Does:**
When activated, the component opens a modal window and executes a detailed rendering process that compiles several distinct sections of memory data:

1.  **System Prompt:** Displays the core instructions guiding the AI's behavior.
2.  **Recall Buffer:** Shows relevant knowledge nodes retrieved from an external search (BM25), displaying their content, associated confidence scores, and a visual representation of their relevance.
3.  **Sliding Window:** Lists the most recent conversation turns or memory entries that are actively part of the immediate context.
4.  **Outside Window:** Displays older, but still relevant, nodes from the overall memory history.
5.  **Engine Statistics:** Provides quantitative metrics about the memory system's health and size (e.g., total number of nodes in the chain, BM25 index size, number of archived blocks).

**Role in the Project:**
This component serves as a critical diagnostic tool. By visualizing the flow of information—from the initial prompt to the retrieved context and finally to the active window—it helps developers debug memory retrieval failures, understand how context is being truncated or prioritized, and verify that the underlying memory engine (including search indexing) is functioning correctly.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init(memory)` — function, line 7

* **Purpose:** Initializes the inspection component by setting internal references to the provided memory data and the designated output display element.
* **Inputs:**
    * `memory`: Object - The memory state or data structure that will be inspected.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Sets module-scoped variables (`memoryRef` and `outputEl`). Requires that an HTML element with the ID 'inspect-output' exists in the DOM when this function is called.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init(memory) {
    memoryRef = memory;
    outputEl = document.getElementById('inspect-output');
  }
```

</details>

#### `open()` — function, line 12

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function open() {
    Modal.open('inspect');
    render();
  }
```

</details>

#### `render()` — function, line 17

* **Purpose:** Renders a comprehensive, structured view of the current conversational context—including system prompts, retrieved memory nodes (recall buffer), and recent conversation history (sliding window)—into a specified DOM element.
* **Inputs:**
    * `memoryRef`: An object containing all memory state and logic, including methods for accessing the node chain (`chain`), performing semantic search (`bm25`), retrieving the current context window (`getWindow`), and configuration settings (`config`).
    * `outputEl`: The target DOM element where the rendered context string will be displayed.
* **Outputs:** None (The function performs a side effect by updating the content of `outputEl`).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This function is responsible for formatting complex memory data into a human-readable, visually structured output using Unicode characters.
    * The rendered context follows a strict hierarchy: System Prompt $\rightarrow$ Recall Buffer (semantically retrieved nodes) $\rightarrow$ Sliding Window (most recent turns).
    * It calculates and displays a visual progress bar for each recalled node based on its normalized BM25 score.
    * If `memoryRef` or `outputEl` are null/undefined, the function exits immediately without rendering.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function render() {
    if (!memoryRef || !outputEl) return;

    var chain = memoryRef.chain.all();
    var windowNodes = memoryRef.getWindow();
    var windowIds = new Set(windowNodes.map(function (n) { return n.id; }));

    var lastTurn = chain.slice().reverse().find(function (n) { return n.role === 'turn' || n.role === 'user'; });
    var query = lastTurn ? (lastTurn.query || lastTurn.content || '') : '';
    var searchResults = query ? memoryRef.bm25.search(query, 10) : [];
    var recallCandidates = searchResults
      .filter(function (r) { return !windowIds.has(r.nodeId); })
      .slice(0, memoryRef.config.maxRetrievedNodes);

    var out = '';

    out += '\u2550\u2550\u2550 WHAT THE AI SEES THIS TURN \u2550\u2550\u2550\n\n';
    out += '\u2500\u2500 SYSTEM PROMPT \u2500\u2500\n  "' + memoryRef.config.systemPrompt + '"\n\n';

    out += '\u2500\u2500 RECALL BUFFER \u2500\u2500 ' + recallCandidates.length + ' nodes\n';
    if (recallCandidates.length === 0) {
      out += '  (empty)\n';
    } else {
      var maxScore = recallCandidates[0].score || 1;
      recallCandidates.forEach(function (r) {
        var node = memoryRef.chain.get(r.nodeId);
        if (!node) return;
        var norm = (r.score / maxScore).toFixed(2);
        var barFull = Math.round(norm * 10);
        var bar = '\u2588'.repeat(barFull) + '\u2591'.repeat(10 - barFull);
        var preview = (node.query || node.content || '').slice(0, 50).replace(/\n/g, ' ');
        out += '  [Node ' + node.id + '] ' + bar + ' ' + norm + ' "' + preview + '"\n';
      });
    }
    out += '\n';

    out += '\u2500\u2500 SLIDING WINDOW \u2500\u2500 ' + windowNodes.length + '/' + memoryRef.config.windowSize + ' nodes\n';
    windowNodes.forEach(function (node) {
      var preview = (node.query || node.content || '').slice(0, 55).replace(/\n/g, ' ');
      out += '  [Node ' + node.id + '] ' + node.role + ': "' + preview + '"\n';
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

</details>

<a id="demouijsinternalcomponentsmodaljs"></a>
<details>
<summary><strong><code>demo/ui/js/internal/components/modal.js</code></strong> — 40 lines, 4 functions</summary>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/modal.js`

**40 lines** | 4 functions/classes

This module serves as a centralized controller for managing the state and behavior of multiple modal dialogs within the application.

**Purpose:** Its primary purpose is to provide a consistent, reusable interface for opening, closing, and initializing various predefined modal components (such as confirmation, search, or settings modals).

**What it Does:**
1.  **Initialization (`init`):** It sets up event listeners for all defined modals, ensuring that clicking the close button or the backdrop area of any modal will correctly trigger its closure. It also listens globally to detect when the user presses the Escape key, which triggers a comprehensive close-all action.
2.  **Control:** It provides methods (`open`, `close`) to programmatically manage individual modals by adding or removing an "open" CSS class.
3.  **Global Management:** It includes a function (`closeAll`) that ensures all active modals are closed simultaneously.

**Role in the Project:** This module acts as a critical utility layer for UI components, abstracting away the direct DOM manipulation and event handling required for modal dialogs. By centralizing this logic, it guarantees consistent behavior across all parts of the application where a modal is used, improving maintainability and robustness.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init()` — function, line 7

* **Purpose:** Initializes all modals on the page by attaching event listeners for closing them via dedicated buttons, backdrops, or pressing the Escape key.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** void
* **Behavioral Notes:** Attaches multiple global event listeners: one click listener pair (button and backdrop) for every modal defined in `modalNames`, and a single document-level keydown listener that triggers `closeAll()` when the 'Escape' key is pressed. It assumes the existence of external functions like `close(name)` and `closeAll()`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init() {
    modalNames.forEach(function (name) {
      var closeBtn = document.getElementById('modal-' + name + '-close');
      var backdrop = document.getElementById('modal-' + name + '-backdrop');
      if (closeBtn) closeBtn.addEventListener('click', function () { close(name); });
      if (backdrop) backdrop.addEventListener('click', function () { close(name); });
    });

    document.addEventListener('keydown', function (e) {
      if (e.key === 'Escape') closeAll();
    });
  }
```

</details>

#### `open(name)` — function, line 20

* **Purpose:** Makes a specified modal component visible and active by adding an 'open' class to both its backdrop and container element.
* **Inputs:** name (string): The unique identifier used to locate the corresponding modal elements on the page.
* **Outputs:** void
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function modifies the global DOM state by manipulating CSS classes. It assumes that the modal structure follows specific ID patterns: `modal-[name]` for the main container and `modal-[name]-backdrop` for the backdrop element. If either element is not found, the function will gracefully skip adding the class to that specific element.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function open(name) {
    var backdrop = document.getElementById('modal-' + name + '-backdrop');
    var modal = document.getElementById('modal-' + name);
    if (backdrop) backdrop.classList.add('open');
    if (modal) modal.classList.add('open');
  }
```

</details>

#### `close(name)` — function, line 27

* **Purpose:** Closes a specified modal and its associated backdrop element on the page by removing the 'open' class from both components.
* **Inputs:** name (string): The unique identifier used to construct the IDs of the modal and its backdrop.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function performs DOM manipulation; it removes CSS classes (`open`) from elements identified by constructing IDs using the provided `name`. It safely checks for the existence of both the backdrop and the modal before attempting class removal.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function close(name) {
    var backdrop = document.getElementById('modal-' + name + '-backdrop');
    var modal = document.getElementById('modal-' + name);
    if (backdrop) backdrop.classList.remove('open');
    if (modal) modal.classList.remove('open');
  }
```

</details>

#### `closeAll()` — function, line 34

* **Purpose:** Closes all currently tracked modals by iterating through the list of modal names and calling the close function for each one.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Relies on a globally or locally scoped array named `modalNames` containing the identifiers of open modals, and assumes the existence of an external `close(name)` function to handle the actual closing logic.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function closeAll() {
    modalNames.forEach(function (name) { close(name); });
  }
```

</details>

</details>

<a id="demouijsinternalcomponentsrecallfxjs"></a>
<details>
<summary><strong><code>demo/ui/js/internal/components/recall-fx.js</code></strong> — 62 lines, 3 functions</summary>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/recall-fx.js`

**62 lines** | 3 functions/classes

This module, `RecallFX`, is an auxiliary UI component designed to provide visual feedback when a memory recall event occurs within the application.

**Purpose:** Its primary purpose is to enhance the user experience by visually representing the act of recalling information, giving the system a "sci-fi" or technological feel during this state change.

**What It Does:**
1.  It initializes and manages a fixed container element positioned in the right whitespace of the screen.
2.  When its `fire` method is called with an array of recalled nodes, it first triggers a global visual effect (a canvas pulse).
3.  It then sequentially displays temporary "ghost node fragments" within its dedicated container. Each fragment shows partial information (ID and content) from the input nodes, styled with a flickering appearance, and automatically fades away after a short duration.

**Role in the Project:** It acts as an event handler for memory recall, ensuring that the user is visually informed about which data points are being accessed or recalled by the system.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init()` — function, line 10

* **Purpose:** Initializes and appends a fixed container element to the document body to serve as a dedicated display area for "ghost nodes."
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Appends a new `div` element with the ID 'recall-fx' directly to the `document.body`, setting its position, layout (flex column), and dimensions using inline CSS styles.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init() {
    // Create a fixed container in the right whitespace for ghost nodes
    containerEl = document.createElement('div');
    containerEl.id = 'recall-fx';
    containerEl.style.cssText =
      'position:fixed; right:6%; top:35%; z-index:3; ' +
      'display:flex; flex-direction:column; gap:6px; ' +
      'max-width:180px; pointer-events:none;';
    document.body.appendChild(containerEl);
  }
```

</details>

#### `fire(nodes)` — function, line 25

* **Purpose:** Triggers a visual "fire" effect by pulsing the canvas and displaying ghost node fragments based on the provided list of nodes.
* **Inputs:**
    * `nodes` (Array<Node>): A list of node objects used to calculate the positions for the visible ghost node fragments.
* **Outputs:** void
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function requires a globally accessible element, `containerEl`, to be defined; if it is falsy, no action is taken.
    * It checks for and calls a global `Canvas.pulse()` method if available.
    * Only the first four nodes (`Math.min(nodes.length, 4)`) are processed to display ghost node fragments using `showGhostNode`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function fire(nodes) {
    if (!containerEl) return;

    // Trigger canvas pulse
    if (typeof Canvas !== 'undefined' && Canvas.pulse) {
      Canvas.pulse();
    }

    // Show ghost node fragments
    for (var i = 0; i < Math.min(nodes.length, 4); i++) {
      showGhostNode(nodes[i], i * 120);
    }
  }
```

</details>

#### `showGhostNode(node, delay)` — function, line 39

* **Purpose:** Displays a temporary, styled "ghost" node preview element on the screen after a specified delay.
* **Inputs:**
    * `node` (Object): An object representing the source node, expected to have an `id`, and either a `query` or `content` property used for generating the preview text.
    * `delay` (Number): The time in milliseconds before the ghost node element is displayed.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This function manipulates the DOM by creating and appending a new `div` element to an external scope variable, `containerEl`.
    * The created element has specific CSS styling (orange/red color scheme) and an animation (`ghostFlicker`).
    * It uses two nested `setTimeout` calls: one for initial display delay, and another (2800ms later) to automatically remove the element from the DOM after the animation completes.
    * The preview text is truncated to 60 characters and newlines are replaced with spaces.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function showGhostNode(node, delay) {
    setTimeout(function () {
      var el = document.createElement('div');
      el.style.cssText =
        'font-family: "SF Mono", Consolas, monospace; font-size: 9px; ' +
        'color: rgba(255, 69, 0, 0.7); line-height: 1.4; ' +
        'padding: 6px 8px; border-left: 2px solid rgba(255, 69, 0, 0.4); ' +
        'opacity: 0; animation: ghostFlicker 2.5s ease-out forwards;';

      var preview = (node.query || node.content || '').slice(0, 60).replace(/\n/g, ' ');
      el.textContent = '[' + node.id + '] ' + preview;

      containerEl.appendChild(el);

      // Remove after animation completes
      setTimeout(function () {
        if (el.parentNode) el.parentNode.removeChild(el);
      }, 2800);
    }, delay);
  }
```

</details>

</details>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/search-modal.js`

**41 lines** | 3 functions/classes

This module implements the **Search Modal Component**, which manages the user interface and logic for searching within a knowledge base or memory structure.

**Purpose:** Its primary purpose is to provide a dedicated, interactive modal window for users to input queries and view structured search results derived from stored data nodes.

**Functionality:**
1.  **Initialization:** It sets up necessary DOM element references (input field and results display area) and attaches an event listener to the input field to trigger the search upon pressing Enter.
2.  **Opening:** It handles the visual opening of the search modal, ensuring the cursor is focused on the input field for immediate use.
3.  **Execution:** When triggered, it retrieves the user's query, passes this query to an external memory object (`memoryRef`) to perform a weighted search, and then formats the returned results. The displayed results include the node ID, relevance score, and a truncated preview of the content for each matching node.

**Role in the Project:** It acts as the primary interface layer between the user and the underlying data retrieval system (the `memoryRef`), ensuring that searches are executed correctly and that complex search output is presented to the user in a clean, readable format within the modal context.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init(memory)` — function, line 7

* **Purpose:** Initializes the search modal component by setting up references to necessary DOM elements and attaching an event listener that triggers the search execution upon pressing 'Enter'.
* **Inputs:**
    * `memory` (Object): The operational memory or state context required for the search functionality.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Requires two specific DOM elements with IDs `'search-input'` and `'search-results'` to exist in the document body.
    * Attaches a global event listener to the input field; pressing 'Enter' will call an external function named `execute()`.
    * Mutates external scope variables (`memoryRef`, `inputEl`, `resultsEl`) to store references used by other component methods.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init(memory) {
    memoryRef = memory;
    inputEl = document.getElementById('search-input');
    resultsEl = document.getElementById('search-results');

    inputEl.addEventListener('keydown', function (e) {
      if (e.key === 'Enter') execute();
    });
  }
```

</details>

#### `open()` — function, line 17

* **Purpose:** Opens the search modal component and programmatically sets focus to an input field within that modal after a short delay.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Relies on external dependencies (`Modal` object, `inputEl` element reference). Uses `setTimeout` to ensure the DOM has updated after opening the modal before attempting to focus the input field.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function open() {
    Modal.open('search');
    setTimeout(function () { inputEl.focus(); }, 100);
  }
```

</details>

#### `execute()` — function, line 22

* **Purpose:** Executes a search query against a memory reference and displays the formatted results, including scores and truncated content, into a designated output element.
* **Inputs:**
    * `inputEl`: HTMLElement - The input field element containing the user's search query.
    * `memoryRef`: Object - An object with a `search(query, k)` method used to retrieve relevant data nodes.
    * `resultsEl`: HTMLElement - The DOM element where the formatted search results will be displayed.
* **Outputs:** void - Modifies the content of the `resultsEl` element directly.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If the input query is empty or `memoryRef` is null/undefined, the function exits immediately without performing a search.
    * The function relies on external scoped variables (`inputEl`, `memoryRef`, `resultsEl`) to function.
    * It formats results by displaying the node ID, the calculated score (to three decimal places), and a truncated version of the content (up to 120 characters).
    * If no results are found, it sets the text content of `resultsEl` to an informative "No results" message.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function execute() {
    var query = inputEl.value.trim();
    if (!query || !memoryRef) return;

    var results = memoryRef.search(query, 8);

    if (results.length === 0) {
      resultsEl.textContent = 'No results for "' + query + '"';
      return;
    }

    resultsEl.textContent = results.map(function (r) {
      var content = (r.node && (r.node.query || r.node.content)) || '';
      return '[Node ' + r.nodeId + '] score: ' + r.score.toFixed(3) + '\n  ' + content.slice(0, 120).replace(/\n/g, ' ') + '...';
    }).join('\n\n');
  }
```

</details>

</details>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/selection-mode.js`

**232 lines** | 15 functions/classes

This module implements the **Selection Mode Component**, which manages a specialized user interface flow for selecting specific segments of chat history within the application.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to allow users to define boundaries—either a continuous range or a single branching point—within the displayed conversation stream, enabling them to decide what content should be kept active and what should be archived (or "cold stored").

**Functionality:**
1.  **Activation:** The module can be initiated in two modes:
    *   **Trim Mode:** Requires selecting two nodes to define a start and end point for keeping a continuous range of messages.
    *   **Branch Mode:** Requires selecting one node, which serves as the point from which all subsequent content is considered active.
2.  **Interaction:** When active, clicking any message in the chat stream selects it visually (with an outline and marker). The module enforces rules like preventing double-selection or exceeding the maximum allowed selections for the current mode.
3.  **Contextual Guidance:** It dynamically updates a context panel to guide the user, showing which mode is active, what action is required next, and details about the nodes already selected.
4.  **Completion & Confirmation:** Once enough selections are made (two for trim, one for branch), it triggers a confirmation modal. Upon proceeding through this modal, it executes a callback function provided by the calling code, passing the defined selection parameters (`keepStartId`, `keepEndId`, or `fromNodeId`).

**Role in the Project:**
This component acts as a state machine and UI controller for content curation. It abstracts complex interaction logic (click handling, visual feedback, sequential steps) into reusable functions, ensuring that the application can reliably capture user intent regarding data retention before proceeding with major archival actions.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init()` — function, line 18

* **Purpose:** Initializes internal references to key HTML elements required for the selection mode component by querying the DOM using specific IDs.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** void
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function relies on several global variables being assigned element references (`streamEl`, `contextEl`, etc.). If any of the targeted IDs are missing from the document, the corresponding variable will be set to `null`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init() {
    streamEl = document.getElementById('chat-stream');
    contextEl = document.getElementById('context-info');
    modeEl = document.getElementById('context-mode');
    messageEl = document.getElementById('context-message');
    selectionEl = document.getElementById('context-selection');
  }
```

</details>

#### `startTrim(callback)` — function, line 30

* **Purpose:** Initializes the component into "Trim Mode," restricting user selection to exactly two nodes for defining a content range.
* **Inputs:**
    * `callback` (Function): A function that will be executed when the trimming operation successfully completes.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Sets global state variables (`mode`, `maxSelections`, `selections`) to reflect the trim mode, resets existing selections, calls an internal activation method, and displays specific instructional context to the user.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function startTrim(callback) {
    mode = 'trim';
    maxSelections = 2;
    selections = [];
    onComplete = callback;
    activate();
    showContext('TRIM MODE', 'Click two nodes to define the range you want to keep. Everything outside will be archived.');
  }
```

</details>

#### `startBranch(callback)` — function, line 43

* **Inputs:**
    * `callback` (Function): A function that will be executed when the branching process is successfully completed.
* **Outputs:** void
* **Behavioral Notes:** Resets several internal state variables (`mode`, `maxSelections`, and `selections`) to reflect the new mode, activates the component's UI, and displays specific context instructions to the user.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function startBranch(callback) {
    mode = 'branch';
    maxSelections = 1;
    selections = [];
    onComplete = callback;
    activate();
    showContext('BRANCH MODE', 'Click a node to branch from. Everything before it will be archived.');
  }
```

#### `cancel()` — function, line 55

```javascript
    deactivate();
  }
```

</details>

#### `isActive()` — function, line 62

```javascript
    return mode !== null;
  }
```

</details>

#### `activate()` — function, line 68

* **Purpose:** Activates a selection mode UI state by modifying the DOM element, setting up click handling on the stream area, and listening for the Escape key globally.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Adds the class `chat-stream--selecting` to the `streamEl`. Attaches a click event listener (`handleClick`) to `streamEl`, and attaches a global keydown event listener (`handleEscape`) to the entire document. This function must be paired with a cleanup mechanism (e.g., an `deactivate` function) to remove these listeners and classes when selection mode is exited, preventing memory leaks.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function activate() {
    streamEl.classList.add('chat-stream--selecting');
    streamEl.addEventListener('click', handleClick);
    document.addEventListener('keydown', handleEscape);
  }
```

</details>

#### `deactivate()` — function, line 74

* **Purpose:** Resets the component's state, removes all selection visuals from the UI, and cleans up associated event listeners when exiting selection mode.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Clears internal state variables (`mode` and `onComplete`) by setting them to `null`.
    * Removes the active 'selecting' class from the main stream element (`streamEl`).
    * Detaches event listeners for both click events on the stream element and keydown (Escape) events globally.
    * Iterates through all stored selections, removing the visual selection class (`message--selected`) and any associated marker elements from the DOM.
    * Resets the internal `selections` array to an empty state.
    * Calls `hideContext()` to hide any displayed context information.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function deactivate() {
    mode = null;
    onComplete = null;

    streamEl.classList.remove('chat-stream--selecting');
    streamEl.removeEventListener('click', handleClick);
    document.removeEventListener('keydown', handleEscape);

    // Remove all selection visuals
    for (var i = 0; i < selections.length; i++) {
      var el = selections[i].element;
      el.classList.remove('message--selected');
      var marker = el.querySelector('.selection-marker');
      if (marker) marker.remove();
    }
    selections = [];

    // Hide context info
    hideContext();
  }
```

</details>

#### `handleEscape(e)` — function, line 95

* **Purpose:** Handles keyboard events, checking if the Escape key was pressed to trigger a deactivation routine.
* **Inputs:** e (Event object): The event object passed by the listener, used to check the value of `e.key`.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Calls an external function `deactivate()` only when the received event key matches `'Escape'`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function handleEscape(e) {
    if (e.key === 'Escape') {
      deactivate();
    }
  }
```

</details>

#### `handleClick(e)` — function, line 101

* **Purpose:** Handles a click event on a message element to select it, provided selection limits are not exceeded and the node has not already been selected.
* **Inputs:**
    * `e` (Event): The DOM event object triggered by the click.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Requires access to external variables: `selections` (array of selected nodes/objects), `maxSelections` (number limit for selections), and `mode` (string determining selection type, e.g., 'trim' or other).
    * It first finds the closest ancestor element with class `.message`. If none is found, it exits immediately.
    * Selection validation checks:
        1.  Ensures a valid `nodeId` can be extracted from the data attribute (`data-node-id`).
        2.  Prevents selecting the same node ID twice (checks against the `selections` array).
        3.  Checks if the total number of selections is less than `maxSelections`.
    * If selection is successful, it adds the class `message--selected` to the clicked element (`msgEl`).
    * It dynamically creates and appends a visual "selection marker" (containing an icon and label) to the selected message element. The content of this marker depends on the value of the external `mode` variable ('trim' vs. other).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function handleClick(e) {
    // Find the closest .message element
    var msgEl = e.target.closest('.message');
    if (!msgEl) return;

    // Get the node ID from the data attribute
    var nodeId = parseInt(msgEl.dataset.nodeId, 10);
    if (!nodeId && nodeId !== 0) return;

    // Don't allow selecting the same node twice
    for (var i = 0; i < selections.length; i++) {
      if (selections[i].nodeId === nodeId) return;
    }

    // Don't exceed max selections
    if (selections.length >= maxSelections) return;

    // Mark as selected
    msgEl.classList.add('message--selected');

    // Add marker button with icon + text
    var marker = document.createElement('div');
    marker.className = 'selection-marker';

    var icon = document.createElement('span');
    icon.className = 'selection-marker__icon';

    var label = document.createElement('span');

    if (mode === 'trim') {
      icon.textContent = '\u2702';  // ✂ scissors
      label.textContent = selections.length === 0 ? 'START' : 'END';
    } else {
      icon.textContent = '\u2387';  // ⎇ branch/fork
      label.textContent = 'FROM';
    }

    marker.appendChild(icon);
    marker.appendChild(label);

  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `completeSelection(data)` — function, line 164

* **Purpose:** Displays a confirmation modal to the user regarding the selected range or branch point, and executes a callback if the user confirms the action.
* **Inputs:**
    * `data` (Object): Contains selection details required for the message, including IDs like `keepStartId`, `keepEndId`, or `fromNodeId`.
* **Outputs:** None

* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This function relies on an external variable `mode` to determine if the action is a 'trim' (keeping a range) or a general branch ('Branch From Here').
    * It performs significant side effects by manipulating the DOM (`document.getElementById`) and interacting with a global `Modal` object to display confirmation messages.
    * The function registers event listeners (`onYes`, `onNo`) on the modal buttons, which are responsible for closing the modal and executing the final callback if confirmed.
    * If successful, it calls an external callback function (`onComplete`) passed implicitly or captured in scope, passing the original selection `data`.
    * The cleanup mechanism ensures that event listeners are removed regardless of whether the user confirms or cancels.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function completeSelection(data) {
    // Build confirmation info
    var title, message;

    if (mode === 'trim') {
      title = 'Trim — Keep Range';
      message = 'You selected Node ' + data.keepStartId + ' through Node ' + data.keepEndId + '. ' +
        'Everything before Node ' + data.keepStartId + ' and after Node ' + data.keepEndId + ' will be archived to cold storage. ' +
        'The selected range stays as your active session. Proceed?';
    } else {
      title = 'Branch From Here';
      message = 'You selected Node ' + data.fromNodeId + '. ' +
        'Everything before this node will be archived to cold storage. ' +
        'Node ' + data.fromNodeId + ' onward stays as your active session. Proceed?';
    }

    // Show confirm modal
    document.getElementById('confirm-title').textContent = title;
    document.getElementById('confirm-message').textContent = message;
    Modal.open('confirm');

    var proceedBtn = document.getElementById('confirm-proceed');
    var cancelBtn = document.getElementById('confirm-cancel');

    function cleanup() {
      proceedBtn.removeEventListener('click', onYes);
      cancelBtn.removeEventListener('click', onNo);
    }

    function onYes() {
      cleanup();
      Modal.close('confirm');
      var cb = onComplete;
      deactivate();
      if (cb) cb(data);
    }

    function onNo() {
      cleanup();
      Modal.close('confirm');
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

<a id="demouijsinternalcomponentssettingsmodaljs"></a>
<details>
<summary><strong><code>demo/ui/js/internal/components/settings-modal.js</code></strong> — 367 lines, 8 functions</summary>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/settings-modal.js`

**367 lines** | 8 functions/classes

This module implements the user interface for a comprehensive **Settings Modal**, providing users with centralized control over various system parameters that govern model behavior and connectivity.

**Purpose:**
The primary purpose is to present configuration settings in a structured, two-panel layout: a navigation sidebar listing different setting groups (e.g., Connection, Context, Memory), and a main content area displaying the specific controls for the selected group.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Initialization and Navigation:** It initializes by attaching event listeners to open the modal and dynamically builds the left-hand navigation panel using predefined schema groups.
2.  **Dynamic Rendering:** When a group is selected, it fetches relevant configuration data from a memory store and renders the corresponding content in the main body.
3.  **Group Handling:** It uses specialized rendering functions for different types of settings:
    *   **Connection Tab:** Detects available local backends (like `llama.cpp` and `Ollama`), displays their status, and allows users to select which backend and model should be active.
    *   **Static Fields:** Renders general configuration parameters (e.g., context window size) based on the schema definition for that group.
    *   **Sampling Tab:** Displays generation parameters pulled live from a connected server.

**Role in the Project:**
This module serves as the core configuration layer of the application. It abstracts complex system settings into an intuitive, navigable UI, allowing users to connect to different model backends, fine-tune operational parameters (like context length and memory limits), and manage generation behavior without direct code interaction.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init(memory)` — function, line 28

* **Purpose:** Initializes the settings modal component by setting up internal references to necessary DOM elements and attaching a click event listener to open the modal.
* **Inputs:**
    * `memory` (Object): The memory or state object that needs to be used throughout the settings module.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Sets global/scoped variables (`memoryRef`, `bodyEl`, `navEl`) by querying the DOM, making the component dependent on specific HTML element IDs existing in the document. Attaches a persistent click event listener to `#btn-settings` that calls the external `open` function.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init(memory) {
    memoryRef = memory;
    bodyEl = document.getElementById('settings-body');
    navEl = document.getElementById('settings-nav');
    document.getElementById('btn-settings').addEventListener('click', open);
  }
```

</details>

#### `open()` — function, line 35

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function open() {
    Modal.open('settings');
    render();
  }
```

</details>

#### `render()` — function, line 40

* **Purpose:** Renders the entire settings modal UI by populating the navigation sidebar with schema groups and initializing the main content area header based on the current active group state.
* **Inputs:** None (Relies on scoped variables: `memoryRef` for data, `navEl` for the navigation container, and `activeGroup` for the currently selected group.)
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function performs significant DOM manipulation by clearing and rebuilding the entire left navigation panel (`navEl`). It automatically attaches click event listeners to all rendered navigation items, which update the `activeGroup` state and trigger a re-render of the component. Groups are sorted alphabetically based on an explicit `order` property found in the schema metadata.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function render() {
    if (!memoryRef || !bodyEl || !navEl) return;

    var schemaData = memoryRef.settings.getSchema();
    var groups = schemaData.groups;
    var fields = schemaData.schema;
    var samplingSchema = schemaData.samplingSchema;
    var values = memoryRef.settings.getAll();

    // Build left nav
    var sortedGroups = Object.entries(groups).sort(function (a, b) { return a[1].order - b[1].order; });
    var navHtml = '';
    sortedGroups.forEach(function (entry) {
      var key = entry[0], meta = entry[1];
      var cls = key === activeGroup ? 'settings-nav__item settings-nav__item--active' : 'settings-nav__item';
      var icon = GROUP_ICONS[key] || '';
      navHtml += '<div class="' + cls + '" data-group="' + key + '">';
      navHtml += '<span class="settings-nav__icon">' + icon + '</span>';
      navHtml += '<span>' + meta.label + '</span>';
      navHtml += '</div>';
    });
    navEl.innerHTML = navHtml;

    navEl.querySelectorAll('.settings-nav__item').forEach(function (item) {
      item.addEventListener('click', function () {
        activeGroup = item.getAttribute('data-group');
        render();
      });
    });

    // Build right content
    var groupMeta = groups[activeGroup];
    var html = '';

    // Section header
    html += '<div class="settings-content__header">';
    html += '<div class="settings-content__title">' + groupMeta.label + '</div>';
    html += '<div class="settings-content__desc">' + (GROUP_DESCRIPTIONS[activeGroup] || '') + '</div>';
    html += '</div>';

  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `renderStaticFields(fields, groupValues)` — function, line 92

* **Purpose:** Renders a section of form fields into an HTML string, filtering only for fields that belong to the currently active group.
* **Inputs:**
    * `fields` (Object): An object where keys represent field identifiers and values contain metadata about those fields.
    * `groupValues` (Object): An object containing the current values associated with the form fields.
* **Outputs:** String, an HTML string containing the rendered static form elements for the active group.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function relies on a scoped variable, `activeGroup`, to determine which fields should be rendered. It also depends on an external helper function, `renderField()`, to generate the individual field markup. If no fields match the `activeGroup`, it returns an empty string.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function renderStaticFields(fields, groupValues) {
    var html = '';
    Object.entries(fields).forEach(function (entry) {
      var key = entry[0], meta = entry[1];
      if (meta.group !== activeGroup) return;
      html += renderField(key, meta, groupValues[key]);
    });
    return html;
  }
```

</details>
