#### `renderSamplingTab(samplingSchema, samplingValues)` — function, line 188

* **Purpose:** Generates the HTML structure for the sampling parameters tab within a settings modal based on detected schema and current values.
* **Inputs:**
    * `samplingSchema` (Object): An object containing metadata for all available sampling parameters, used to determine which fields should be rendered.
    * `samplingValues` (Object, optional): An object containing the currently selected or loaded values for the sampling parameters.
* **Outputs:** String, representing the complete HTML markup for the sampling tab content.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If `samplingSchema` is falsy (null/undefined), it renders a specific "No sampling parameters detected yet" empty state message and returns immediately.
    * It always includes a detection bar with a button to query the server, displaying status based on whether `samplingSchema` was provided.
    * For each parameter found in `samplingSchema`, it calls an external function (`renderField`) to generate the input field HTML.
    * If `samplingValues` is provided, it uses the corresponding value; otherwise, it defaults to the value specified in the schema's metadata (`meta.default`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function renderSamplingTab(samplingSchema, samplingValues) {
    var html = '';

    // Always show detect button
    html += '<div class="settings-detect-bar">';
    html += '<button class="btn-control" id="settings-detect">\u27F3 Detect from Server</button>';
    html += '<span class="settings-detect-bar__status" id="settings-detect-status">';
    if (samplingSchema) {
      html += '\u2713 ' + Object.keys(samplingSchema).length + ' parameters loaded';
    } else {
      html += 'Click to pull parameters from your running model';
    }
    html += '</span>';
    html += '</div>';

    // If no schema detected yet, show empty state
    if (!samplingSchema) {
      html += '<div style="text-align:center; padding:var(--space-32) 0; color:var(--color-text-muted);">';
      html += '<div style="font-size:var(--text-lg); margin-bottom:var(--space-8);">\u2699</div>';
      html += '<div>No sampling parameters detected yet.</div>';
      html += '<div style="font-size:var(--text-sm); margin-top:var(--space-4);">Hit detect to query your llama.cpp or Ollama server.</div>';
      html += '</div>';
      return html;
    }

    // Render detected params as fields
    Object.entries(samplingSchema).forEach(function (entry) {
      var key = entry[0], meta = entry[1];
      var value = samplingValues ? samplingValues[key] : meta.default;
      html += renderField(key, meta, value);
    });

    return html;
  }
```

</details>

#### `renderField(key, meta, value)` — function, line 223

* **Purpose:** Generates the HTML markup for a single settings field component based on its configuration metadata and current value.
* **Inputs:**
    * `key` (string): A unique identifier used to construct IDs and labels for the setting field.
    * `meta` (object): Configuration object defining the field's properties, including `label`, `type` ('number', 'boolean', 'select', etc.), constraints (`min`, `max`, `step`), default value, description, and options.
    * `value` (any): The current value of the setting that should be displayed or pre-selected in the rendered field.
* **Outputs:** string: A complete HTML string representing the structured settings field element.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function dynamically adjusts the generated HTML based on `meta.type`.
    * For 'number' types, it renders both a range slider (`<input type="range">`) and a number input box for better user experience.
    * Default constraints are applied: numbers default to min=0, max=100, and step=1 if not specified in `meta`.
    * If `value` is undefined or null, the function attempts to use `meta.default` (or 0 for number types) as a fallback value.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function renderField(key, meta, value) {
    var html = '';
    html += '<div class="settings-field">';
    html += '<label class="settings-field__label" for="setting-' + key + '">' + (meta.label || key) + '</label>';

    if (meta.type === 'number') {
      var displayVal = value !== undefined && value !== null ? value : (meta.default || 0);
      html += '<div class="settings-field__row">';
      html += '<input type="range" class="settings-range" id="setting-' + key + '-range"'
        + ' min="' + (meta.min !== undefined ? meta.min : 0) + '"'
        + ' max="' + (meta.max !== undefined ? meta.max : 100) + '"'
        + ' step="' + (meta.step || 1) + '"'
        + ' value="' + displayVal + '">';
      html += '<input type="number" class="settings-input settings-input--num" id="setting-' + key + '"'
        + ' min="' + (meta.min !== undefined ? meta.min : 0) + '"'
        + ' max="' + (meta.max !== undefined ? meta.max : 100) + '"'
        + ' step="' + (meta.step || 1) + '"'
        + ' value="' + displayVal + '">';
      html += '</div>';
    } else if (meta.type === 'boolean') {
      html += '<label class="settings-toggle">';
      html += '<input type="checkbox" id="setting-' + key + '"' + (value ? ' checked' : '') + '>';
      html += '<span class="settings-toggle__slider"></span>';
      html += '</label>';
    } else if (meta.type === 'select') {
      html += '<select class="settings-input" id="setting-' + key + '">';
      (meta.options || []).forEach(function (opt) {
        html += '<option value="' + opt + '"' + (opt === value ? ' selected' : '') + '>' + opt + '</option>';
      });
      html += '</select>';
    } else if (meta.type === 'textarea') {
      html += '<textarea class="settings-input settings-input--textarea" id="setting-' + key + '" rows="4">' + (value || '') + '</textarea>';
    } else {
      html += '<input type="text" class="settings-input" id="setting-' + key + '" value="' + (value || '') + '">';
    }

    if (meta.description) {
      html += '<span class="settings-field__hint">' + meta.description + '</span>';
    }
    html += '</div>';
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `wireHandlers(fieldsOrSampling)` — function, line 266

<a id="demouijsinternalcomponentsstatusmodaljs"></a>
<details>
<summary><strong><code>demo/ui/js/internal/components/status-modal.js</code></strong> — 48 lines, 3 functions</summary>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/status-modal.js`

**48 lines** | 3 functions/classes

This module implements a **Status Modal Component** responsible for displaying detailed system metrics.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to provide users with a real-time visual overview of the underlying system's operational status and resource consumption.

**What it Does:**
The component initializes by accepting a reference to the core memory management object. When activated, it renders a comprehensive set of metrics into a designated area on the page. These metrics include: total nodes, current memory usage versus limits (with a visual progress bar), overall utilization percentage, archive block counts, window size statistics, and details about internal indexes like BM25 and Bloom Filters.

**Role in the Project:**
It acts as a dedicated User Interface component for system monitoring. It abstracts the complex data retrieval from the core memory object into a clean, formatted, and easily consumable visual display within a modal window.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init(memory)` — function, line 7

* **Purpose:** Initializes the status modal component by setting up internal references to the memory state and the target DOM element for displaying status information.
* **Inputs:**
    * `memory` (Object): The global or application memory store object used by the component.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Sets two scoped variables (`memoryRef` and `bodyEl`) that are used throughout the component's lifecycle; it assumes an element with the ID 'status-body' exists in the DOM.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init(memory) {
    memoryRef = memory;
    bodyEl = document.getElementById('status-body');
  }
```

</details>

#### `open()` — function, line 12

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function open() {
    Modal.open('status');
    render();
  }
```

</details>

#### `render()` — function, line 17

* **Purpose:** Updates a designated DOM element with various system and memory status metrics by reading data from `memoryRef`.
* **Inputs:** None (Relies on scoped variables: `memoryRef` (object) containing methods like `status()`, `getWindow()`, and properties like `bm25`; `bodyEl` (HTMLElement) which is the target container for rendering).
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function modifies the DOM directly by setting `innerHTML` of `bodyEl`. It will exit early if either `memoryRef` or `bodyEl` are not defined. The rendered content includes calculated metrics, such as the progress bar width based on utilization percentage.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function render() {
    if (!memoryRef || !bodyEl) return;
    var s = memoryRef.status();

    bodyEl.innerHTML =
      '<div class="metric-card" style="margin-bottom:12px">' +
        '<div class="metric-card__label">Nodes <span class="metric-card__value">' + s.totalNodes + '</span></div>' +
      '</div>' +
      '<div class="metric-card" style="margin-bottom:12px">' +
        '<div class="metric-card__label">Memory <span class="metric-card__value">' + s.memoryUsageMB + ' MB / ' + s.limitMB + ' MB</span></div>' +
        '<div class="progress-bar"><div class="progress-bar__fill" style="width:' + Math.max(s.utilizationPercent, 0.5) + '%"></div></div>' +
      '</div>' +
      '<div class="metric-card" style="margin-bottom:12px">' +
        '<div class="metric-card__label">Utilization <span class="metric-card__value">' + s.utilizationPercent + '%</span></div>' +
      '</div>' +
      '<div class="metric-card" style="margin-bottom:12px">' +
        '<div class="metric-card__label">Archives <span class="metric-card__value">' + s.archiveBlocks + '</span></div>' +
      '</div>' +
      '<div class="metric-card" style="margin-bottom:12px">' +
        '<div class="metric-card__label">Window <span class="metric-card__value">' + memoryRef.getWindow().length + ' / ' + memoryRef.config.windowSize + '</span></div>' +
      '</div>' +
      '<div class="metric-card" style="margin-bottom:12px">' +
        '<div class="metric-card__label">BM25 Index <span class="metric-card__value">' + memoryRef.bm25.docCount + ' docs / ' + memoryRef.bm25.index.size + ' terms</span></div>' +
      '</div>' +
      '<div class="metric-card">' +
        '<div class="metric-card__label">Bloom Filter <span class="metric-card__value">' + memoryRef.bloom.itemCount + ' items</span></div>' +
      '</div>';
  }
```

<a id="demouijsinternalcomponentstoolbarjs"></a>
<details>
<summary><strong><code>demo/ui/js/internal/components/toolbar.js</code></strong> — 16 lines, 1 functions</summary>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/toolbar.js`

**16 lines** | 1 functions/classes

The `Toolbar` component is responsible for initializing and wiring up the functionality of a set of memory control buttons within the user interface. Its purpose is to act as an event handler layer that connects physical UI elements to core application logic.

It achieves this by accepting an external `actions` object during initialization. It then attaches specific click event listeners to predefined HTML button IDs (such as "trim," "branch," and "search"). When any of these buttons are clicked, the component delegates the actual execution of the required function call to the corresponding method provided by the passed `actions` object.

Its role in the project is to decouple the UI structure from the business logic, ensuring that button clicks trigger the correct, defined actions within the application.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init(actions)` — function, line 6

* **Purpose:** Initializes the toolbar functionality by attaching click event listeners to predefined buttons using handler methods provided in the `actions` object.
* **Inputs:**
    * actions (Object): An object containing action handlers, including methods like `onTrim`, `onBranch`, `onSearch`, `onInspect`, and `onStatus`.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function relies on the existence of specific HTML elements with IDs (`btn-trim`, `btn-branch`, etc.) in the DOM; if any element is missing, the corresponding event listener will not be attached. It modifies the global state by adding permanent click listeners to the document body.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init(actions) {
    document.getElementById('btn-trim').addEventListener('click', actions.onTrim);
    document.getElementById('btn-branch').addEventListener('click', actions.onBranch);
    document.getElementById('btn-search').addEventListener('click', actions.onSearch);
    document.getElementById('btn-inspect').addEventListener('click', actions.onInspect);
    document.getElementById('btn-status').addEventListener('click', actions.onStatus);
  }
```

</details>

</details>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/components/topbar.js`

**43 lines** | 4 functions/classes

This module implements the `Topbar` component, which is responsible for managing and displaying critical operational status and resource statistics across the application's user interface (specifically in the header and sidebar).

**Purpose:** To provide users with a centralized, real-time visual indicator of the system's current state and resource consumption.

**Functionality:**
1.  **Initialization (`init`):** Sets up references to the necessary DOM elements for status display.
2.  **Status Management (`setStatus`):** Updates the connection or operational status (e.g., active or idle) in both the main header and the sidebar, changing visual indicators accordingly.
3.  **Statistics Update (`updateStats`):** Populates various resource counters—including node count, current window usage against a limit, memory consumption, and total tokens processed—in the dedicated sidebar sections.
4.  **Configuration:** Allows external configuration of display limits (like maximum window size or memory capacity).

**Role in Project:** It acts as the primary feedback mechanism for system health and resource monitoring, ensuring that users are always aware of the application's operational status and current usage metrics.

### Functions & Methods

#### `init()` — function, line 7

* **Purpose:** Initializes global references (`dotEl` and `labelEl`) by retrieving specific status elements from the Document Object Model (DOM).
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function relies on two specific HTML elements existing in the DOM with IDs 'status-dot' and 'status-label'. If either element is missing, the corresponding variable will be assigned `null`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function init() {
    dotEl = document.getElementById('status-dot');
    labelEl = document.getElementById('status-label');
  }
```

</details>

#### `setStatus(state, label)` — function, line 12

* **Purpose:** Updates the visual status indicators—including a colored dot, main label, and side panel text—to reflect the current operational state.
* **Inputs:**
    * `state` (string): The current status of the component. If this value is `'active'`, the dot will be styled as active; otherwise, it defaults to an idle style.
    * `label` (string): The textual message that should be displayed for the current status.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function performs multiple side effects by manipulating the DOM elements `dotEl`, `labelEl`, and potentially the element with ID `side-status`. If `dotEl` is not defined, the function exits immediately without making any changes.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function setStatus(state, label) {
    if (!dotEl) return;
    dotEl.className = state === 'active' ? 'topbar__dot' : 'topbar__dot topbar__dot--idle';
    labelEl.textContent = label;
    // Also update sidebar
    var sideStatus = document.getElementById('side-status');
    if (sideStatus) sideStatus.textContent = label;
  }
```

</details>

#### `updateStats(nodes, windowCount, memoryMB, totalTokens)` — function, line 21

* **Purpose:** Updates various statistical counters displayed in the sidebar UI elements using provided metrics and component limits.
* **Inputs:**
    * `nodes` (string | number): The current count of nodes to display in the side panel.
    * `windowCount` (number): The current window usage count.
    * `memoryMB` (number): The current memory usage in megabytes.
    * `totalTokens` (number | null | undefined): The total number of tokens used, or null/undefined if none are available.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function directly manipulates the DOM by setting `textContent` on elements with specific IDs (`side-nodes`, `side-window`, etc.). It relies on the existence of global state properties (`Topbar._windowSize` and `Topbar._memoryLimitMB`) to calculate display limits. If any target element is not found in the DOM, the corresponding update will be skipped gracefully.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function updateStats(nodes, windowCount, memoryMB, totalTokens) {
    // Update sidebar stats
    var sideNodes = document.getElementById('side-nodes');
    var sideWindow = document.getElementById('side-window');
    var sideMemory = document.getElementById('side-memory');
    var sideTokens = document.getElementById('side-tokens');
    if (sideNodes) sideNodes.textContent = nodes;
    if (sideWindow) sideWindow.textContent = windowCount + '/' + (Topbar._windowSize || 20);
    if (sideMemory) sideMemory.textContent = memoryMB + ' / ' + (Topbar._memoryLimitMB || 2048) + ' MB';
    if (sideTokens) sideTokens.textContent = totalTokens ? totalTokens.toLocaleString() : '0';
  }
```

</details>

#### `setConfig(config)` — function, line 36

* **Purpose:** Sets the internal configuration parameters for the Topbar component using values provided in a configuration object.
* **Inputs:** config (Object) - An object containing optional settings, expected to include `windowSize` and `memoryLimitMB`.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** If `config.windowSize` is not provided or is falsy, the internal `_windowSize` will default to 20. If `config.memoryLimitMB` is not provided or is falsy, the internal `_memoryLimitMB` will default to 2048. This function mutates the static state of the `Topbar` object.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function setConfig(config) {
    Topbar._windowSize = config.windowSize || 20;
    Topbar._memoryLimitMB = config.memoryLimitMB || 2048;
  }
```

</details>

<a id="demouijsinternalmockresponsesjs"></a>
<details>
<summary><strong><code>demo/ui/js/internal/mock-responses.js</code></strong> — 43 lines, 1 functions</summary>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/mock-responses.js`

**43 lines** | 1 functions/classes

This module provides a mock implementation of an LLM (Large Language Model) interface, designed to simulate complex conversational and memory retrieval behavior when a live LLM server is unavailable.

**Purpose:** Its primary purpose is to enable testing and development continuity by providing predictable, canned responses that mimic the functionality of advanced context recall systems.

**What it Does:** The module exposes a `getResponse` function that accepts a user query. It operates in two ways:
1.  **Keyword Matching (Specific):** If the query contains specific keywords (e.g., 'pineapple', 'dog', 'bm25', 'tokyo'), it returns detailed, hardcoded responses simulating successful retrieval of facts or memories related to that topic.
2.  **Fallback Mechanism (General):** If no specific keywords are matched, it cycles through a predefined list of generic "canned" messages, simulating general context searching and processing steps.

**Role in the Project:** It acts as a crucial mock dependency, allowing developers to test the application's logic that relies on LLM output—such as memory graph interaction or retrieval pipeline handling—without needing access to external AI services.

### Functions & Methods

#### `getResponse(query)` — function, line 16

* **Purpose:** Returns a canned or retrieved memory response based on keyword matching within the provided query string.
* **Inputs:**
    * `query` (string): The search query used to determine which predefined memory snippet should be returned.
* **Outputs:**
    * string: A hardcoded text response representing a recalled piece of information.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function performs case-insensitive keyword matching on the input query.
    * It has significant side effects, as it increments an external counter variable (`idx`) if none of the predefined keywords are matched.
    * If no specific keywords are found (pineapple, dog, bm25, tokyo, music, pc), it falls back to retrieving a response from an external array named `responses` using the modulo operator on the global index counter `idx`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function getResponse(query) {
    var q = query.toLowerCase();
    if (q.indexOf('pineapple') !== -1 || q.indexOf('code word') !== -1 || q.indexOf('secret') !== -1) {
      return 'The secret code word is pineapple \u2014 you set it up back in turn 4 during the BM25 Python session. Retrieved from node 4 via BM25 recall.';
    }
    if (q.indexOf('dog') !== -1 || q.indexOf('corgi') !== -1 || q.indexOf('biscuit') !== -1 || q.indexOf('mochi') !== -1) {
      return "From memory chain: your friend has a corgi named Biscuit who herds cats. You're getting your own corgi named Mochi. Vet is Dr. Nakamura, Hillside Animal Clinic, 4th street.";
    }
    if (q.indexOf('bm25') !== -1 || q.indexOf('python') !== -1 || q.indexOf('k1') !== -1) {
      return 'Retrieved from chain: Python 3.11.4, project at C:\\Users\\dev\\projects\\memory-engine. BM25 settings for conversational text: k1=1.2, b=0.4.';
    }
    if (q.indexOf('tokyo') !== -1 || q.indexOf('japan') !== -1 || q.indexOf('cherry') !== -1 || q.indexOf('flight') !== -1) {
      return 'From recalled nodes: Tokyo trip March 28 - April 8, ANA flight NH109. Airalo eSIM, Suica card, budget ~\u00A510,000-15,000/day. Vegetarian: try shojin ryori.';
    }
    if (q.indexOf('music') !== -1 || q.indexOf('beat') !== -1 || q.indexOf('fl studio') !== -1) {
      return "From memory: FL Studio on Windows, lo-fi at 80 BPM, trap at 140 BPM. Splice username: beatmaker_mochi. Focusrite Scarlett 2i2 interface.";
    }
    if (q.indexOf('pc') !== -1 || q.indexOf('cpu') !== -1 || q.indexOf('build') !== -1 || q.indexOf('ryzen') !== -1) {
      return 'From chain: Ryzen 7 7800X3D, MSI MAG B650 TOMAHAWK, 32GB DDR5-6000, RTX 4060 Ti, Fractal Meshify 2, Corsair RM750x. Microcenter Tustin.';
    }
    var resp = responses[idx % responses.length];
    idx++;
    return resp;
  }

## `demo/ui/js/internal/sovereign-loop.js`

**550 lines** | 9 functions/classes

The `SovereignLoop` module implements a sophisticated context management strategy designed to facilitate iterative, self-critiquing reasoning loops within an AI conversation.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to manage the state and flow of complex interactions where the AI must critique its own output (self-correction) and refine its answers over multiple passes without allowing the conversational history to grow unboundedly or become structurally inconsistent.

**What It Does:**
1.  **Context Management:** It maintains a "lean" context by rebuilding the message history for each iteration using only the base prompt, the accumulated summary of previous critiques, the last response, and the current critique. This prevents memory bloat while preserving critical information.
2.  **Role Handling:** It includes utility functions to fix role alternation issues in raw messages, ensuring strict adherence to user/assistant roles for API compatibility.
3.  **Structured Critique Parsing:** The `streamEphemeral` function handles the core self-critique mechanism. It prompts the AI (the "Ephemeral Mind") to output a highly structured JSON payload containing detailed analysis (`thought_block`), identified successes (`resolved_nodes`), and specific flaws or missing information (`unresolved_tension`).
4.  **Streaming Interactions:** It provides dedicated asynchronous functions (`streamSovereign` and `streamEphemeral`) to stream both the main AI response and the internal critique, providing real-time user feedback while managing the underlying message state.

**Role in the Project:**
This module acts as the central orchestrator for advanced conversational reasoning. By enforcing a structured self-critique layer (the "Sovereign Loop"), it elevates the conversation from simple Q&A to a rigorous process of analysis, flaw detection, and iterative refinement, significantly improving the quality and depth of the AI's final output.

### Functions & Methods

#### `fixRoles(msgs)` — function, line 18

* **Purpose:** Processes an array of message objects, merging consecutive messages that share the same role and adjusting system roles for display consistency.
* **Inputs:**
    * `msgs` (Array<{role: string, content: string}>): An array of raw message objects containing a `role` and `content`.
* **Outputs:** Array<{role: string, content: string}>: A new array representing the cleaned list of messages, where consecutive messages with matching roles have been merged into single entries.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * System roles (`'system'`) are automatically converted to user roles (`'user'`) if they appear after the first message in the sequence.
    * Consecutive messages sharing the same role (after potential system role conversion) are merged by appending their content, separated by two newline characters (`\n\n`), into the content of the preceding entry.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function fixRoles(msgs) {
    var fixed = [];
    msgs.forEach(function (m, i) {
      var role = m.role;
      if (role === 'system' && i > 0) role = 'user';
      var last = fixed[fixed.length - 1];
      if (last && last.role === role) {
        last.content += '\n\n' + m.content;
      } else {
        fixed.push({ role: role, content: m.content });
      }
    });
    return fixed;
  }
```

</details>

#### `parseEphemeralPayload(rawOutput)` — function, line 36

* **Purpose:** Parses a raw string output, attempting to extract and standardize structured data from an embedded JSON payload, even if the input is wrapped in markdown fences or contains parsing errors.
* **Inputs:**
    * `rawOutput` (string): The raw text content that may contain the desired JSON object.
* **Outputs:**
    * object: A standardized structure containing key components of a processing cycle, including thought blocks, resolved nodes, and unresolved tension.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If the input is null, undefined, or empty/whitespace, it returns a default fallback object immediately.
    * It automatically cleans the input by stripping common markdown code fences (e.g., ````json`, ````).
    * The function uses depth counting to accurately locate the boundaries of the primary JSON object within the cleaned string.
    * If standard `JSON.parse()` fails, it falls back to using regular expressions to attempt extraction of critical fields like `thought_block` and `unresolved_tension`.
    * All returned properties are guaranteed to have default values (e.g., empty arrays for `resolved_nodes`, boolean `false` for `requires_further_recursion`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function parseEphemeralPayload(rawOutput) {
    var fallback = {
      thought_block: rawOutput || '',
      resolved_nodes: [],
      unresolved_tension: '',
      requires_further_recursion: false
    };

    if (!rawOutput || rawOutput.trim().length === 0) return fallback;

    var cleaned = rawOutput
      .replace(/^```json\s*/i, '')
      .replace(/^```\s*/i, '')
      .replace(/\s*```$/i, '')
      .trim();

    var openIdx = cleaned.indexOf('{');
    if (openIdx === -1) return fallback;

    var depth = 0;
    var closeIdx = -1;
    for (var i = openIdx; i < cleaned.length; i++) {
      if (cleaned[i] === '{') depth++;
      else if (cleaned[i] === '}') { depth--; if (depth === 0) { closeIdx = i + 1; break; } }
    }
    if (closeIdx <= openIdx) return fallback;

    var jsonStr = cleaned.slice(openIdx, closeIdx);

    try {
      var parsed = JSON.parse(jsonStr);
      return {
        thought_block: parsed.thought_block || parsed.analysis || parsed.thought || rawOutput,
        resolved_nodes: Array.isArray(parsed.resolved_nodes) ? parsed.resolved_nodes : [],
        unresolved_tension: parsed.unresolved_tension || parsed.tension || '',
        requires_further_recursion: Boolean(parsed.requires_further_recursion)
      };
    } catch (e) {
      var thoughtMatch = jsonStr.match(/"thought_block"\s*:\s*"([\s\S]*?)(?:"|$)/);
      var tensionMatch = jsonStr.match(/"unresolved_tension"\s*:\s*"([\s\S]*?)(?:"|$)/);
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `validateTension(tension, originalQuery)` — function, line 93

* **Purpose:** Checks if a given tension string contains at least one word of four or more characters that is also present in the original query string.
* **Inputs:**
    * `tension` (string): The text to validate for overlapping words. Must be at least 15 characters long to proceed with validation.
    * `originalQuery` (string): The source text used to check for word overlap.
* **Outputs:** boolean: Returns `true` if an overlap of at least one common word (4+ characters) is found, otherwise returns `false`.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Validation is case-insensitive.
    * Only words containing 4 or more characters are considered for overlap.
    * If `tension` is null, undefined, or shorter than 15 characters, the function immediately returns `false`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function validateTension(tension, originalQuery) {
    if (!tension || tension.length < 15) return false;
    var queryWords = {};
    originalQuery.toLowerCase().split(/\W+/).forEach(function (w) {
      if (w.length >= 4) queryWords[w] = true;
    });
    var tensionWords = tension.toLowerCase().split(/\W+/).filter(function (w) { return w.length >= 4; });
    var overlap = tensionWords.filter(function (w) { return queryWords[w]; });
    return overlap.length >= 1;
  }
```

</details>

#### `streamSovereign(messages, memory, config, opts)` — function, line 107

* **Purpose:** Streams a conversational response using provided messages and memory, updating both the UI (DOM) and internal application state during the process.
* **Inputs:**
    * `messages` (Array<string>): The list of messages to be processed by the model.
    * `memory` (Object): An object containing the transport layer (`transport`) used for streaming and memory management.
    * `config` (Object): Configuration settings, including a boolean flag `thinking` that controls UI behavior.
    * `opts` (Object): Options object containing mutable state (`streamState`), which is updated throughout the function execution.
* **Outputs:** An object `{ text: string, domElement: HTMLElement }` containing the final streamed text and the associated DOM element for display.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * **Side Effects:** The function modifies the global `opts.streamState` by setting `lastStreamMsg`, `lastPartialResponse`, `lastMessages`, and `currentAbort`. It also directly manipulates the DOM (e.g., updating elements with IDs like `'side-sent'` and manipulating the structure of `streamMsg`).
    * **Streaming Logic:** The core logic uses `memory.transport.stream` which processes tokens via callbacks (`onThink`, `onToken`, `onDone`), cleaning out specific channel tags (`<\|channel>`) and thinking blocks (`<think>`).
    * **State Cleanup:** It resets `opts.streamState.currentAbort` to `null` upon completion.
    * **UI Handling:** If `config.thinking` is false, it explicitly disables the "thinking" UI element for visual feedback.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async function streamSovereign(messages, memory, config, opts) {
    var streamMsg = Chat.createStreamingMessage(memory.chain.length + 1);
    opts.streamState.lastStreamMsg = streamMsg;
    opts.streamState.lastPartialResponse = '';

    if (!config.thinking && streamMsg && streamMsg.thinkWrapper) {
      streamMsg.thinkWrapper.querySelector('div').textContent = 'thinking disabled';
      streamMsg.thinkWrapper.style.opacity = '0.35';
      streamMsg.thinkWrapper.open = false;
    }

    opts.streamState.lastMessages = messages;
    opts.streamState.currentAbort = new AbortController();

    var streamResult = await memory.transport.stream(messages, {
      onThink: function (token) { if (streamMsg) streamMsg.appendThink(token); },
      onToken: function (token) {
        var clean = token.replace(/<\|channel>\w+\s*<channel\|>/g, '');
        if (clean) {
          opts.streamState.lastPartialResponse += clean;
          if (streamMsg) streamMsg.appendContent(clean);
        }
      },
      onDone: function (full, think) {
        var cleaned = full
          .replace(/<\|channel>\w+\s*<channel\|>/g, '')
          .replace(/<think>[\s\S]*?<\/think>\s*/g, '')
          .trim();
        if (streamMsg) streamMsg.finalize(cleaned);
      }
    }, opts.streamState.currentAbort.signal);

    opts.streamState.currentAbort = null;

    if (streamResult.usage) {
      var sideSentEl = document.getElementById('side-sent');
      if (sideSentEl) sideSentEl.textContent = streamResult.usage.promptTokens.toLocaleString();
    }

    return { text: streamResult.text, domElement: streamMsg };
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `streamEphemeral(ephPrompt, memory, config)` — function, line 152

* **Purpose:** Initiates an asynchronous stream to generate an internal, critical review (Ephemeral Mind) of a prompt using a specialized AI model endpoint.
* **Inputs:**
    * `ephPrompt`: String - The user-provided prompt that needs to be critiqued or reviewed by the Ephemeral Mind.
    * `memory`: Object - An object containing a `transport` property used for streaming communication with the backend service.
    * `config`: Object - Configuration object used to manage internal state, specifically tracking whether the system was previously in "thinking" mode.
* **Outputs:** Promise<void> - Resolves when the critique stream is complete and the UI updates are finished.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * **State Mutation:** It temporarily sets `config.thinking` to `false` at the start and restores its original value (`wasThinking`) before exiting, ensuring state integrity.
    * **DOM Manipulation (Side Effect):** It adds a visual "Ephemeral Mind critiquing..." indicator element to the `#chat-stream` container when the function starts, and removes it shortly after starting the stream process.
    * **Streaming:** The core logic streams content into `ephStreamMsg`, appending cleaned tokens (`onToken`) and finally setting the full, cleaned text in `ephFullText` upon completion (`onDone`).
    * **Cleanup:** It includes a small delay (100ms) after adding the indicator before proceeding with the stream.

  async function streamEphemeral(ephPrompt, memory, config) {
    var wasThinking = config.thinking;
    config.thinking = false;

    var ephMessages = [
      { role: 'system', content: 'You are the Ephemeral Mind \u2014 the internal critique layer. Your job is NOT to be agreeable. Look for edge cases, missing logic, hidden contradictions, and structural flaws in the answer you receive.\n\nYou MUST output ONLY a JSON payload with this exact structure:\n{"thought_block": "your detailed critique and analysis...", "resolved_nodes": ["what was done well or correctly"], "unresolved_tension": "what is flawed, missing, or contradictory", "requires_further_recursion": true/false}\n\nIf the answer is genuinely complete and correct, set unresolved_tension to "" and requires_further_recursion to false.\nDo NOT fake tensions. Do NOT be lazy. Be specific.' },
      { role: 'user', content: ephPrompt }
    ];

    var indicator = document.createElement('div');
    indicator.className = 'ephemeral-indicator';
    indicator.innerHTML = '<span class="ephemeral-indicator__pulse"></span> <span class="ephemeral-indicator__text">Ephemeral Mind critiquing\u2026</span>';
    document.getElementById('chat-stream').appendChild(indicator);
    document.getElementById('chat-stream').scrollTop = document.getElementById('chat-stream').scrollHeight;

    await new Promise(function (r) { setTimeout(r, 100); });
    if (indicator.parentElement) indicator.remove();

    var ephStreamMsg = Chat.createEphemeralStreamingMessage();
    var ephFullText = '';

    await memory.transport.stream(ephMessages, {
      onThink: function () {},
      onToken: function (token) {
        var clean = token.replace(/<\|channel>\w+\s*<channel\|>/g, '');
        if (clean) {
          ephFullText += clean;
          if (ephStreamMsg) ephStreamMsg.appendContent(clean);
        }
      },
      onDone: function (full) {
        var cleaned = full
          .replace(/<\|channel>\w+\s*<channel\|>/g, '')
          .replace(/<think>[\s\S]*?<\/think>\s*/g, '')
          .trim();
        ephFullText = cleaned;
      }
    });

    config.thinking = wasThinking;

#### `buildRefinementContext(baseMessages, userMsg, resolvedSummary, lastResponse, tension)` — function, line 205

* **Purpose:** Constructs a comprehensive message history array designed to prompt an LLM to refine a previous answer based on specific critique feedback and the original user query.
* **Inputs:**
    * `baseMessages`: Array of message objects (e.g., system/user/assistant roles) representing the initial conversation context.
    * `userMsg`: String containing the original question or prompt that needs refinement.
    * `resolvedSummary`: Optional string summarizing what has been resolved in previous steps, added as a system message.
    * `lastResponse`: String containing the last response given by the "Sovereign" (the model's previous output).
    * `tension`: String detailing the specific flaw or critique found in the previous answer that needs addressing.
* **Outputs:** Array of message objects, formatted for an LLM API call, representing the complete context for refinement.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function ensures the original conversation history is preserved, adds a system-level summary of resolved information, and constructs a final user prompt that explicitly reminds the model of the original question and instructs it to correct the flaw identified by `tension`. It relies on an external helper function, `fixRoles`, to standardize the roles within the message array.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function buildRefinementContext(baseMessages, userMsg, resolvedSummary, lastResponse, tension) {
    // Start with the original base (system prompt + window + recall + user query)
    var msgs = baseMessages.slice();

    // Add context about what's been resolved so far (capped)
    if (resolvedSummary) {
      msgs.push({
        role: 'system',
        content: '[Resolved so far]: ' + resolvedSummary
      });
    }

    // Add the Sovereign's last response
    msgs.push({ role: 'assistant', content: lastResponse });

    // Add the critique feedback with the original query reminder
    msgs.push({
      role: 'user',
      content: '[ORIGINAL QUESTION]: ' + userMsg + '\n\n[Your internal critique found a flaw in your answer]\nFlaw: "' + tension + '"\n\nRefine your answer to address this issue while staying focused on the original question above. Write your complete, updated response.'
    });

    return fixRoles(msgs);
  }
```

</details>

It relies on an external `fixRoles` function to ensure the roles in the resulting message array are correctly formatted for the target LLM API.

<details>

  function buildSynthesisContext(baseMessages, userMsg, resolvedSummary, lastResponse) {
    var msgs = baseMessages.slice();

    msgs.push({ role: 'assistant', content: lastResponse });
    msgs.push({
      role: 'user',
      content: '[ORIGINAL QUESTION]: ' + userMsg + '\n\n[Self-critique complete \u2014 all tensions resolved]\nResolved insights: ' + (resolvedSummary || 'N/A') + '\n\nNow write your FINAL answer to the original question above. This should be a clean, unified response that synthesizes everything you worked out. Address the user directly \u2014 do not reference the critique process. Just deliver the best possible answer.'
    });

    return fixRoles(msgs);
  }
```

#### `run(opts)` — function, line 247

* **Purpose:** Executes a multi-pass, iterative "Sovereign Loop" process to generate a comprehensive and refined response based on initial context, user messages, and internal critique cycles.
* **Inputs:**
    * `opts` (object): Configuration options for the loop.
        * `opts.messages` (Array<string>): The base conversation history used as initial context.
        * `opts.memory` (object): The persistent memory object containing the chat chain, indexing structures (BM25), and a queue for mid-loop notes (`pocket`).
        * `opts.config` (object): Configuration settings governing the loop's behavior or model parameters.
        * `opts.userMsg` (string): The specific user message that initiates or guides the current response generation.
* **Outputs:** None explicitly returned, but it manages side effects by updating the provided `memory` object and rendering output via external components (`Chat`, `Topbar`).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * **Side Effects/State Mutation:** This function heavily mutates the passed `opts.memory` object by appending content to `memory.chain` and adding nodes to `memory.bm25`. It also interacts with global UI elements (e.g., setting status via `Topbar`, rendering chat messages via `Chat`).
    * **Abortion:** The loop can be aborted at any time if the user presses the 'Escape' key, which sets an internal flag (`aborted`) and breaks the processing cycle.
    * **Iteration Limit:** The process is strictly limited by `MAX_LOOPS` (100) or when explicitly aborted.
    * **Mid-Loop Injection:** It checks for and processes pending notes from a "pocket queue" (`memory.pocket`), allowing external input to influence the critique loop mid-process.

<a id="demouijsinternaltoolstoolchainjs"></a>
<details>
<summary><strong><code>demo/ui/js/internal/tools/toolchain.js</code></strong> — 158 lines, 5 functions</summary>

## `demo/ui/js/internal/tools/toolchain.js`

**158 lines** | 5 functions/classes

The `ToolChain` module is a sophisticated workflow engine designed to manage complex, multi-step interactions with external tools.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to provide deterministic execution for common user requests (e.g., "explain this code," "find where X is located"). It acts as an intermediary layer that translates natural language intent into structured, sequential tool calls.

**What it Does:**
1. **Recipe Matching:** It maintains a set of predefined "recipes" (workflows), each associated with specific trigger keywords. When a user message arrives, the module checks if the message matches any recipe.
2. **Sequential Execution:** If a match is found, it executes the steps defined in that recipe sequentially. Each step performs a specific action (e.g., `grep` for searching code, `read` for file content, or `shell` for running commands).
3. **Context Passing:** Crucially, the output of one tool step automatically becomes the input context for the next step, allowing complex operations like "find X using grep, then read the resulting file."
4. **Output Assembly:** Finally, it aggregates all results from every executed step into a single, formatted context block that is passed to the Language Model (LLM).

**Role in the Project:**
The `ToolChain` module structures and automates complex reasoning paths. By handling the entire execution flow—from intent recognition to multi-step data gathering—it ensures that the LLM receives a complete, assembled, and highly relevant context block, rather than just raw user input or isolated tool outputs. This makes the system's behavior predictable and robust for technical tasks involving codebases and file systems.

### Functions & Methods

#### `match(msg)` — function, line 42

* **Purpose:** Checks an input message against a predefined list of recipe triggers and returns the first matching recipe object found.
* **Inputs:**
    * `msg`: The string message to be checked for trigger phrases. (Type: String)
* **Outputs:**
    * Object or null: Returns `{ recipe: RecipeObject }` if a match is found, otherwise returns `null`.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The matching process is case-insensitive because the input message is converted to lowercase before comparison.
    * It uses a "first match wins" strategy; it stops and returns immediately upon finding the first trigger that matches any recipe.
    * This function relies on an external, scoped variable named `recipes`, which must be an array of objects containing a `triggers` array.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function match(msg) {
    var lower = msg.toLowerCase();
    for (var i = 0; i < recipes.length; i++) {
      var recipe = recipes[i];
      for (var j = 0; j < recipe.triggers.length; j++) {
        if (lower.includes(recipe.triggers[j])) {
          return { recipe: recipe };
        }
      }
    }
    return null;
  }
```

</details>

#### `execute(recipe, msg)` — function, line 61

* **Purpose:** Executes a predefined sequence of steps (a recipe) by calling various internal APIs based on the step's action and passing context from previous results.
* **Inputs:**
    * `recipe` (`object`): An object containing an array of steps (`steps`), where each step defines an action (e.g., 'grep', 'read') and parameters for execution.
    * `msg` (`string`): A fallback message or initial context used as input if a step cannot derive necessary parameters from previous results.
* **Outputs:** Array of objects, representing the successful results of each executed step, including the step index, action taken, and the resulting data.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function processes steps sequentially, using the result of the current step (`prevResult`) as context for building parameters in subsequent steps (via `step.buildParams(prevResult)`). If a step lacks necessary parameters or if the action is unrecognized, it logs a skip message and continues execution without modifying state. It relies on an external `apiCall` function to perform all actual I/O operations.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async function execute(recipe, msg) {
    console.log('[ToolChain] Executing recipe: ' + recipe.name);
    var results = [];
    var prevResult = null;

    for (var i = 0; i < recipe.steps.length; i++) {
      var step = recipe.steps[i];
      var result = null;

      if (step.action === 'grep') {
        var keyword = step.buildParams(prevResult || msg);
        if (!keyword) { console.log('[ToolChain] Step ' + i + ': no keyword, skipping'); continue; }
        console.log('[ToolChain] Step ' + i + ': grep "' + keyword + '"');
        result = await apiCall('/api/search?q=' + encodeURIComponent(keyword));
      } else if (step.action === 'read') {
        var filePath = step.buildParams(prevResult);
        if (!filePath) { console.log('[ToolChain] Step ' + i + ': no file path, skipping'); continue; }
        console.log('[ToolChain] Step ' + i + ': read "' + filePath + '"');
        result = await apiCall('/api/read?path=' + encodeURIComponent(filePath));
      } else if (step.action === 'shell') {
        console.log('[ToolChain] Step ' + i + ': shell "' + step.command + '"');
        result = await apiCall('/api/shell?cmd=' + encodeURIComponent(step.command));
      } else if (step.action === 'websearch') {
        var query = step.buildParams(prevResult || msg);
        if (!query) { console.log('[ToolChain] Step ' + i + ': no query, skipping'); continue; }
        console.log('[ToolChain] Step ' + i + ': websearch "' + query + '"');
        result = await apiCall('/api/websearch?q=' + encodeURIComponent(query));
      } else if (step.action === 'fetch') {
        var fetchUrl = step.buildParams(prevResult);
        if (!fetchUrl) { console.log('[ToolChain] Step ' + i + ': no URL, skipping'); continue; }
        console.log('[ToolChain] Step ' + i + ': fetch "' + fetchUrl + '"');
        result = await apiCall('/api/fetch?url=' + encodeURIComponent(fetchUrl));
      }

      if (result) {
        results.push({ step: i, action: step.action, data: result });
        prevResult = result;
      }
    }

  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `apiCall(endpoint)` — function, line 112

* **Purpose:** Asynchronously fetches data from a specified API endpoint relative to a predefined server URL, returning the parsed JSON body upon success or `null` upon failure.
* **Inputs:**
    * `endpoint`: `string` - The specific path or resource identifier for the API call (e.g., `/users/1`).
* **Outputs:** `Promise<object | null>` - A promise that resolves to a JavaScript object (parsed JSON) if the request is successful, or `null` if the request fails due to network issues or bad HTTP status codes.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function relies on an external variable, `serverUrl`, to construct the full API URL. It handles both non-successful HTTP responses (`res.ok` is false) and network/parsing errors by logging a warning to the console and returning `null`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async function apiCall(endpoint) {
    try {
      var res = await fetch(serverUrl + endpoint);
      if (!res.ok) return null;
      return await res.json();
    } catch (e) {
      console.warn('[ToolChain] API call failed:', endpoint, e.message);
      return null;
    }
  }
```

</details>

#### `cleanQuery(input)` — function, line 126

* **Purpose:** Cleans and sanitizes a raw user query string by removing conversational prefixes, punctuation, converting to lowercase, trimming whitespace, and limiting the length.
* **Inputs:**
    * `input` (string | any): The raw query string provided by the user that needs cleaning.
* **Outputs:** string | null: The cleaned, standardized version of the input query, or `null` if the input was an object.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If the input is detected as an object (and not null), the function immediately returns `null`.
    * It aggressively removes common conversational phrases at the start of the string (e.g., "can you", "please").
    * All punctuation marks (`?`, `!`, `.`, `,`) are removed globally.
    * The resulting string is truncated to a maximum length of 100 characters.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function cleanQuery(input) {
    if (typeof input === 'object') return null;
    return input.toLowerCase()
      .replace(/^(can you |please |hey |search for |search |look up |lookup |google |find online |find |look this up )/i, '')
      .replace(/[?!.,]/g, '')
      .trim()
      .slice(0, 100);
  }
```

</details>

#### `extractKeyword(input)` — function, line 139

* **Purpose:** Extracts a concise set of keywords from user input, prioritizing file paths if the input is an object result, or stripping filler words and taking the first few meaningful terms if the input is a string.
* **Inputs:**
    * `input` (any): The raw input data. It can be a string (e.g., a query) or an object (expected to contain previous step results).
* **Outputs:**
    * `string`: A cleaned, keyword-rich phrase (up to 3 words), or the fully stripped original string if no keywords are found. Returns `null` if the input is an object but lacks the expected file path structure.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If `input` is an object and contains a result array (`results[0].file`), it attempts to return that file path immediately, ignoring further string processing.
    * When processing strings, the function aggressively removes common conversational filler phrases (e.g., "how does," "can you," "please") and punctuation.
    * It filters out any resulting words shorter than three characters before selecting the final keywords.
    * If all meaningful words are filtered out, it returns the fully stripped string instead of an empty string.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function extractKeyword(input) {
    if (typeof input === 'object' && input !== null) {
      // Previous step result — extract from it if possible
      if (input.results && input.results[0] && input.results[0].file) return input.results[0].file;
      return null;
    }
    // String input — strip filler, get keywords
    var stripped = input.toLowerCase()
      .replace(/^(how does|explain|what does|show me how|walk me through|where is|find the|which file|locate|can you|please|the)\s+/g, '')
      .replace(/\s+(work|works|function|do|does|in this project|in the code|in the codebase)\s*/g, ' ')
      .replace(/[?!.,]/g, '')
      .trim();
    // Take first 2-3 meaningful words
    var words = stripped.split(/\s+/).filter(function (w) { return w.length > 2; });
    return words.slice(0, 3).join(' ') || stripped;
  }
```

</details>

</details>

## `ingest-docs.js`

**188 lines** | 2 functions/classes

This script is an **ingestion utility** designed to populate and persist the core knowledge base (Luminal Memory) for the project.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to take various source markdown documents—which contain reference material and architectural summaries—and transform them into structured, searchable data nodes within a specialized memory system (`LuminalMemory`). It also ingests conversational fixtures that demonstrate how the system should be used.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Reads and Processes Documents:** It iterates through defined source files (e.g., `luminal-memory-reference.md`).
2.  **Intelligent Chunking:** It uses a complex function (`chunkByHeadings`) to parse large markdown documents, splitting them first by major headings (`##`) and then further breaking down excessively long sections into smaller, manageable chunks (limited to 2000 characters) based on paragraph breaks.
3.  **Filtering:** It applies custom filters to certain documents (like `reasoning_loop_summary.md`), allowing the script to selectively include only relevant sections while skipping philosophical or non-actionable content.
4.  **Node Creation and Indexing:** For every valid chunk, it creates a structured "node" containing the heading and content, appends this node to the system's memory chain, and adds it to an internal vector index (BM25) for retrieval.
5.  **State Persistence:** Finally, it serializes the entire populated memory state (including all nodes and the conversation history) into a JSON file (`data/memory-state.json`), making the knowledge base persistent between runs.

**Role in the Project:**
This script is a critical **setup step**. It must be run once to build the foundational knowledge graph. After execution, the resulting `memory-state.json` file contains all the necessary context and memory nodes that subsequent components (like `serve.js`) will load to restore the system's operational state and allow the demo to function correctly.

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/index.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `chunkByHeadings(text)` — function, line 52

* **Purpose:** Splits a raw markdown text document into structured chunks by first identifying sections based on headings (`##` or `###`) and then further splitting overly large sections into smaller, paragraph-delimited chunks.
* **Inputs:** 
    * `text` (string): The full content of the document to be processed.
* **Outputs:** Array of objects/chunks: An array where each element represents a chunk containing structured data (heading and content), suitable for ingestion.
* **Behavioral Notes:** 
    * Sections are only created if the accumulated content within that section is longer than 50 characters.
    * The function processes headings starting with `##` or `###`.
    * Any resulting chunk exceeding 2000 characters will be automatically split by paragraph boundaries (double newlines, `\n\n+`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function chunkByHeadings(text) {
  const lines = text.split('\n');
  const sections = [];
  let currentHeading = '';
  let currentContent = [];

  for (const line of lines) {
    if (line.startsWith('## ') || line.startsWith('### ')) {
      if (currentContent.length > 0) {
        const content = currentContent.join('\n').trim();
        if (content.length > 50) {
          sections.push({ heading: currentHeading, content });
        }
      }
      currentHeading = line.replace(/^#+\s*/, '');
      currentContent = [];
    } else {
      currentContent.push(line);
    }
  }

  if (currentContent.length > 0) {
    const content = currentContent.join('\n').trim();
    if (content.length > 50) {
      sections.push({ heading: currentHeading, content });
    }
  }

  // Now split large sections into paragraph-level chunks
  const chunks = [];
  for (const section of sections) {
    if (section.content.length <= 2000) {
      // Small enough — keep as one chunk
      chunks.push(section);
    } else {
      // Split by double-newline (paragraph boundaries)
      const paragraphs = section.content.split(/\n\n+/);
      let currentChunk = '';
      let chunkIdx = 0;

* **Outputs:** None (The primary output is side effects, specifically the population and state change of the `LuminalMemory` object passed to it).
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function processes documents sequentially. For each document, it reads the full text, splits it into sections using `chunkByHeadings`, and then iterates over these chunks. Each chunk is optionally filtered using `doc.filter`. Successfully processed chunks are concatenated with a prefix and stored in both the memory's chain store (`memory.chain`) and its BM25 index (`memory.bm25`). The function also includes a separate, hardcoded step to ingest a knowledge base fixture conversation.

async function main() {
  console.log('=== Luminal Memory Document Ingestion ===\n');

  const memory = new LuminalMemory({});
  await memory.init();

  let totalNodes = 0;

  for (const doc of DOCS) {

    const text = await readFile(doc.path, 'utf8');
    const chunks = chunkByHeadings(text);

      if (doc.filter && !doc.filter(chunk.heading)) {
        continue;
      }

      // Store full content — no truncation
      const nodeContent = `${doc.prefix} ${chunk.heading}\n${chunk.content}`;

      const node = memory.chain.append('system', nodeContent);
      memory.bm25.add(node);
      ingested++;
    }

    console.log(`  Ingested ${ingested} sections as nodes`);
    totalNodes += ingested;
  }

## `package.json`

**52 lines** | 0 functions/classes

This package provides a specialized utility library designed to implement **infinite context memory** for Large Language Models (LLMs).

**Purpose:**
The primary goal of `@automacene/luminal-memory` is to solve the challenge of limited token windows in LLMs by providing a robust, scalable mechanism for storing and retrieving conversational history or external knowledge over extended periods.

**Functionality:**
It acts as a zero-dependency memory layer that can operate seamlessly across both browser and Node.js environments. Beyond simple text context management, its dependencies indicate advanced data ingestion capabilities, allowing it to process complex sources such as:
*   PDF documents (via parsing).
*   Images (via OCR/text extraction).
*   Web content.

**Role in the Project:**
This library serves as a foundational component for any application requiring persistent and scalable memory when interacting with AI models. It abstracts away the complexity of context management, allowing developers to build LLM-powered applications that maintain long-term conversational state or recall information from vast external datasets without hitting typical context limits.

<a id="readmemd"></a>
<details>
<summary><strong><code>README.md</code></strong> — 292 lines, 0 functions</summary>

## `README.md`

**292 lines** | 0 functions/classes

**Purpose:**
Liminal Memory is an advanced memory layer designed to solve the fundamental limitation of Large Language Models (LLMs): the finite context window. Its primary purpose is to give AI applications the ability to maintain persistent, unlimited conversation history and recall information from long-past interactions without requiring additional models, vector databases, or significant computational resources.

**Functionality:**
The system operates by intercepting communication between an application and any LLM via its HTTP API. It manages memory through several sophisticated mechanisms:

1.  **Context Management (Sliding Window):** Instead of passing the entire conversation history, Liminal Memory intelligently reconstructs the prompt for each turn, sending only the most recent and relevant messages to the model's "working memory."
2.  **Retrieval:** It uses pure algorithmic search techniques (like BM25) to find specific pieces of information referenced outside the current working window, injecting them back into the prompt instantly.
3.  **Tool Integration:** It automatically discovers and manages external capabilities (e.g., web search, date/time). When a user query matches a tool's description, the system activates that tool for the turn, allowing the LLM to use real-world data or functions.
4.  **Archiving and Compaction:** For truly long conversations, it implements "Trim & Branch" memory management. Old sections are compressed, summarized, and indexed into cold storage, ensuring they remain searchable without slowing down the active conversation flow.

**Role in the Project:**
Liminal Memory acts as a universal, zero-dependency middleware that sits between the application logic and the LLM endpoint. Its role is to abstract away complex memory management, allowing developers to build stateful AI assistants capable of remembering everything across sessions. By relying solely on algorithmic search rather than resource-intensive embedding models or vector databases, it ensures compatibility with any existing LLM (OpenAI, Ollama, etc.) and runs efficiently in both browser and Node.js environments.

</details>

## `serve.js`

**429 lines** | 2 functions/classes

This file implements a development web server designed to serve the project files and provide various API endpoints for an "explorer tool." Its primary purpose is to allow users (or other parts of the application) to interact with, read, search, and process content from the local codebase programmatically via HTTP requests.

**What it Does:**
The server handles several core functionalities through specific API routes:

1.  **File System Interaction:** It provides endpoints (`/api/read` and `/api/list`) to securely read the contents of specified files or list the directories within the project structure.
2.  **Code Search:** The `/api/search` endpoint recursively traverses the entire codebase (limited to specific file types like JS, MD, HTML, etc.) and searches for a given query string, returning matches with file paths and line numbers.
3.  **Documentation Generation:** Using a POST request (`/api/docgen`), it generates comprehensive documentation for a specified folder. This process streams progress updates using Server-Sent Events (SSE) while interacting with an external Large Language Model (LLM).
4.  **Web Content Fetching:** The `/api/fetch` endpoint fetches content from an arbitrary URL and converts the resulting HTML into Markdown format.

**Role in the Project:**
This module acts as the central backend gateway for local development and exploration. By exposing file system operations, search capabilities, and complex data processing (like documentation generation and web scraping) through standardized API calls, it allows the front-end or client components of the explorer tool to interact with the project's content without needing direct access to the underlying file system or external services.

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/extensions/ingest-file.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `walk(dir)` — function, line 108

* **Purpose:** Recursively traverses a directory structure, searching through specified file types (js, md, html, json, css) for occurrences of a given query string.
* **Inputs:**
    * `dir` (string): The starting directory path to begin the traversal.
* **Outputs:** None (It modifies the external array `matches`).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This function relies on several external variables/functions: `readdir`, `join`, `readFile`, `relative`, `SKIP` (a Set of strings), `query` (the search term), and `matches` (an array to store results).
    * Traversal stops immediately if 20 matches are found (`matches.length >= 20`).
    * It skips files/directories whose names start with a dot (`.`) or are listed in the global `SKIP` set.
    * File reading errors are silently ignored (using `try...catch { /* skip */ }`).
    * Matches are stored as objects containing the relative file path, 1-based line number, and a trimmed snippet of the content (up to 120 characters).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
    async function walk(dir) {
      let entries;
      try { entries = await readdir(dir, { withFileTypes: true }); }
      catch { return; }

      for (const entry of entries) {
        if (entry.name.startsWith(".") || SKIP.has(entry.name)) continue;
        const full = join(dir, entry.name);

        if (entry.isDirectory()) {
          await walk(full);
        } else if (/\.(js|md|html|json|css)$/.test(entry.name)) {
          try {
            const content = await readFile(full, "utf8");
            const lines = content.split("\n");
            for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
              if (lines[i].toLowerCase().includes(query)) {
                matches.push({
                  file: relative(__dirname, full).replace(/\\/g, "/"),
                  line: i + 1,
                  content: lines[i].trim().slice(0, 120)
                });
                if (matches.length >= 20) return;
              }
            }
          } catch { /* skip */ }
        }
        if (matches.length >= 20) return;
      }
    }
```

</details>

        };

## `src/config.js`

**115 lines** | 1 functions/classes

This file establishes the comprehensive default configuration for "Luminal Memory," an advanced conversational AI system designed to manage context, long-term memory, and tool usage for a Large Language Model (LLM).

**Purpose and Role:**
The primary role of this module is to define all operational parameters, ensuring that the AI assistant maintains coherence across extended conversations while efficiently managing computational resources. It provides a structured blueprint that can be overridden by user-specific settings via the accompanying `createConfig` function.

**What it Does (Key Functionality):**
1.  **LLM Connection:** Configures connection details (endpoint, API format) and sets a detailed system prompt defining the AI's persona and conversational rules.
2.  **Context Management:** Defines limits for conversation history (`windowSize`) and total token usage (`maxTokenBudget`), ensuring prompts remain within acceptable size constraints.
3.  **Memory Retrieval (Recall):** Manages long-term memory injection by setting parameters for retrieving historical context from an archive, including allocating a specific portion of the prompt budget to recalled nodes.
4.  **Resource and Archiving:** Sets limits on in-memory storage (`memoryLimitMB`) and defines strategies for data compaction and archiving (e.g., `archiveBlockSize`, summary format).
5.  **Tooling and Search:** Controls external capabilities, such as enabling/disabling web search and setting thresholds for determining when a tool or retrieved piece of information is relevant to the current query.

### Exports

- `createConfig` (function)
- `defaultConfig` (const)

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `createConfig(userConfig = {})` — function, line 107

* **Purpose:** Creates and returns a complete configuration object by deeply merging user-provided settings (`userConfig`) over predefined default values (`defaultConfig`).
* **Inputs:**
    * `userConfig`: Object - Optional configuration overrides provided by the user. Defaults to an empty object `{}`.
* **Outputs:** Object - The final, merged configuration object containing all active settings.
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function performs a deep merge for specific nested sections (`bm25` and `bloom`), ensuring that default values are retained unless explicitly overridden by the user. It relies on an external variable named `defaultConfig`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export function createConfig(userConfig = {}) {
  return {
    ...defaultConfig,
    ...userConfig,
    bm25: { ...defaultConfig.bm25, ...(userConfig.bm25 || {}) },
    bloom: { ...defaultConfig.bloom, ...(userConfig.bloom || {}) }
  };
}
```

## `src/core/buffer.js`

**200 lines** | 16 functions/classes

This file provides a system for managing and selecting context nodes—representing information chunks—for use in large language model prompts, ensuring that the total token count remains within a specified budget.

**Purpose:**
The primary purpose of this module is to act as an intelligent "memory buffer" that selects a subset of available knowledge (nodes) based on defined constraints (token limits and selection strategy). This prevents context overflow while maximizing the inclusion of relevant information.

**What it Does:**
1.  **`Buffer` (Base Class):** Establishes core functionality, including tracking maximum token capacity (`maxTokens`), current usage, and providing methods to add (`push`), remove (`pop`/`shift`), and clear nodes. It manages the state of the buffer and converts its contents into a format suitable for LLM message construction.
2.  **`SlidingBuffer`:** Implements "working memory." When filled, it prioritizes **recency**, selecting the most recent nodes from an ordered sequence until the token budget or node count limit is reached.
3.  **`RecallBuffer`:** Implements "long-term recall." When filled, it prioritizes **relevance (score)**, taking the highest-scoring nodes first and pruning lower-scoring nodes if a new token budget requires reduction.

**Role in the Project:**
These classes serve as critical context managers within an application pipeline. They abstract away the complexity of managing LLM input constraints, allowing developers to easily define whether the model's prompt should be based on recent conversation history (SlidingBuffer) or highly relevant historical facts retrieved from a knowledge base (RecallBuffer).

### Exports

- `Buffer` (class)
- `SlidingBuffer` (class)
- `RecallBuffer` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/core/conversation-manager.js`, `src/index.js`, `tests/conversation.test.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `for()` — class, line 2

* **Purpose:** Manages an ordered set of nodes and enforces a token budget to select a subset of nodes for prompt building.
* **Inputs:**
    * `options`: Object containing configuration parameters.
        * `maxTokens` (number): The maximum total token budget allowed for this buffer.
        * `name` (string, optional): An identifier used for logging purposes.
* **Outputs:**
    * `tokenCount` (number): The current accumulated token usage of the nodes in the buffer.
    * `remaining` (number): The remaining token capacity available within the maximum budget.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Buffers are designed to be ephemeral and do not own the nodes they select; they only reference them from a source chain.
    * Token usage is tracked internally (`_tokenTotal`) against a fixed `maxTokens` limit.
    * Subclasses (like `SlidingBuffer` or `RecallBuffer`) must implement specific selection strategies to adhere to the token budget.

 * A buffer holds an ordered set of nodes and manages a token budget.

 * Buffers are ephemeral per-turn. They don't own the nodes — they just

#### `Buffer()` — class, line 12

* **Purpose:** Manages a fixed-size collection of data nodes while tracking total token usage against a defined maximum budget.
* **Inputs:**
    * `options`: Object containing configuration parameters.
        * `maxTokens` (number): The absolute maximum number of tokens the buffer can hold.
        * `name` (string, optional): An identifier used for logging purposes.
* **Outputs:**
    * `tokenCount` (getter, number): The current total token usage within the buffer.
    * `remaining` (getter, number): The remaining token capacity available before hitting the limit.
    * `length` (getter, number): The count of nodes currently stored in the buffer.
    * `isEmpty` (getter, boolean): Indicates whether the buffer contains any nodes.
* **Behavioral Notes:** Token usage is tracked internally and determines the effective capacity; methods that add data must respect the `maxTokens` limit.

<details>

```javascript
export class Buffer {
  /**
   * @param {object} options
   * @param {number} options.maxTokens - maximum token budget for this buffer
   * @param {string} [options.name] - identifier for logging
   */
  constructor({ maxTokens, name = "buffer" }) {
    this.maxTokens = maxTokens;
    this.name = name;
    this.nodes = [];
    this._tokenTotal = 0;
  }

  /**
   * Current token usage.
   * @returns {number}
   */
  get tokenCount() {
    return this._tokenTotal;
  }

  /**
   * Remaining token capacity.
   * @returns {number}
   */
  get remaining() {
    return Math.max(0, this.maxTokens - this._tokenTotal);
  }

  /**
   * Number of nodes currently in the buffer.
   * @returns {number}
   */
  get length() {
    return this.nodes.length;
  }

  /**
   * Whether the buffer is empty.

#### `push(node)` — method, line 62

* **Purpose:** Adds a node to the buffer if the total resulting token count does not exceed the predefined maximum limit.
* **Inputs:**

* **Outputs:** Boolean indicating success (`true`) or failure due to capacity constraints (`false`).
* **Behavioral Notes:** Mutates the internal state by pushing the node to `this.nodes` and incrementing `this._tokenTotal` only if the token limit check passes. If the addition exceeds `this.maxTokens`, no changes are made, and `false` is returned.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

  push(node) {
    if (this._tokenTotal + node.tokenCount > this.maxTokens) {
      return false;
    }
    this.nodes.push(node);
    this._tokenTotal += node.tokenCount;
    return true;
  }
```

#### `pop()` — method, line 75

* **Purpose:** Removes and returns the last node from the internal list of nodes (`this.nodes`), simultaneously decrementing the total token count by that node's token amount.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** The popped node object, or `undefined` if the buffer was empty.
* **Behavioral Notes:** Mutates the internal state by removing an element from `this.nodes` and modifying `this._tokenTotal`. If called on an empty buffer, it returns `undefined` without error.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  pop() {
    const node = this.nodes.pop();
    if (node) {
      this._tokenTotal -= node.tokenCount;
    }
    return node;
  }
```

* **Purpose:** Converts an internal array of nodes into a standardized list of message objects suitable for external consumption.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  toMessages() {
    const messages = [];
    for (const node of this.nodes) {
      const msg = node.toMessage ? node.toMessage() : { role: node.role, content: node.content };
      if (msg) messages.push(msg);
    }
    return messages;
  }
```

#### `SlidingBuffer(extends Buffer)` — class, line 135

* **Purpose:** Manages a fixed-size buffer of nodes by prioritizing the inclusion of the most recent content until either a token budget or node count limit is reached.
* **Inputs:**
    * `options`: Object containing configuration parameters.
        * `maxTokens` (number): The maximum total number of tokens allowed in the buffer.
        * `maxNodes` (number): The absolute maximum number of nodes the buffer can hold (defaults to Infinity).
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The constructor initializes the base `Buffer` class and sets the specific node limit (`this.maxNodes`).
    * The `fill(allNodes)` method clears any existing content before populating the buffer.
    * It processes the input nodes backwards (from newest to oldest) and adds them to the front of the internal list, ensuring that the most recent data is retained first.
    * Node token count is calculated either from `node.tokenCount` or estimated as `ceil(content length / 4)` if not provided.
    * Filling stops immediately when adding a node would exceed `maxNodes` or `maxTokens`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class SlidingBuffer extends Buffer {
  /**
   * @param {object} options
   * @param {number} options.maxTokens - token budget
   * @param {number} options.maxNodes - max number of nodes regardless of tokens
   */
  constructor({ maxTokens, maxNodes = Infinity }) {
    super({ maxTokens, name: "sliding" });
    this.maxNodes = maxNodes;
  }

  /**
   * Fill the buffer from a chain, taking the most recent nodes that fit.
   * @param {import('./node.js').Node[]|object[]} allNodes - full chain of nodes (ordered oldest to newest)
   */
  fill(allNodes) {
    this.clear();
    // Walk backwards from most recent
    for (let i = allNodes.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const node = allNodes[i];
      const tokens = node.tokenCount || Math.ceil((node.content || "").length / 4);

      if (this.nodes.length >= this.maxNodes) break;
      if (this._tokenTotal + tokens > this.maxTokens) break;

      this.nodes.unshift(node);
      this._tokenTotal += tokens;
    }
  }
}
```

</details>

* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The method first clears any existing nodes in the buffer before processing new inputs.
    * Nodes are processed and added in reverse order (from the end of `allNodes` to the beginning), ensuring that the most recent nodes are prioritized for inclusion.
    * Addition stops immediately if adding the next node would exceed `this.maxNodes` or `this.maxTokens`.

      if (this.nodes.length >= this.maxNodes) break;
