#### `RecallBuffer(extends Buffer)` — class, line 170

* **Purpose:** Manages a collection of high-scoring nodes, ensuring the total token count remains within a specified maximum limit.
* **Inputs:**
    * `maxTokens` (number): The maximum number of tokens the buffer can hold.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The buffer assumes that input nodes provided to `fill()` are already sorted in descending order by relevance score.
    * Tokens for a node are calculated using `node.tokenCount` if available, otherwise estimated as `ceil(content length / 4)`.
    * `fill()` clears the existing buffer and adds nodes sequentially until the token limit is reached.
    * `pruneToFit(newBudget)` removes nodes from the end of the internal list (which correspond to the lowest-scoring nodes added last) until the total token count falls below `newBudget`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class RecallBuffer extends Buffer {
  constructor({ maxTokens }) {
    super({ maxTokens, name: "recall" });
  }

  /**
   * Fill the buffer with scored nodes, dropping lowest-scoring until they fit.
   * @param {{ node: object, score: number }[]} scoredNodes - nodes with relevance scores, sorted desc
   */
  fill(scoredNodes) {
    this.clear();
    // Nodes come in sorted by score (highest first)
    for (const { node } of scoredNodes) {
      const tokens = node.tokenCount || Math.ceil((node.content || "").length / 4);
      if (this._tokenTotal + tokens > this.maxTokens) break;
      this.nodes.push(node);
      this._tokenTotal += tokens;
    }
  }

  /**
   * Drop the lowest-scoring node until total fits within a new budget.
   * @param {number} newBudget
   */
  pruneToFit(newBudget) {
    while (this._tokenTotal > newBudget && this.nodes.length > 0) {
      this.pop(); // removes last (lowest score since we filled highest-first)
    }
  }
}
```

</details>

<details>
<summary><strong><code>src/core/chain.js</code></strong> — 224 lines, 16 functions</summary>

## `src/core/chain.js`

**224 lines** | 16 functions/classes

The `Chain` class serves as an in-memory data structure designed to maintain the complete, linear history of a conversation or interaction. It models this history as a sequence of "nodes," where each node represents a piece of conversational context (a user query, an assistant response, or system metadata).

**What it Does:**
The class provides comprehensive methods for managing this state:
*   **Adding Context:** It can append full conversation turns (user query paired with assistant response) using `appendTurn`, or add single-role messages (like "system" prompts or standalone user inputs) using `append`.
*   **Modification and Annotation:** Users can annotate existing nodes by adding "pocket notes," which updates the node's content and recalculates its token count.
*   **Retrieval and Management:** It allows retrieval of specific context segments, such as getting the last $N$ turns (`tail`), fetching a node by ID (`get`), or retrieving ranges of nodes.
*   **State Control:** The class supports advanced state management, including removing nodes by ID range, inserting nodes at specific positions, and methods to serialize/deserialize the entire chain for persistence.

**Role in the Project:**
The `Chain` acts as the central memory component for any conversational AI system. Its primary role is to accurately track and manage the context window—the cumulative history of dialogue turns—which is essential for providing coherent, state-aware responses from an assistant. By maintaining sequential IDs and supporting efficient retrieval (like getting the last $N$ messages), it ensures that the model always has access to the necessary conversational background.

### Exports

- `Chain` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`, `tests/chain.test.js`, `tests/compaction.test.js`, `tests/conversation.test.js`, `tests/retrieval.test.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `Chain()` — class, line 7

* **Purpose:** Manages and stores a sequential history of interactions (nodes) for an AI conversation or workflow chain.
* **Inputs:**
    * **`query`** (`string`): The user's message when calling `appendTurn`.
    * **`response`** (`string`): The assistant's response when calling `appendTurn`.
    * **`metadata`** (`object`, optional): Optional extra data for the node.
    * **`role`** (`"user"` | `"assistant"` | `"system"` | `"compaction"`): The role of the content being appended (used in `append`).
    * **`content`** (`string`): The text content associated with a specific role (used in `append`).
    * **`metadata`** (`object`, optional): Optional extra data for the node.
* **Outputs:**
    * **`object`**: A newly created and structured node representing an interaction turn or single message, containing its ID, parent relationship, content, and metadata.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The class maintains internal state (`this.nodes`) which accumulates all appended nodes.
    * `appendTurn` automatically constructs a full turn node, setting the `parentId` to reference the previous node in the chain (unless it is the first node).
    * Both methods increment `this.nextId`, ensuring unique and sequential IDs for every added node.
    * The internal structure of nodes includes calculated fields like `content` (formatted text) and `tokenCount`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class Chain {
  constructor() {
    this.nodes = [];
    this.nextId = 1;
  }

  /**
   * Append a complete turn (user query + assistant response) as a single node.
   * @param {string} query - the user's message
   * @param {string} response - the assistant's response
   * @param {object} metadata - optional extra data
   * @returns {object} the created node
   */
  appendTurn(query, response, metadata = {}) {
    const content = `[user]: ${query}\n[assistant]: ${response}`;
    const node = {
      id: this.nextId,
      parentId: this.nextId > 1 ? this.nextId - 1 : 0,
      role: "turn",
      query,
      response,
      content,
      timestamp: Date.now(),
      tokenCount: estimateTokens(content),
      pocketNotes: [],
      metadata
    };
    this.nodes.push(node);
    this.nextId++;
    return node;
  }

  /**
   * Append a single-role node (for system messages, compaction markers, or pending user queries).
   * @param {string} role - "user" | "assistant" | "system" | "compaction"
   * @param {string} content - the text content
   * @param {object} metadata - optional extra data
   * @returns {object} the created node
   */
```

#### `append(role, content, metadata = {})` — method, line 46

* **Purpose:** Appends a new conversational turn (node) to the chain history, updating internal state and returning the newly created node object.
* **Inputs:**
    * `role` (string): The role of the speaker ("user" or "assistant").
    * `content` (any): The textual content associated with this turn.
    * `metadata` (object): Optional key-value pairs for additional metadata attached to the node.
* **Outputs:** object: A complete representation of the newly created node, including IDs, timestamps, and token counts.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Mutates state by pushing the new node into `this.nodes`.
    * Increments `this.nextId` after successful appending.
    * The `parentId` is automatically set to the ID of the previous node in the chain (or 0 if it's the first node).
    * Populates the `query` field if `role` is "user", and populates the `response` field if `role` is "assistant".

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  append(role, content, metadata = {}) {
    const node = {
      id: this.nextId,
      parentId: this.nextId > 1 ? this.nextId - 1 : 0,
      role,
      query: role === "user" ? content : "",
      response: role === "assistant" ? content : "",
      content,
      timestamp: Date.now(),
      tokenCount: estimateTokens(content),
      pocketNotes: [],
      metadata
    };
    this.nodes.push(node);
    this.nextId++;
    return node;
  }
```

#### `addPocketNote(nodeId, note)` — method, line 71

* **Purpose:** Adds a new pocket note to the specified node and updates the node's token count accordingly.
* **Inputs:**
    * `nodeId` (string): The unique identifier of the node to which the note should be added.
    * `note` (string): The content of the note to be saved.
* **Outputs:** Node object or null: Returns the updated node object if successful, otherwise returns `null` if the node ID is invalid.
* **Behavioral Notes:** Modifies the state of the target node by appending the new note to `pocketNotes`. It recalculates and updates the node's `tokenCount` based on the combined content of the main body and all pocket notes. If the node does not exist, no changes are made, and `null` is returned.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  addPocketNote(nodeId, note) {
    const node = this.get(nodeId);
    if (!node) return null;
    if (!node.pocketNotes) node.pocketNotes = [];
    node.pocketNotes.push({
      timestamp: Date.now(),
      content: note
    });
    // Update token count to reflect the added note
    const fullContent = node.content + ' ' + node.pocketNotes.map(n => n.content).join(' ');
    node.tokenCount = estimateTokens(fullContent);
    return node;
  }
```

</details>

#### `remove(startId, endId)` — method, line 127

* **Purpose:** Removes all nodes from the chain whose IDs fall inclusively between `startId` and `endId`, modifying the internal node list.
* **Inputs:**
    * `startId`: Number/String - The starting ID of the range (inclusive).
    * `endId`: Number/String - The ending ID of the range (inclusive).
* **Outputs:** Array<Node> - An array containing all the nodes that were successfully removed from the chain.
* **Behavioral Notes:** Mutates the internal state (`this.nodes`) by filtering out the specified range of nodes. If `startId` is greater than `endId`, no nodes will be removed, and an empty array will be returned.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  remove(startId, endId) {
    const removed = [];
    this.nodes = this.nodes.filter(n => {
      if (n.id >= startId && n.id <= endId) {
        removed.push(n);
        return false;
      }
      return true;
    });
    return removed;
  }
```

</details>

#### `insert(node)` — method, line 143

* **Purpose:** Inserts a new node into the internal list, maintaining sorted order based on the node's ID, and updates the expected next available ID.
* **Inputs:**
    * `node`: An object that must contain an `id` property used for sorting and state management.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Modifies the internal array (`this.nodes`) by inserting the node at the correct sorted position. It also updates `this.nextId` to ensure it is always greater than or equal to the ID of the newly inserted node plus one, preventing future ID conflicts.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  insert(node) {
    const idx = this.nodes.findIndex(n => n.id > node.id);
    if (idx === -1) {
      this.nodes.push(node);
    } else {
      this.nodes.splice(idx, 0, node);
    }
    if (node.id >= this.nextId) {
      this.nextId = node.id + 1;
    }
  }
```

</details>

## `src/core/compaction.js`

**287 lines** | 12 functions/classes

The `Compaction` class serves as the core engine for managing the data lifecycle of nodes within the system, handling the movement of historical content from active memory to persistent cold storage (IndexedDB).

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to prevent memory bloat and optimize performance by systematically archiving older or less relevant node data while maintaining a searchable history. It manages the creation and maintenance of "compaction markers" that reference archived blocks, allowing the system to know what data exists in cold storage without keeping it active in memory.

**Functionality:**
The class provides comprehensive methods for various archival scenarios:

*   **Archiving (`trim`):** Moves a specified range of nodes from active memory into cold storage, creating a marker that summarizes the archived block's content (including TF-IDF vectors and Bloom filter data). It then removes the original nodes from the active chain and search indexes.
*   **Selective Archival:** Offers specialized methods like `trimKeepRange` (archiving everything before or after a specified window), `branchFrom` (archiving history up to a certain point for a new session), and `trimBeforeWindow` (clearing old data outside the current active viewing window).
*   **Session Management (`branch`):** Archives all existing nodes, effectively starting a fresh, clean session.
*   **Restoration (`restore`):** Retrieves an archived block from cold storage using its key, re-inserts the nodes into the active memory chain, and updates the search indexes accordingly.

**Role in Project:**
The `Compaction` class is critical for system scalability and efficiency. It acts as the gatekeeper between volatile (active) and persistent (cold) data stores. By managing this archival process, it ensures that the active working set remains small and fast to query, while still preserving a complete, indexed history of all user interactions or generated content.

### Exports

- `Compaction` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`, `tests/compaction.test.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `Compaction()` — class, line 6

* **Purpose:** Manages the process of archiving a range of data nodes from active memory into cold storage, updating various indices and metadata in the process.
* **Inputs:**
    * `chain` (`object`): The underlying data chain or structure used to retrieve node ranges.
    * `bm25` (`object`): A BM25 index component used for calculating term frequencies (though not explicitly used in `trim`, it is a dependency).
    * `bloom` (`object`): A Bloom filter implementation used to track terms present in the archived block.
    * `tfidf` (`object`): The TF-IDF vectorizer/calculator used to compute and register the content vector for the archived block.
    * `archive` (`object`): The storage mechanism responsible for persisting data into cold storage.
    * `config` (`object`): Configuration settings required for compaction operations.
* **Outputs:**
    * `trim(startId, endId, summary)`: Promise resolving to an object representing the newly created compaction marker node metadata.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The class maintains internal state tracking references to compaction marker metadata via `this.markers`.
    * The `trim` method performs several side effects: it reads data from `this.chain`, computes and registers a TF-IDF vector using `this.tfidf`, updates the Bloom filter using `this.bloom`, and asynchronously stores the raw node data in cold storage using `this.archive`.
    * If no nodes are found within the specified range (`startId` to `endId`), it throws an error.

```javascript
export class Compaction {
  constructor(chain, bm25, bloom, tfidf, archive, config) {
    this.chain = chain;
    this.bm25 = bm25;
    this.bloom = bloom;
    this.tfidf = tfidf;
    this.archive = archive;
    this.config = config;
    this.markers = []; // references to compaction marker metadata
  }

    // Extract nodes to archive

    // Compress and store in cold storage

    await this.archive.store(archiveKey, nodesToArchive);
```

#### `trim(startId, endId, summary)` — method, line 24

* **Purpose:** Compresses a specified range of nodes into cold storage and creates a marker node summarizing the archived content and metadata.
* **Inputs:**
    * `startId` (number): The starting ID of the node range to be compacted (inclusive).
    * `endId` (number): The ending ID of the node range to be compacted (inclusive).
    * `summary` (object, optional): Optional summary data containing topics, key decisions, or open threads for the marker metadata.
* **Outputs:** Promise<void> - Does not return a value upon successful completion.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Throws an error if no nodes are found within the specified `startId` to `endId` range.
    * Side effects include:
        * Archiving the content of all nodes in the range using `this.archive.store()`.
        * Updating the TF-IDF model by registering the concatenated text (`this.tfidf.registerBlock()`).
        * Updating the Bloom filter with the concatenated text (`this.bloom.addText()`).
    * A new "compaction" marker node is created and conceptually inserted into the chain immediately after `endId`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async trim(startId, endId, summary) {
    // Extract nodes to archive
    const nodesToArchive = this.chain.range(startId, endId);
    if (nodesToArchive.length === 0) {
      throw new Error(`No nodes found in range ${startId}-${endId}`);
    }

    // Concatenate all content for indexing
    const fullText = nodesToArchive.map(n => n.content).join(" ");

    // Compute TF-IDF vector for this block
    const vector = this.tfidf.computeVector(fullText);
    this.tfidf.registerBlock(fullText);

    // Add terms to bloom filter
    const bloomTermCount = this.bloom.addText(fullText);

    // Compress and store in cold storage
    const archiveKey = `archive_${startId}_${endId}`;
    await this.archive.store(archiveKey, nodesToArchive);

    // Create compaction marker
    const marker = {
      id: endId + 0.5, // will be positioned after endId in the chain
      parentId: startId > 1 ? startId - 1 : 0,
      role: "compaction",
      content: "",
      timestamp: Date.now(),
      tokenCount: 0,
      metadata: {
        type: "compaction",
        startNode: startId,
        endNode: endId,
        nodeCount: nodesToArchive.length,
        summary: summary || { startTopic: "", keyDecisions: [], openThreads: [] },
        archiveKey,
        tfidfVector: TfidfSerialize(vector),
        bloomTerms: bloomTermCount
      }
    };
  // ... (truncated)
```

#### `trimKeepRange(keepStartId, keepEndId, beforeSummary = null, afterSummary = null)` — method, line 100

* **Purpose:** Archives all content nodes that fall chronologically before or after a specified "keep range" within the current data chain.
* **Inputs:**
    * `keepStartId` (`number`): The ID of the first node that should be kept (inclusive).
    * `keepEndId` (`number`): The ID of the last node that should be kept (inclusive).
    * `beforeSummary` (`object`, optional): A pre-calculated summary object to use when archiving content before the keep range.
    * `afterSummary` (`object`, optional): A pre-calculated summary object to use when archiving content after the keep range.
* **Outputs:** `{ before: Object | null, after: Object | null }`: An object containing two properties, each representing the resulting marker (summary/ID) for the archived 'before' and 'after' sections, respectively.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The method modifies the internal state of `this.chain` by calling `await this.trim()` twice if content needs archiving. If no nodes exist in the chain, it returns `{ before: null, after: null }`. When calculating the summary for the 'after' section, it explicitly filters out any nodes that might have been marked as "compaction" during the first trimming step.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async trimKeepRange(keepStartId, keepEndId, beforeSummary = null, afterSummary = null) {
    const allNodes = this.chain.all();
    if (allNodes.length === 0) return { before: null, after: null };

    const firstId = allNodes[0].id;
    const lastId = allNodes[allNodes.length - 1].id;

    let beforeMarker = null;
    let afterMarker = null;

    // Archive everything BEFORE the keep range
    if (keepStartId > firstId) {
      const beforeNodes = this.chain.range(firstId, keepStartId - 1);
      if (beforeNodes.length > 0) {
        const summary = beforeSummary || {
          startTopic: (beforeNodes[0].query || beforeNodes[0].content || "").slice(0, 80),
          keyDecisions: [],
          openThreads: []
        };
        beforeMarker = await this.trim(firstId, beforeNodes[beforeNodes.length - 1].id, summary);
      }
    }

    // Archive everything AFTER the keep range
    // Re-fetch allNodes since trim modified the chain
    const remainingNodes = this.chain.all();
    const nodesAfter = remainingNodes.filter(n => n.id > keepEndId && n.role !== "compaction");
    if (nodesAfter.length > 0) {
      const afterStartId = nodesAfter[0].id;
      const afterEndId = nodesAfter[nodesAfter.length - 1].id;
      const summary = afterSummary || {
        startTopic: (nodesAfter[0].query || nodesAfter[0].content || "").slice(0, 80),
        keyDecisions: [],
        openThreads: []
      };
      afterMarker = await this.trim(afterStartId, afterEndId, summary);
    }

    return { before: beforeMarker, after: afterMarker };
  }
```

</details>

#### `branchFrom(fromNodeId, summary = null)` — method, line 149

* **Purpose:** Creates a new branch history by trimming the current node chain up to a specified `fromNodeId`, archiving relevant summary data in the process.
* **Inputs:**
    * `fromNodeId` (number): The ID of the node from which the new branch should start.
    * `summary` (object, optional): An existing summary object containing metadata for the archived history (e.g., `startTopic`, `keyDecisions`, `openThreads`). If null or undefined, a default summary is generated based on the nodes preceding the branch point.
* **Outputs:** Promise<void | object>: A promise that resolves to `null` if no trimming/branching occurs, or an object representing the result of the underlying `this.trim()` operation.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If the internal node chain (`this.chain`) is empty, it returns `null`.
    * If `fromNodeId` is less than or equal to the ID of the very first node in the chain, no history exists to archive, and it returns `null`.
    * The method calculates a summary based on all nodes *before* `fromNodeId`. This summary includes the content/query of the earliest preceding node.
    * It relies on calling `this.trim()` internally, which performs the actual state mutation (archiving/truncating the history).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async branchFrom(fromNodeId, summary = null) {
    const allNodes = this.chain.all();
    if (allNodes.length === 0) return null;

    const firstId = allNodes[0].id;

    // Nothing to archive if we're branching from the first node
    if (fromNodeId <= firstId) return null;

    const beforeNodes = this.chain.range(firstId, fromNodeId - 1);
    if (beforeNodes.length === 0) return null;

    const archiveSummary = summary || {
      startTopic: (beforeNodes[0].query || beforeNodes[0].content || "").slice(0, 80),
      keyDecisions: [],
      openThreads: []
    };

    return this.trim(firstId, beforeNodes[beforeNodes.length - 1].id, archiveSummary);
  }
```

</details>

#### `trimBeforeWindow(windowSize, summary)` — method, line 176

* **Purpose:** Trims the internal chain to retain only the most recent `windowSize` nodes, returning null if no trimming is necessary.
* **Inputs:**
    * `windowSize`: Number representing the desired minimum number of nodes to keep at the end of the chain.
    * `summary`: Object containing summary data passed through to the underlying trim operation.
* **Outputs:** Promise resolving to null (if no trimming occurs) or the result of the internal `this.trim()` method, representing the state after compaction.
* **Behavioral Notes:** If the total number of nodes in the chain is less than or equal to `windowSize`, the function immediately returns `null` without performing any trimming operations.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async trimBeforeWindow(windowSize, summary) {
    const totalNodes = this.chain.length;
    if (totalNodes <= windowSize) return null;

    const allNodes = this.chain.all();
    const cutoffIdx = totalNodes - windowSize;
    const startId = allNodes[0].id;
    const endId = allNodes[cutoffIdx - 1].id;

    return this.trim(startId, endId, summary);
  }
```

</details>

#### `branch(summary)` — method, line 193

* **Purpose:** Trims and archives the current chain segment defined by the first and last node IDs, returning a unique identifier for the archived branch.
* **Inputs:** summary (`Object`): A summary object passed to the underlying `trim` operation.
* **Outputs:** string | null: An archive identifier string (e.g., "archive_startId_endId"), or `null` if the chain contains no nodes.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The method relies on the internal state of `this.chain`. It performs a side effect by calling `this.trim()`, which is responsible for modifying or cleaning up the data represented by the chain segment. If `this.chain` is empty, it returns `null` immediately without performing any trimming operations.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async branch(summary) {
    const allNodes = this.chain.all();
    if (allNodes.length === 0) return null;

    const startId = allNodes[0].id;
    const endId = allNodes[allNodes.length - 1].id;

    await this.trim(startId, endId, summary);
    return `archive_${startId}_${endId}`;
  }
```

</details>

#### `restore(archiveKey)` — method, line 208

* **Purpose:** Restores a set of archived nodes associated with a given key, re-integrating them into the active system indexes and cleaning up all related metadata.
* **Inputs:**
    * `archiveKey` (string): The unique identifier used to retrieve the archived nodes from cold storage.
* **Outputs:** None (async void)
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This method performs multiple side effects, modifying the internal state of the class instance (`this`).
    * It first retrieves all associated nodes from `this.archive` and throws an error if no nodes are found for the key.
    * It removes any existing "compaction" marker referencing this `archiveKey` from `this.chain`.
    * The retrieved nodes are re-inserted into `this.chain`, and non-compaction roles are added back to `this.bm25`.
    * It updates several external indexes: `this.bloom` (removes the combined content), `this.tfidf` (unregisters the block).
    * It filters out the `archiveKey` from `this.markers`.
    * Finally, it deletes the archive data from cold storage using `this.archive.delete(archiveKey)`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async restore(archiveKey) {
    // Fetch from cold storage
    const nodes = await this.archive.retrieve(archiveKey);
    if (!nodes || nodes.length === 0) {
      throw new Error(`Archive not found: ${archiveKey}`);
    }

    // Find and remove the compaction marker referencing this key
    const markerNode = this.chain.all().find(
      n => n.role === "compaction" && n.metadata?.archiveKey === archiveKey
    );
    if (markerNode) {
      this.chain.removeById(markerNode.id);
    }

    // Re-insert nodes into chain
    this.chain.insertMany(nodes);

    // Re-add to BM25 index
    for (const node of nodes) {
      if (node.role !== "compaction") {
        this.bm25.add(node);
      }
    }

    // Decrement bloom filter
    const fullText = nodes.map(n => n.content).join(" ");
    this.bloom.removeText(fullText);

    // Unregister from TF-IDF
    this.tfidf.unregisterBlock(fullText);

    // Remove from markers list
    this.markers = this.markers.filter(m => m.archiveKey !== archiveKey);

    // Delete from cold storage
    await this.archive.delete(archiveKey);
  }
```

</details>

```javascript
      markers: this.markers.map(m => ({
        ...m,

```

## `src/core/conversation-manager.js`

**144 lines** | 8 functions/classes

The `ConversationManager` is a core utility responsible for managing and structuring the entire context window sent to a large language model (LLM). Its primary purpose is to ensure that all components of the prompt—including system instructions, attached documents, historical conversation snippets, and recent dialogue—fit within the model's strict token limit.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Budget Calculation:** It calculates the available token budget by subtracting reserved tokens and initial system/attached context costs from the maximum allowed tokens.
2.  **Context Management:** It manages three types of contextual data:
    *   **Attached Context:** Static, persistent information (like PDFs or code) that must be included in every prompt.
    *   **Recall Buffer:** Historical knowledge retrieved from a database, which is allocated a configurable portion of the budget.
    *   **Sliding Buffer:** The most recent conversation turns, ensuring conversational continuity.
3.  **Prompt Assembly:** It orchestrates the final prompt construction by allocating the available tokens between the recall and sliding buffers, filling them with relevant data (while respecting token limits), and assembling a structured array of messages ready for the LLM API call.

**Role in the Project:**
The `ConversationManager` acts as the central gatekeeper and assembler for all input context. It abstracts away the complexity of token budgeting and prompt formatting, allowing other parts of the application to simply provide raw data (e.g., a list of chat nodes or retrieved documents), while the manager handles the necessary calculations and structuring required to generate a coherent, budget-compliant prompt.

### Exports

- `ConversationManager` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/core/buffer.js`

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`, `tests/conversation.test.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `ConversationManager()` — class, line 21

* **Purpose:** Manages the construction of comprehensive LLM prompts by calculating available token budgets and assembling structured inputs from various sources like conversation history, attached documents, and external knowledge retrieval.
* **Inputs:**
    * `config` (object): Configuration object containing parameters like `maxTokenBudget`, `reservedTokens`, and `recallBufferRatio`.
* **Outputs:**
    * `getBudget()`: An object `{ total: number, system: number, available: number }` representing the token budget breakdown.
    * `buildPrompt()`: An object `{ messages: object[], slidingBuffer: object, recallBuffer: object }` containing the structured components needed for the final prompt assembly.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The class maintains an internal state (`this.attachedContext`) used to augment context and calculate token usage.
    * `getBudget()` calculates available tokens by subtracting reserved system tokens from the maximum budget, ensuring a non-negative result.
    * `buildPrompt()` dynamically allocates the available token budget between the recall buffer (up to 30% or configured ratio) and the sliding window/main message history.
    * The prompt construction process is designed to ensure that all assembled components fit within the calculated token budget.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class ConversationManager {
  constructor(config) {
    this.config = config;
    this.attachedContext = []; // additional context (PDFs, code, etc.)
  }

  /**
   * Calculate the total available token budget after system prompt + attached context.
   * @returns {{ total: number, system: number, available: number }}
   */
  getBudget() {
    const total = this.config.maxTokenBudget - this.config.reservedTokens;
    const systemTokens = this._systemTokens();
    const available = Math.max(0, total - systemTokens);
    return { total, system: systemTokens, available };
  }

  /**
   * Build the full prompt: system + recall + sliding window.
   * Ensures everything fits within budget.
   * 
   * @param {object[]} chainNodes - all nodes in the chain
   * @param {{ node: object, score: number }[]} retrievedNodes - scored recall candidates
   * @returns {{ messages: object[], slidingBuffer: object, recallBuffer: object }}
   */
  buildPrompt(chainNodes, retrievedNodes = []) {
    const { available } = this.getBudget();
    
    // Split available budget between recall and sliding
    // Recall gets up to 30% of available, sliding gets the rest
    const recallRatio = this.config.recallBufferRatio || 0.3;
    const recallBudget = Math.floor(available * recallRatio);
    const slidingBudget = available - recallBudget;

    // Fill recall buffer (best-matching historical nodes, pruned to fit)
    const recallBuffer = new RecallBuffer({ maxTokens: recallBudget });
    if (retrievedNodes.length > 0) {
      recallBuffer.fill(retrievedNodes);
    }

  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

```javascript
  getBudget() {
    const total = this.config.maxTokenBudget - this.config.reservedTokens;
    const systemTokens = this._systemTokens();
    const available = Math.max(0, total - systemTokens);
    return { total, system: systemTokens, available };
  }
```

</details>

#### `buildPrompt(chainNodes, retrievedNodes = [])` — method, line 46

* **Purpose:** Constructs a structured list of messages (a prompt) for an LLM by combining system instructions, attached context, and relevant conversation history nodes while managing token budgets.
* **Inputs:**
    * `chainNodes` (Array): Nodes representing the most recent or core conversational history used to fill the sliding window buffer.
    * `retrievedNodes` (Array): Optional array of historical nodes retrieved from memory (used for the recall buffer).
* **Outputs:** Array<Object> - A list of message objects formatted for an LLM API call, containing roles ("system") and content strings.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The method dynamically calculates token budgets by splitting the available context window budget between a "recall" component (for historical memory) and a "sliding" component (for recent conversation history). It relies heavily on internal state (`this.config`, `this.attachedContext`) for system prompts and fixed context blocks, which are prepended to the final message array.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  buildPrompt(chainNodes, retrievedNodes = []) {
    const { available } = this.getBudget();
    
    // Split available budget between recall and sliding
    // Recall gets up to 30% of available, sliding gets the rest
    const recallRatio = this.config.recallBufferRatio || 0.3;
    const recallBudget = Math.floor(available * recallRatio);
    const slidingBudget = available - recallBudget;

    // Fill recall buffer (best-matching historical nodes, pruned to fit)
    const recallBuffer = new RecallBuffer({ maxTokens: recallBudget });
    if (retrievedNodes.length > 0) {
      recallBuffer.fill(retrievedNodes);
    }

    // Give unused recall budget to sliding
    const actualRecallUsed = recallBuffer.tokenCount;
    const adjustedSlidingBudget = slidingBudget + (recallBudget - actualRecallUsed);

    // Fill sliding buffer (most recent nodes that fit)
    const slidingBuffer = new SlidingBuffer({
      maxTokens: adjustedSlidingBudget,
      maxNodes: this.config.windowSize
    });
    slidingBuffer.fill(chainNodes);

    // Assemble final messages array
    const messages = [];

    // System prompt
    messages.push({ role: "system", content: this.config.systemPrompt });

    // Attached context (if any)
    if (this.attachedContext.length > 0) {
      const ctxBlock = this.attachedContext
        .map(c => `[${c.type}]: ${c.content}`)
        .join("\n\n");
      messages.push({ role: "system", content: ctxBlock });
    }

  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `_systemTokens()` — method, line 136

* **Purpose:** Calculates an estimated total number of tokens consumed by the system prompt and all attached context materials.
* **Inputs:** None (Relies on internal state via `this`)
    * `this.config.systemPrompt`: The string content of the system prompt.
    * `this.attachedContext`: An array of objects, where each object must contain a `content` string property.
* **Outputs:** Number representing the estimated total token count.
* **Behavioral Notes:** Token counting is an approximation based on dividing character length by 4 and rounding up (`Math.ceil`). If `this.config.systemPrompt` is undefined, it defaults to zero tokens. The function only counts context/system material, not user input or model output.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  _systemTokens() {
    let tokens = Math.ceil((this.config.systemPrompt || "").length / 4);
    for (const ctx of this.attachedContext) {
      tokens += Math.ceil(ctx.content.length / 4);
    }
    return tokens;
  }
```

</details>

</details>

## `src/core/node.js`

**126 lines** | 3 functions/classes

The `Node` class serves as the fundamental data structure for representing a single unit of conversation within an application (e.g., one user message or one AI response).

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to model and manage conversational history in a structured, self-contained manner, allowing developers to track not only the content but also critical metadata and resource usage associated with that unit.

**What it Does:**
The class encapsulates conversation details (ID, parent ID, role, content, timestamp) and provides several utility functions:
*   **Metrics Calculation:** It automatically calculates estimated metrics for the node's content, including token count, memory size, character count, and word count, which is crucial for budgeting API calls.
*   **Role Handling:** It supports various roles ("user," "assistant," "system") and includes specialized logic for handling "compaction" markers, allowing it to summarize large segments of conversation history.
*   **Serialization/Deserialization:** It provides methods (`toJSON` and `static from`) to easily convert the object into a plain JSON format for storage or transmission, and vice versa.
*   **API Formatting:** The `toMessage()` method formats the node's data into the specific structure required by external Large Language Model (LLM) APIs.

**Role in the Project:**
The `Node` class acts as the core building block for managing conversation state. It structures the entire dialogue history, enabling the system to accurately track context, manage resource consumption, and efficiently prepare summarized or segmented conversations for interaction with AI models.

### Exports

- `Node` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `Node()` — class, line 5

* **Purpose:** Represents a single, structured message or node within a conversation history or chat thread.
* **Inputs:**
    * `id` (number): Sequential unique identifier for this node.
    * `parentId` (number): The ID of the parent node; 0 if this is a root message.
    * `role` (string): Defines the sender's role ("user", "assistant", "system", or "compaction").
    * `content` (string): The textual content of the message.
    * `metadata` (object): Optional extensible metadata associated with the node.
    * `timestamp` (number): Unix timestamp in milliseconds when the node was created (defaults to current time).
* **Outputs:** An instance of the Node class, representing a complete chat history entry.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The `tokenCount` and `memSize` properties are calculated estimates that are cached upon first access for performance optimization. Token count is estimated using a heuristic of dividing content length by 4.

```javascript
export class Node {
  /**
   * @param {object} params

   * @param {string} params.role - "user" | "assistant" | "system" | "compaction"
   * @param {string} params.content - the text content
   * @param {object} [params.metadata] - optional extensible metadata
   * @param {number} [params.timestamp] - unix ms (defaults to now)
   */
  constructor({ id, parentId, role, content, metadata = {}, timestamp = Date.now() }) {
    this.id = id;
    this.parentId = parentId;
    this.role = role;
    this.content = content;
    this.metadata = metadata;
    this.timestamp = timestamp;

    // Pre-computed sizing
    this._tokenCount = null;
    this._memSize = null;
  }

  /**
   * Estimated token count (~4 chars per token for English).
   * Cached after first calculation.
   * @returns {number}
   */
  get tokenCount() {
    if (this._tokenCount === null) {
      this._tokenCount = Math.ceil((this.content || "").length / 4);
    }
    return this._tokenCount;
  }

  /**
   * Estimated memory size in bytes (JS strings are UTF-16 + object overhead).
   * @returns {number}
   */
```

```javascript
  toMessage() {
    if (this.isCompaction) {
      const summary = this.metadata?.summary;
      if (summary) {
        return {
          role: "system",
          content: `[Archived summary - Nodes ${this.metadata.startNode}-${this.metadata.endNode}]: ${JSON.stringify(summary)}`
        };
      }
      return null;
    }
    return { role: this.role, content: this.content };
  }
```

## `src/core/pocket.js`

**146 lines** | 12 functions/classes

The `Pocket` class serves as a dedicated, persistent instruction queue that manages user input and corrections asynchronously relative to the main system execution flow. Its primary purpose is to act as a buffer where user intent accumulates until the core system is ready to process it.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Queuing (`add`):** Users can add notes (instructions, corrections, waypoints) at any time. These notes are stored in a pending queue and do not immediately affect the running process.
2.  **Consumption (`consume`):** The system consumes these queued notes at specific checkpoints. This action removes the note from the pending queue and applies its content to the next step of execution, simulating the application of user intent.
3.  **State Management:** It maintains a clear separation between pending notes (the `queue`) and notes that have already been applied (the `consumed` history). It also provides methods to peek at the next note or consume all pending notes simultaneously.

**Role in the Project:**
The `Pocket` acts as a "liminal space" or parallel instruction queue, decoupling user input from system processing. By accumulating instructions separately, it allows users to provide continuous feedback and guidance without interrupting the current workflow. The core system relies on calling `consume()` at appropriate checkpoints to retrieve and apply the next batch of accumulated user intent, ensuring that all queued notes are eventually factored into the final output or process steps.

### Exports

- `Pocket` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `Pocket()` — class, line 11

* **Purpose:** Manages a sequential queue of instructional notes that can be added by the user and consumed by the system at specific checkpoints.
* **Inputs:**
    * `content` (string): The instruction or correction content to be queued.
    * `meta` (object, optional): Optional metadata associated with the note (e.g., target step, priority).
* **Outputs:**
    * `add()`: object - The newly created and queued note object.
    * `consume()`: object|null - The consumed note object if available, or `null` if the queue is empty.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * State Mutation: The class maintains two internal arrays: `queue` (pending notes) and `consumed` (history). Both are mutated upon calling `add` or `consume`.
    * Side Effects: Calls `console.log` messages to indicate when a note is queued or consumed.
    * Queue Management: Notes are processed in FIFO (First-In, First-Out) order; `consume()` uses `shift()` on the internal queue.
    * Note ID Generation: The unique ID for a note is generated based on the current length of both the queue and the consumed history, ensuring monotonically increasing IDs across all operations.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class Pocket {
  constructor() {
    this.queue = [];       // pending notes waiting to be consumed
    this.consumed = [];    // notes that have been applied (history)
    this.listeners = [];   // callbacks for when notes are added
  }

  /**
   * Queue a pocket note. Called by the user at any time.
   * @param {string} content - the instruction/correction
   * @param {object} [meta] - optional metadata (target step, priority, etc.)
   * @returns {object} the queued note
   */
  add(content, meta = {}) {
    const note = {
      id: this.queue.length + this.consumed.length + 1,
      content,
      timestamp: Date.now(),
      consumed: false,
      consumedAt: null,
      meta
    };
    this.queue.push(note);
    this._emit(note);
    console.log(`[Pocket] Queued #${note.id}: "${content.slice(0, 60)}"`);
    return note;
  }

  /**
   * Consume the next queued note. Called by the system at checkpoints.
   * Returns the note content or null if queue is empty.
   * @returns {object|null} the consumed note, or null
   */
  consume() {
    if (this.queue.length === 0) return null;
    const note = this.queue.shift();
    note.consumed = true;
    note.consumedAt = Date.now();
    this.consumed.push(note);
    console.log(`[Pocket] Consumed #${note.id}: "${note.content.slice(0, 60)}"`);
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

* **Purpose:** Notifies all registered event listeners (callbacks) with a given note, ensuring that failures in one listener do not prevent others from being called.

<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  _emit(note) {

      try { cb(note); } catch (e) { /* swallow */ }
    }
  }
```

<a id="srccoreretrievaljs"></a>
<details>

## `src/core/retrieval.js`

**131 lines** | 5 functions/classes

The `Retrieval` class serves as the central **orchestration engine** for the entire search pipeline, responsible for finding and retrieving relevant historical context nodes based on a user query.

**What it Does:**
It executes a multi-stage retrieval process:
1.  **Initial Check (Active Window):** It first runs a BM25 score against the current active window of nodes. If the match is highly confident, no further searching is needed.
2.  **Archive Filtering (Bloom Filter & TF-IDF):** If the initial check fails, it uses a Bloom filter to quickly determine if the query has any potential matches in the deep archive. If positive, it ranks all archived blocks using TF-IDF similarity to identify the most promising candidates.
3.  **Deep Search:** It retrieves and decompresses the top candidate archive blocks. Finally, it performs an inner BM25 search within these retrieved nodes to pinpoint the most relevant historical context.

**Role in the Project:**
This class is critical infrastructure that bridges the gap between a user's query and the project's knowledge base. Its role is to intelligently decide *where* to look (active window vs. deep archive) and *how* to score relevance, ensuring that only the most contextually relevant historical nodes are extracted for injection into subsequent prompts or processing steps.

### Exports

- `Retrieval` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `Retrieval()` — class, line 7

* **Purpose:** Manages a complex, multi-stage retrieval pipeline that determines relevant historical nodes for injecting into prompts based on user queries and current context.
* **Inputs:**
    * `chain` (object): A dependency object representing the overall processing chain.
    * `bm25` (object): An interface implementing BM25 scoring logic, used to check immediate relevance within the active window.
    * `bloom` (object): An interface using a Bloom filter to quickly determine if relevant terms exist in archived data.
    * `tfidf` (object): An interface handling TF-IDF calculations for ranking archive blocks.
    * `compaction` (object): An interface responsible for managing and retrieving block vectors from compacted archives.
    * `archive` (object): A dependency object representing the historical or archived knowledge base.
    * `transport` (object): A dependency object handling data transport or communication.
    * `config` (object): Configuration settings, including `retrievalThreshold` and `maxRetrievedNodes`.
    * `query` (string): The user's input message used for retrieval.
    * `windowNodes` (object[]): An array of nodes representing the current context or sliding window.
* **Outputs:**
    * `Promise<{ nodes: object[], deepResponse: string|null }>`: A promise resolving to an object containing `nodes` (an array of retrieved historical nodes) and `deepResponse` (a detailed response string, which is null if simple retrieval was used).
* **Behavioral Notes:** The retrieval process follows a strict hierarchy: 1. Check BM25 confidence on the active window; if high enough, no further search occurs. 2. If not confident, check Bloom filter against archives; if negative, no archive search occurs. 3. If both checks pass, TF-IDF is used to rank and retrieve relevant blocks from the compacted archive.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class Retrieval {
  constructor(chain, bm25, bloom, tfidf, compaction, archive, transport, config) {
    this.chain = chain;
    this.bm25 = bm25;
    this.bloom = bloom;
    this.tfidf = tfidf;
    this.compaction = compaction;
    this.archive = archive;
    this.transport = transport;
    this.config = config;
  }

  /**
   * Full retrieval pipeline. Returns historical nodes to inject into the prompt.
   * For simple queries: returns nodes for injection.
   * For complex queries: returns null (deep retrieval handles the full response).
   * 
   * @param {string} query - the user's message
   * @param {object[]} windowNodes - current sliding window nodes
   * @returns {{ nodes: object[], deepResponse: string|null }}
   */
  async retrieve(query, windowNodes) {
    const { retrievalThreshold, maxRetrievedNodes } = this.config;

    // Step 1: Check BM25 confidence on active window
    const windowScore = this.bm25.bestScore(query);

    // If active window has a strong match, no need to dig into archives
    if (windowScore >= retrievalThreshold) {
      return { nodes: [], deepResponse: null };
    }

    // Step 2: Check if any archived blocks might contain relevant terms
    if (!this.bloom.testQuery(query)) {
      // Bloom filter says definitely not in any archive
      return { nodes: [], deepResponse: null };
    }

    // Step 3: Rank archive blocks by TF-IDF cosine similarity
    const blockVectors = this.compaction.getBlockVectors();
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

    * **bm25** (`Object`): An implementation of the BM25 ranking algorithm.
    * **bloom** (`Object`): A Bloom filter instance used for efficient set membership testing.
    * **tfidf** (`Object`): The mechanism responsible for calculating or storing TF-IDF scores.
    * **compaction** (`Object`): An object handling data compaction routines.
    * **archive** (`Object`): An interface or service dedicated to archiving data.
    * **transport** (`Object`): The transport layer used for communication (e.g., network connection).
    * **config** (`Object`): General configuration settings for the retrieval system.

```javascript
    this.chain = chain;
```

#### `retrieve(query, windowNodes)` — method, line 28

* **Purpose:** Executes a multi-stage retrieval process to find relevant nodes for a given query, first checking the active window context and then searching compressed archives if necessary.
* **Inputs:**
    * `query` (string): The search query used to retrieve information.
    * `windowNodes` (Array<any>): Nodes belonging to the current active context or window.
* **Outputs:** An object `{ nodes: Array<any>, deepResponse: null }` containing the retrieved relevant nodes and a potential deep response structure.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The retrieval process is highly optimized, exiting early if the local window provides a strong match (BM25 score >= `retrievalThreshold`).
    * It uses a Bloom filter (`this.bloom`) to quickly determine if any archived blocks are likely relevant before performing expensive TF-IDF ranking.
    * Retrieval is limited to searching within the top 2 most relevant archive blocks identified by TF-IDF cosine similarity.
    * The method relies on several internal components: `this.bm25`, `this.bloom`, `this.tfidf`, and `this.archive`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async retrieve(query, windowNodes) {
    const { retrievalThreshold, maxRetrievedNodes } = this.config;

    // Step 1: Check BM25 confidence on active window
    const windowScore = this.bm25.bestScore(query);

    // If active window has a strong match, no need to dig into archives
    if (windowScore >= retrievalThreshold) {
      return { nodes: [], deepResponse: null };
    }

    // Step 2: Check if any archived blocks might contain relevant terms
    if (!this.bloom.testQuery(query)) {
      // Bloom filter says definitely not in any archive
      return { nodes: [], deepResponse: null };
    }

    // Step 3: Rank archive blocks by TF-IDF cosine similarity
    const blockVectors = this.compaction.getBlockVectors();
    if (blockVectors.length === 0) return { nodes: [], deepResponse: null };

    const ranked = this.tfidf.rankBlocks(query, blockVectors);
    if (ranked.length === 0 || ranked[0].score === 0) {
      return { nodes: [], deepResponse: null };
    }

    // Filter to blocks with non-zero scores
    const relevantBlocks = ranked.filter(r => r.score > 0);

    // Step 4: Simple retrieval — decompress top candidates and search within
    const results = [];
    const maxBlocks = Math.min(2, relevantBlocks.length);

    for (let i = 0; i < maxBlocks; i++) {
      const archiveKey = relevantBlocks[i].key;
      const nodes = await this.archive.retrieve(archiveKey);
      if (!nodes || nodes.length === 0) continue;

      const innerResults = this._searchWithinBlock(query, nodes);
      results.push(...innerResults);
  // ... (truncated)
```

#### `_searchWithinBlock(query, nodes)` — method, line 81

* **Purpose:** Calculates and returns the nodes within a provided block that are most relevant to the given query using a BM25-like scoring algorithm.
* **Inputs:**
    * `query` (string): The search query string used for relevance matching.
    * `nodes` (Array<Object>): An array of content nodes to be searched against. Each node is expected to have at least a `content` property and potentially a `role`.
* **Outputs:** Array<Object> - An array containing the original node objects, sorted in descending order based on their calculated relevance score.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Nodes with the role `"compaction"` are explicitly ignored during scoring.
    * The calculation relies heavily on internal configuration parameters (`this.config.bm25`) for BM25 constants (k1, b).
    * If the query is empty or tokenization results in no terms, an empty array is returned immediately.
    * The function calculates document frequency (DF) and inverse document frequency (IDF) across all provided nodes to normalize scores.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  _searchWithinBlock(query, nodes) {
    const queryTerms = tokenizeLocal(query);
    if (queryTerms.length === 0) return [];

    const avgdl = nodes.reduce((sum, n) => sum + (n.content || "").split(/\s+/).length, 0) / nodes.length;
    const scored = [];

    for (const node of nodes) {
      if (node.role === "compaction") continue;
      const terms = tokenizeLocal(node.content);
      const termFreqs = new Map();
      for (const t of terms) {
        termFreqs.set(t, (termFreqs.get(t) || 0) + 1);
      }

      let score = 0;
      for (const qt of queryTerms) {
        const freq = termFreqs.get(qt) || 0;
        if (freq === 0) continue;

        const df = nodes.filter(n => n.content && n.content.toLowerCase().includes(qt)).length;
        const idf = Math.log(1 + (nodes.length - df + 0.5) / (df + 0.5));
        const dl = terms.length;
        const numerator = freq * (this.config.bm25.k1 + 1);
        const denominator = freq + this.config.bm25.k1 * (1 - this.config.bm25.b + this.config.bm25.b * (dl / avgdl));
        score += idf * (numerator / denominator);
      }

      if (score > 0) {
        scored.push({ node, score });
      }
    }

    return scored
      .sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score)
      .map(s => s.node);
  }
```

</details>

#### `tokenizeLocal(text)` — function, line 123

* **Purpose:** Converts a raw string of text into an array of cleaned, lowercase tokens suitable for indexing or searching.
* **Inputs:**
    * `text` (`string`): The input text to be tokenized.
* **Outputs:** `string[]`: An array containing the individual words (tokens) from the processed text.
* **Behavioral Notes:** Returns an empty array if the input `text` is falsy (null, undefined, or empty string). It converts all characters to lowercase and removes punctuation before splitting by whitespace.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function tokenizeLocal(text) {
  if (!text) return [];
  return text
    .toLowerCase()
    .replace(/[^\w\s]/g, " ")
    .split(/\s+/)
    .filter(t => t.length > 0);
}
```

</details>

## `src/core/settings.js`

**412 lines** | 17 functions/classes

This file implements a robust `Settings` class designed as the central runtime configuration manager for the application.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to provide a single, typed source of truth for all configurable parameters—ranging from connection details (API endpoints, formats) and context management (token budgets, window size) to advanced features like memory limits and retrieval mechanisms. It ensures that settings are validated against defined schemas before being applied.

**Functionality:**
1. **Schema Management:** It defines a comprehensive schema (`SCHEMA`) for static settings, allowing UI components to dynamically build configuration panels based on data types (string, number, boolean, select) and constraints (min/max).
2. **Dynamic Detection:** It supports dynamic detection of parameters (e.g., available models and endpoints) from external LLM servers like `llama.cpp` or Ollama, integrating these into the schema for completeness.
3. **Validation & Mutability:** The class provides methods (`set`, `setMany`) that validate incoming values against the defined types and constraints. It handles type coercion (e.g., converting strings to numbers) and enforces boundaries.
4. **State Management:** It manages the live configuration state, supporting hot-reloading of settings without requiring an application restart.
5. **Event System:** It includes an event system (`onChange`) allowing other parts of the application to subscribe to changes in specific settings values.

**Role in Project:**
The `Settings` class acts as the core configuration layer. By centralizing all parameters, it decouples the UI from the underlying logic and ensures that any component needing a setting value (whether for connecting to an API or calculating context size) accesses a validated, consistent, and up-to-date source of truth.

### Exports

- `Settings` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `Settings()` — class, line 52

* **Purpose:** Manages and provides access to the application's configuration settings, including schemas for UI generation and current values grouped by category.
* **Inputs:**
    * `config` (`object`): The live configuration object that will be mutated in place.
    * `transport` (`import('../transport/llm.js').LLMTransport`): An instance of the transport layer used for LLM interactions.
* **Outputs:**
    * `getSchema()`: `{ schema: object, groups: object, samplingSchema: object|null }` - A structure containing the full configuration schema definition, grouping metadata, and dynamically detected sampling parameters.
    * `getAll()`: `object` - An object representing all current configuration values, grouped by their respective categories (groups).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The class holds internal state for various defaults (`_serverDefaults`) and dynamically detected schemas/backends (`_samplingSchema`, `_backends`).
    * The `config` object passed to the constructor is mutated in place.
    * `getAll()` populates values using the provided `SCHEMA` definition, falling back to `null` if a key is undefined in the current configuration.
    * Sampling parameters are dynamically included in both `getSchema()` and `getAll()` if they have been detected.

```javascript
  constructor(config, transport) {

    this._samplingSchema = null; // populated by detectServerParams

      groups: GROUPS,
```

#### `getAll()` — method, line 83

* **Purpose:** Retrieves a comprehensive object containing all configured settings, organized by defined groups and including dynamically sampled values.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** Object representing all structured configuration settings (grouped by schema).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The primary structure is built using the `SCHEMA`, grouping keys under their respective `meta.group`.
    * If a setting key exists in the schema but not in `this.config`, its value will be set to `null`.
    * It includes a special section named `sampling` if `this._samplingSchema` is defined, populating it with values from `this.config` or falling back to the default value specified in `this._samplingSchema`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  getAll() {
    const result = {};
    for (const [key, meta] of Object.entries(SCHEMA)) {
      if (!result[meta.group]) result[meta.group] = {};
      result[meta.group][key] = this.config[key] !== undefined ? this.config[key] : null;
    }
    // Include sampling values from config (dynamically detected keys)
    if (this._samplingSchema) {
      result.sampling = {};
      for (const key of Object.keys(this._samplingSchema)) {
        result.sampling[key] = this.config[key] !== undefined ? this.config[key] : this._samplingSchema[key].default;
      }
    }
    return result;
  }
```

#### `set(key, value)` — method, line 114

* **Purpose:** Sets a configuration value for a given key after validating and coercing the input based on predefined schema rules.
* **Inputs:**
    * `key` (string): The identifier of the setting to be updated.
    * `value` (any): The new value to set for the key.
* **Outputs:** boolean - Returns `true` if the value was successfully set and validated, otherwise returns `false`.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Validation occurs against a schema (`SCHEMA` or `this._samplingSchema`). If no schema exists for the key, setting fails immediately.
    * Type coercion is applied: 'number' values are converted to `Number`, checked against min/max bounds, and clamped if necessary; 'boolean' values are coerced using `Boolean()`; 'select' values must be present in defined options.
    * If the new value differs from the old value (`this.config[key]`), an event is emitted notifying listeners of the change (key, newValue, oldValue).
    * Setting a value that fails validation results in no change to `this.config` and returns `false`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  set(key, value) {
    const meta = SCHEMA[key] || (this._samplingSchema && this._samplingSchema[key]);
    if (!meta) return false;

    // Type coercion and validation
    if (meta.type === 'number') {
      value = Number(value);
      if (isNaN(value)) return false;
      if (meta.min !== undefined && value < meta.min) value = meta.min;
      if (meta.max !== undefined && value > meta.max) value = meta.max;
    } else if (meta.type === 'boolean') {
      value = Boolean(value);
    } else if (meta.type === 'select') {
      if (meta.options && !meta.options.includes(value)) return false;
    }
    // string and textarea: accept as-is

    const oldValue = this.config[key];
    this.config[key] = value;

    // Notify listeners
    if (oldValue !== value) {
      this._emit(key, value, oldValue);
    }
    return true;
  }
```

</details>

```javascript
  setMany(values) {
    const failed = [];
    for (const [key, val] of Object.entries(values)) {
      if (!this.set(key, val)) failed.push(key);
    }
    return failed;
  }
```

#### `detectBackends()` — method, line 167

* **Purpose:** Detects and initializes available local Large Language Model backends by checking connectivity to configured endpoints for llama.cpp and Ollama.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** Promise<Object> - An object containing connection details (`endpoint`, `models`, `type`) for detected backends (e.g., `{ llamacpp: {...}, ollama: {...} }`).
* **Behavioral Notes:** The method is asynchronous and attempts to connect to two specific services: llama.cpp (using a configurable endpoint or default) and Ollama (using the default port 11434). Connection checks use a 2000ms timeout, and failures for either service are silently ignored (`catch {}`), allowing detection of partial availability. The detected backends are stored internally in `this._backends`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async detectBackends() {
    const results = { llamacpp: null, ollama: null };

    // Check llama.cpp on configured endpoint
    try {
      const endpoint = this.config.endpoint || 'http://127.0.0.1:8081';
      const res = await fetch(`${endpoint}/v1/models`, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(2000) });
      if (res.ok) {
        const data = await res.json();
        const models = (data.data || []).map(m => m.id);
        results.llamacpp = { endpoint, models, type: 'openai' };
      }
    } catch { /* not running */ }

    // Check Ollama on default port
    try {
      const ollamaEndpoint = 'http://127.0.0.1:11434';
      const res = await fetch(`${ollamaEndpoint}/api/tags`, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(2000) });
      if (res.ok) {
        const data = await res.json();
        const models = (data.models || []).map(m => ({
          name: m.name,
          size: m.size,
          paramSize: m.details?.parameter_size || null,
          family: m.details?.family || null,
          quantization: m.details?.quantization_level || null
        }));
        results.ollama = { endpoint: ollamaEndpoint, models, type: 'ollama' };
      }
    } catch { /* not running */ }

    this._backends = results;
    return results;
  }
```

</details>

The method's execution path is determined entirely by the value of `this.config.apiFormat`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async detectServerParams() {
    const { endpoint, apiFormat, model } = this.config;

    try {
      if (apiFormat === 'ollama') {
        return await this._detectOllama(endpoint, model);
      } else {
        return await this._detectLlamaCpp(endpoint);
      }
    } catch (e) {
      console.warn('[Settings] Server detection failed:', e.message);
      return null;
    }
  }
```

#### `_detectLlamaCpp(endpoint)` — method, line 259

* **Purpose:** Detects and initializes sampling parameters, server information, and configuration defaults by fetching metadata from a specified LlamaCpp endpoint.
* **Inputs:**
    * `endpoint` (string): The base URL of the LlamaCpp service endpoint used to fetch model properties.
* **Outputs:** None (The method modifies internal class state: `this._samplingSchema`, `this._serverDefaults`, and `this.config`).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * It makes an asynchronous HTTP GET request to `${endpoint}/props` with a 3000ms timeout. If the response is not successful (`!res.ok`), it returns `null`.
    * The method populates several internal state variables:
        * `this._samplingSchema`: A schema containing only numeric, sampling-relevant parameters found in the server's properties.
        * `this._serverDefaults`: An object combining detected server info (model alias, context length, thinking flag) and default values for all sampled parameters.
    * It updates `this.config` by setting every detected numeric parameter value from the server response.
    * The detection process relies on filtering out non-numeric or explicitly skipped keys (`SKIP_KEYS`) to build a restricted sampling schema.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async _detectLlamaCpp(endpoint) {
    const res = await fetch(`${endpoint}/props`, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(3000) });
    if (!res.ok) return null;

    const props = await res.json();
    const dgs = props.default_generation_settings || {};
    const params = dgs.params || dgs;

    // Extract server info
    const serverInfo = {
      model: props.model_alias || null,
      contextLength: dgs.n_ctx || null,
      thinking: !!(props.thinking || (props.chat_template || '').includes('<think>'))
    };

    // Build dynamic schema from params — only include numeric sampling-relevant ones
    const SKIP_KEYS = new Set([
      'stream', 'n_probs', 'n_keep', 'n_discard', 'ignore_eos', 'min_keep',
      'chat_format', 'reasoning_format', 'reasoning_in_content', 'generation_prompt',
      'samplers', 'speculative.types', 'timings_per_token', 'post_sampling_probs',
      'backend_sampling', 'lora'
    ]);

    const samplingSchema = {};
    for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(params)) {
      if (SKIP_KEYS.has(key)) continue;
      if (typeof value !== 'number') continue;

      // Build reasonable ranges based on the param name and value
      const meta = this._inferParamMeta(key, value);
      samplingSchema[key] = meta;

      // Apply current server value to config
      this.config[key] = value;
    }

    this._samplingSchema = samplingSchema;
    this._serverDefaults = { ...serverInfo, params: Object.fromEntries(
      Object.entries(samplingSchema).map(([k]) => [k, this.config[k]])
    )};
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `_detectOllama(endpoint, model)` — method, line 306

* **Purpose:** Detects and extracts configuration parameters for a specified model from an Ollama server endpoint, updating the class's internal state with these settings.
* **Inputs:**
    * `endpoint` (string): The base URL of the running Ollama API instance.
    * `model` (string): The name of the specific model to query parameters for.
* **Outputs:** An object containing detected server information, including the model name and context length (`num_ctx`), and potentially a schema used for sampling configuration.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Performs an asynchronous network request using POST to `/api/show`.
    * Updates the class instance's internal state (`this.config`) with all detected numerical parameters from the Ollama response.
    * The function uses a 3000ms timeout for the API call.
    * It filters out specific keys (`num_ctx`, `num_gpu`, `num_thread`) when building the sampling schema, as these are handled separately or are considered context-specific.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async _detectOllama(endpoint, model) {
    const res = await fetch(`${endpoint}/api/show`, {
      method: 'POST',
      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
      body: JSON.stringify({ name: model }),
      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(3000)
    });
    if (!res.ok) return null;

    const data = await res.json();
    const rawParams = {};

    // Ollama returns modelfile params as "parameter <name> <value>" lines
    if (data.parameters) {
      const lines = data.parameters.split('\n');
      for (const line of lines) {
        const match = line.match(/^(\w+)\s+(.+)$/);
        if (match) {
          const num = Number(match[2]);
          if (!isNaN(num)) rawParams[match[1]] = num;
        }
      }
    }

    // Also check model_info for context length
    const serverInfo = {
      model: model,
      contextLength: rawParams.num_ctx || null
    };

    // Build dynamic schema
    const SKIP_KEYS = new Set(['num_ctx', 'num_gpu', 'num_thread']);
    const samplingSchema = {};

    for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(rawParams)) {
      if (SKIP_KEYS.has(key)) continue;

      const meta = this._inferParamMeta(key, value);
      samplingSchema[key] = meta;
      this.config[key] = value;
  // ... (truncated)
```

#### `_emit(key, newValue, oldValue)` — method, line 406

* **Purpose:** Notifies all registered listeners about a change by calling each function with the key, new value, and old value.
* **Inputs:**
    * `key` (any): The identifier or name of the setting that changed.
    * `newValue` (any): The new value assigned to the key.
    * `oldValue` (any): The previous value associated with the key.
* **Behavioral Notes:** Listener execution is robust; errors thrown by individual listeners will be caught and ignored, allowing all other listeners to execute successfully.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  _emit(key, newValue, oldValue) {
    for (const fn of this._listeners) {
      try { fn(key, newValue, oldValue); } catch (e) { /* don't break on listener errors */ }
    }
  }
```

## `src/core/window.js`

**98 lines** | 4 functions/classes

The `Window` class is responsible for managing and formatting the conversational history (context) provided to a Large Language Model (LLM) at each interaction turn.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to implement a constrained "sliding window" mechanism that ensures the LLM receives only the most relevant, recent context while strictly adhering to defined token limits. It acts as the gatekeeper for conversational memory.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Context Selection (`select`):** It analyzes the recent history (the chain) and selects a subset of nodes. This selection process respects both a maximum node count (`windowSize`) and a total token budget (`maxTokenBudget`), ensuring that only the most information-dense, relevant nodes are passed forward without exceeding API limits.
2.  **Message Formatting (`buildMessages`):** It takes the selected window nodes, along with optional historical context (retrieved nodes) and system prompts, and transforms them into a structured array of messages suitable for an LLM API call. This method correctly interprets different node types (e.g., conversation turns, summaries/compaction notes) and formats them into appropriate roles (`user`, `assistant`, `system`).

**Role in the Project:**
The `Window` class is critical infrastructure that manages state persistence across conversational turns. By clearing and rebuilding the LLM context every turn using this class, it prevents context overflow, maintains focus on recent interactions, and ensures the model has access to both immediate conversation history and relevant historical retrieval data.

### Exports

- `Window` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`, `tests/conversation.test.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `Window()` — class, line 5

* **Purpose:** Manages the context window for an LLM interaction by selecting relevant historical nodes based on token budgets and constructing the final message payload.
* **Inputs:**
    * **constructor(config):** `config` (object) - Configuration object containing `windowSize`, `maxTokenBudget`, and `reservedTokens`.
    * **select(chain):** `chain` (`Chain`) - The current processing chain object, used to access the tail nodes.
    * **buildMessages(windowNodes, retrievedNodes, systemPrompt):**
        * `windowNodes` (object[]) - Nodes selected from the sliding window.
        * `retrievedNodes` (object[] | null) - Optional historical nodes injected via retrieval.
        * `systemPrompt` (string | null) - The initial system message for the LLM.
* **Outputs:**
    * **select(chain):** `object[]` - An array of node objects that fit within the token budget and are contained within the specified window size.
    * **buildMessages(...):** `object[]` - A structured array representing the messages payload required by the LLM API (e.g., [{"role": "system", "content": "..."}, {"role": "user", "content": "..."}]).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The `select` method prioritizes keeping the most recent nodes while ensuring the total token count does not exceed `maxTokenBudget - reservedTokens`.
    * If no candidates are available in the chain, `select` returns an empty array.
    * The `buildMessages` method constructs the message history by placing any `retrievedNodes` before the `windowNodes`, followed by the system prompt (if applicable).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class Window {
  constructor(config) {
    this.config = config;
  }

  /**
   * Get the nodes that should be included in the next LLM prompt.
   * Respects both windowSize and maxTokenBudget.
   * @param {import('./chain.js').Chain} chain
   * @returns {object[]} nodes for the prompt
   */
  select(chain) {
    const { windowSize, maxTokenBudget, reservedTokens } = this.config;
    const availableTokens = maxTokenBudget - reservedTokens;
    const candidates = chain.tail(windowSize);

    if (candidates.length === 0) return [];

    // Walk backwards from most recent, accumulating tokens
    let tokenSum = 0;
    let startIdx = candidates.length;

    for (let i = candidates.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const nodeTokens = candidates[i].tokenCount || 0;
      if (tokenSum + nodeTokens > availableTokens) break;
      tokenSum += nodeTokens;
      startIdx = i;
    }

    return candidates.slice(startIdx);
  }

  /**
   * Build the messages array for the LLM from window nodes + optional retrieved nodes.
   * @param {object[]} windowNodes - nodes from the sliding window
   * @param {object[]} retrievedNodes - historical nodes from retrieval (injected before window)
   * @param {string} systemPrompt - system message
   * @returns {object[]} messages array for the API
   */
  buildMessages(windowNodes, retrievedNodes = [], systemPrompt = "") {
  // ... (truncated)
```

#### `select(chain)` — method, line 16

* **Purpose:** Selects the most recent sequence of nodes from a chain whose cumulative token count does not exceed the available budget, limited by a specified window size.
* **Inputs:**
    * `chain`: An object representing a data stream or linked list (must have a `tail(windowSize)` method).
* **Outputs:** Array of Node objects, representing the selected candidates from the chain's tail.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The selection process is greedy and works backward from the most recent node (`candidates.length - 1`). It stops immediately when adding the next candidate would exceed `availableTokens` (calculated as `maxTokenBudget - reservedTokens`). If no nodes are available within the window, an empty array is returned.

```javascript
    const { windowSize, maxTokenBudget, reservedTokens } = this.config;
    const availableTokens = maxTokenBudget - reservedTokens;
    const candidates = chain.tail(windowSize);

    if (candidates.length === 0) return [];

    // Walk backwards from most recent, accumulating tokens
    let tokenSum = 0;
    let startIdx = candidates.length;

    for (let i = candidates.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const nodeTokens = candidates[i].tokenCount || 0;
      if (tokenSum + nodeTokens > availableTokens) break;
      tokenSum += nodeTokens;
      startIdx = i;
    }

    return candidates.slice(startIdx);
  }
```

#### `buildMessages(windowNodes, retrievedNodes = [], systemPrompt = "")` — method, line 44

* **Purpose:** Constructs a structured list of messages suitable for an LLM API call by combining initial system prompts, retrieved historical context, and the conversation history stored in window nodes.
* **Inputs:**
    * `windowNodes` (Array<Object>): The primary array containing the recent conversation turns and compacted summaries that form the current conversational context.
    * `retrievedNodes` (Array<Object>, optional): An array of historical nodes retrieved from memory that are relevant to the current query, prepended as system context.

* **Behavioral Notes:** The function processes nodes based on their role: "compaction" nodes inject archived summaries; "turn" nodes are split into separate user query and assistant response messages (which also incorporate inline pocket notes); and any other node type is processed using its raw content. If `retrievedNodes` are present, they are injected as a high-priority system message before the conversation history begins.
