## `src/extensions/docgen.js`

**680 lines** | 22 functions/classes

This source file implements a comprehensive, multi-stage documentation generator designed to create exhaustive technical documentation for an entire codebase.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to automate the process of documenting project structure, dependencies, and functionality by combining deep structural code analysis with advanced Large Language Model (LLM) capabilities.

**What It Does:**
The system executes a two-phase pipeline:

1.  **Structural Analysis (Deterministic):** It first traverses the entire file system, building a detailed tree view of the project structure. For every source file, it programmatically extracts critical metadata, including imports, exports, function signatures, and dependency relationships (both internal module dependencies and global/external references).
2.  **Documentation Generation (LLM-Driven):** It then iteratively processes each file:
    *   It writes a high-level summary of the file's purpose, what it does, and its role in the project using an LLM call.
    *   It documents explicit exports and detected dependencies.
    *   Finally, it compiles all these sections into a single `DOCUMENTATION.md` file, appending content incrementally as processing occurs.

**Role in the Project:**
This module acts as the core documentation engine for the project. It abstracts away the complexity of manual documentation by performing deep static analysis (identifying structure and relationships) and then leveraging an external LLM service to generate natural language explanations for summaries and functions, resulting in a single, comprehensive, and highly structured markdown document.

### Exports

- `generateDocumentation` (function)

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/transport/llm.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `signatures(deterministic)` — function, line 6

* **Purpose:** Orchestrates a comprehensive documentation generation process by performing deterministic structural analysis of source code, generating detailed explanations and summaries using an LLM, and incrementally writing the results into a markdown file.
* **Inputs:**
    * `sourceDirectory`: The path to the project or module directory containing the source files to be analyzed.
    * `llmTransport`: An initialized instance of the LLM communication utility used for generating explanations (e.g., an object implementing `LLMTransport`).
* **Outputs:** A string representing the final content written to `DOCUMENTATION.md`, which contains the project overview, file summaries, and function explanations.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The process is divided into two phases: deterministic structural analysis (extracting imports, dependencies, signatures) and generative explanation writing (using LLM calls).
    * All LLM interactions are stateless; each call uses a clean context without relying on previous history.
    * Structural analysis steps include building a dependency map and detecting external resources (CDNs/bundles).
    * The output is written incrementally to `DOCUMENTATION.md`, with the final project overview generated last from all collected summaries.

#### `generateDocumentation(rootDir, outputPath, opts = {})` — function, line 56

* **Purpose:** Analyzes a source code directory structure by extracting file contents, imports, exports, and function signatures to generate comprehensive technical documentation using an LLM.
* **Inputs:**
    * `rootDir` (string): The absolute or relative path to the root directory of the codebase that needs documentation.
    * `outputPath` (string): The destination path where the final generated documentation will be written.
    * `opts` (object): Optional configuration object for controlling the generation process.
        * `llmConfig` (object, optional): Configuration settings for the Large Language Model used for generation. Defaults to a local Ollama setup if not provided.
        * `onProgress` (function, optional): A callback function that receives a string message and is called during different phases of documentation generation.
* **Outputs:** Promise<void> - Resolves when the entire documentation generation process, including structural analysis and LLM calls, has completed successfully.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function operates in multiple asynchronous phases (Structural Analysis, Content Extraction, Documentation Generation).
    * It automatically detects and skips common "Tier 2" tooling folders (`.kiro`, `.vscode`, etc.), acknowledging their presence but excluding them from documentation analysis.
    * Files smaller than 20 characters are skipped during structural analysis to prevent unnecessary processing.
    * The function relies heavily on external dependencies like `LLMTransport` for interacting with the configured LLM endpoint.

export async function generateDocumentation(rootDir, outputPath, opts = {}) {
  const llmConfig = opts.llmConfig || {
    endpoint: 'http://127.0.0.1:11434',
    apiFormat: 'ollama',
    model: 'gemma4-e4b:gpu',
    thinking: false
  };
  const transport = new LLMTransport(llmConfig);
  const onProgress = opts.onProgress || (() => {});
  const folderName = rootDir.split(/[/\\]/).pop();

  // === PHASE 1: Structural Analysis (deterministic) ===
  onProgress('Phase 1: Walking folder structure...');
  const fileEntries = await listFiles(rootDir);
  onProgress(`Found ${fileEntries.length} source files`);

  // Detect Tier 2 tooling folders (acknowledge but don't document)
  const toolingNotes = [];
  const TIER2_FOLDERS = { '.kiro': 'Kiro AI coding agent', '.opencode': 'OpenCode AI agent', '.vscode': 'VS Code workspace config', '.idea': 'JetBrains IDE config', '.cursor': 'Cursor AI editor', '.devcontainer': 'Dev container config' };
  let rootEntries;
  try { rootEntries = await readdir(rootDir); } catch { rootEntries = []; }
  for (const name of rootEntries) {
    if (TIER2_FOLDERS[name]) toolingNotes.push(`- **${name}/**: ${TIER2_FOLDERS[name]}`);
  }

  // Build tree view
  const treeView = buildTreeView(fileEntries);

  // Analyze each file
  onProgress('Phase 1: Extracting structure...');
  const analyzed = [];
  for (const entry of fileEntries) {
    try {
      const code = await readFile(entry.absPath, 'utf8');
      if (code.length < 20) continue;

      const imports = extractImports(code);
      const exports = extractExports(code);
      const signatures = extractSignatures(code);

  // ... (truncated)

#### `callLLM(transport, systemPrompt, userContent)` — function, line 255

* **Purpose:** Calls an external Large Language Model (LLM) API using a provided transport client, sending system instructions and user content to generate a text response.
* **Inputs:**

    * `systemPrompt` (string): The initial set of instructions or context provided to guide the model's behavior.
    * `userContent` (string): The specific content or query submitted by the user to the model.
* **Outputs:** Promise<string> - A promise that resolves to the trimmed text response generated by the LLM, or an empty string if an error occurs.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
async function callLLM(transport, systemPrompt, userContent) {
  try {
    const messages = [
      { role: 'system', content: systemPrompt },
      { role: 'user', content: userContent }
    ];
    const { text } = await transport.complete(messages);
    return (text || '').trim();
  } catch (err) {
    console.log('[DocGen:LLM] Error:', err.message);
    return '';
  }
}
```

</details>

#### `detectGlobalDependencies(analyzedFiles)` — function, line 278

* **Purpose:** Detects and maps global dependencies between files based on IIFE-defined variables and subsequent property access across the codebase.
* **Inputs:**
    * `analyzedFiles`: Array of objects, where each object represents a file and must contain at least `relPath` (string) and `code` (string).
* **Outputs:** Map<string, {uses: string[], usedBy: string[]}>, A map where keys are relative file paths (`file.relPath`), and the values track which global variables that file uses (`uses`) and which other files depend on this file's globals (`usedBy`).
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function specifically targets variables defined using Immediately Invoked Function Expressions (IIFE) patterns (`var X = (function(){...})()`) as potential global entry points. Dependencies are tracked only when a file references a property of an identified global (e.g., `GlobalName.someProperty`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function detectGlobalDependencies(analyzedFiles) {
  const map = new Map();
  for (const file of analyzedFiles) {
    map.set(file.relPath, { uses: [], usedBy: [] });
  }

  // Phase 1: Find all IIFE-defined globals (var X = (function(){...})())
  const globalDefs = new Map(); // globalName → defining file
  for (const file of analyzedFiles) {
    const iifeRe = /(?:var|const|let)\s+(\w+)\s*=\s*\(function\s*\(/g;
    let m;
    while ((m = iifeRe.exec(file.code)) !== null) {
      globalDefs.set(m[1], file.relPath);
    }
  }

  // Phase 2: For each file, find references to other files' globals
  for (const file of analyzedFiles) {
    for (const [globalName, defFile] of globalDefs) {
      if (defFile === file.relPath) continue; // Skip self-references

      // Check if this file uses GlobalName.something
      const useRe = new RegExp('\\b' + globalName + '\\.(\\w+)', 'g');
      if (useRe.test(file.code)) {
        const entry = map.get(file.relPath);
        if (entry && !entry.uses.includes(globalName)) {
          entry.uses.push(globalName);
        }
        const defEntry = map.get(defFile);
        if (defEntry && !defEntry.usedBy.includes(file.relPath)) {
          defEntry.usedBy.push(file.relPath);
        }
      }
    }
  }

  return map;
}
```

</details>

#### `detectExternalDeps(rootDir)` — function, line 327

* **Purpose:** Scans a specified root directory for HTML and JavaScript files to detect external dependencies, including CDNs, bundled assets, and imported packages.
* **Inputs:**
    * `rootDir` (`string`): The absolute or relative path to the root directory where dependency scanning should begin.
* **Outputs:**
    * `Promise<Set<string>>`: A set containing unique strings that identify detected external dependencies (e.g., "CDN: url", "Bundle: path").
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function uses regular expressions to parse file contents, making it sensitive to the specific patterns used for imports and links.
    * It handles errors during file reading by skipping problematic files (`try...catch`).
    * Dependency detection is categorized (e.g., "CDN:", "Bundle:", "CDN CSS:") based on where and how the dependency was found.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
async function detectExternalDeps(rootDir) {
  const deps = new Set();

  // Scan for HTML files that might have script/link tags
  const htmlFiles = [];
  await walkDir(rootDir, '', htmlFiles, new Set(['.html', '.htm']));

  for (const file of htmlFiles) {
    try {
      const html = await readFile(file.absPath, 'utf8');

      // CDN script tags
      const scriptRe = /<script[^>]+src=["']([^"']+)["']/g;
      let m;
      while ((m = scriptRe.exec(html)) !== null) {
        const src = m[1];
        if (src.startsWith('http') || src.startsWith('//')) {
          deps.add(`CDN: ${src}`);
        } else if (src.includes('dist/') || src.includes('vendor/') || src.includes('.min.')) {
          deps.add(`Bundle: ${src}`);
        }
      }

      // CDN link tags (CSS)
      const linkRe = /<link[^>]+href=["']([^"']+)["'][^>]*rel=["']stylesheet["']/g;
      while ((m = linkRe.exec(html)) !== null) {
        if (m[1].startsWith('http') || m[1].startsWith('//')) {
          deps.add(`CDN CSS: ${m[1]}`);
        }
      }
    } catch { /* skip */ }
  }

  // Scan JS files for external package imports (not relative)
  const jsFiles = [];
  await walkDir(rootDir, '', jsFiles);
  for (const file of jsFiles) {
    try {
      const code = await readFile(file.absPath, 'utf8');
      const importRe = /(?:import|require)\s*\(?['"]([^'"./][^'"]*)['"]\)?/g;
  // ... (truncated)

#### `buildTocTree(analyzed)` — function, line 389

* **Purpose:** Builds and returns a nested, formatted Table of Contents (TOC) string from an array of analyzed file objects, structured by directory hierarchy.
* **Inputs:**
    * `analyzed`: Array of objects representing files, where each object must contain at least `relPath` (string path relative to the root) and `lineCount` (number of lines).
* **Outputs:** String containing the formatted Markdown TOC structure.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function processes file paths by splitting them into directory components to build a nested tree structure (`{ dirs: {}, files: [] }`). Files are listed before their parent directories in the output, and subdirectories are rendered recursively. Directory names and file links use lowercase slugs derived from `relPath` for anchor linking. Directories are sorted alphabetically when rendering. The final output string includes an extra newline character.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function buildTocTree(analyzed) {
  // Build nested object: { dirs: { name: subtree }, files: [file, ...] }
  const root = { dirs: {}, files: [] };
  for (const file of analyzed) {
    const parts = file.relPath.split('/');
    let current = root;
    for (let i = 0; i < parts.length - 1; i++) {
      if (!current.dirs[parts[i]]) current.dirs[parts[i]] = { dirs: {}, files: [] };
      current = current.dirs[parts[i]];
    }
    current.files.push(file);
  }

  function renderNode(node, depth = 0) {
    let out = '';
    const indent = '  '.repeat(depth);

    // Files first
    for (const file of node.files) {
      const slug = file.relPath.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]/g, '');
      const fname = file.relPath.split('/').pop();
      out += `${indent}- [\`${fname}\`](#${slug}) — ${file.lineCount} lines\n`;
    }

    // Then subdirectories
    const dirs = Object.keys(node.dirs).sort();
    for (const dir of dirs) {
      out += `${indent}- **${dir}/**\n`;
      out += renderNode(node.dirs[dir], depth + 1);
    }

    return out;
  }

  return renderNode(root, 0) + '\n';
}
```

</details>

#### `renderTree(node, prefix = '', isLast = true)` — function, line 446

* **Purpose:** Recursively traverses a nested object structure representing a file system and returns an ASCII art string visualization of the directory tree.
* **Inputs:**
    * `node` (Object): The current node in the file system structure to render. Keys are names, and values are either `null` (indicating a file) or another object (indicating a directory).
    * `prefix` (string): The accumulated indentation string for the current level of recursion. Defaults to an empty string.
    * `isLast` (boolean): A flag indicating if the current node is the last child of its parent. Defaults to `true`.
* **Outputs:** string: The formatted, indented string representing the rendered portion of the directory tree.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function sorts entries alphabetically, ensuring that directories are always listed before files regardless of their key order in the input object.
    * It uses recursion to process nested directories.
    * The `prefix` and connector characters (`├──`, `└──`, `│`) are dynamically adjusted based on whether the current entry is the last child of its parent, ensuring correct visual representation (e.g., using a blank space for the prefix if it's the last item).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function renderTree(node, prefix = '', isLast = true) {
    const entries = Object.keys(node).sort((a, b) => {
      // Directories first, then files
      const aIsDir = node[a] !== null;
      const bIsDir = node[b] !== null;
      if (aIsDir && !bIsDir) return -1;
      if (!aIsDir && bIsDir) return 1;
      return a.localeCompare(b);
    });

    let result = '';
    for (let i = 0; i < entries.length; i++) {
      const name = entries[i];
      const isLastEntry = i === entries.length - 1;
      const connector = isLastEntry ? '└── ' : '├── ';
      const childPrefix = isLastEntry ? '    ' : '│   ';

      if (node[name] === null) {
        // File
        result += prefix + connector + name + '\n';
      } else {
        // Directory
        result += prefix + connector + name + '/\n';
        result += renderTree(node[name], prefix + childPrefix, isLastEntry);
      }
    }
    return result;
  }

#### `walkDir(dir, relPath, files, validExtsOverride = null)` — function, line 489

* **Purpose:** Recursively traverses a directory, collecting paths and metadata for files that match specified extensions while skipping hidden or ignored directories/files.
* **Inputs:**
    * `dir`: string - The absolute path of the directory to start traversing from.
    * `relPath`: (string | null) - The relative path prefix used when constructing the relative path names for found files.
    * `files`: Array<Object> - An array passed by reference that will accumulate objects containing file metadata (`relPath`, `absPath`, `ext`).
    * `validExtsOverride`: (Set<string> | null) - Optional set of allowed file extensions; if provided, overrides the default list.
* **Outputs:** None (It modifies the `files` array passed as an argument).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function uses asynchronous I/O (`readdir`) and handles potential reading errors by silently returning.
    * It skips entries that start with a dot (`.`) or are present in the global `IGNORES` set.
    * File extensions are checked against the provided (or default) valid extensions, ensuring case-insensitive matching.
    * The function mutates the `files` array passed to it.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
async function walkDir(dir, relPath, files, validExtsOverride = null) {
  let entries;
  try { entries = await readdir(dir, { withFileTypes: true }); }
  catch { return; }

  const exts = validExtsOverride || VALID_EXTS;

  for (const entry of entries) {
    if (entry.name.startsWith('.') || IGNORES.has(entry.name)) continue;
    const fullPath = join(dir, entry.name);
    const rel = relPath ? relPath + '/' + entry.name : entry.name;

    if (entry.isDirectory()) {
      await walkDir(fullPath, rel, files, validExtsOverride);
    } else {
      const ext = extname(entry.name).toLowerCase();
      if (!exts.has(ext)) continue;
      files.push({ relPath: rel, absPath: fullPath, ext });
    }
  }
}
```

</details>

#### `extractExports(code)` — function, line 539

* **Purpose:** Parses a string containing JavaScript code and extracts structured metadata about all explicitly exported identifiers using regular expressions.
* **Inputs:**
    * `code` (string): The JavaScript source code snippet to analyze for exports.
* **Outputs:** Array of objects (`Array<{ name: string, type: string }>`) representing the list of exported entities found in the code.
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function relies on multiple regular expressions to identify specific export patterns (functions, classes, constants, default exports). It may fail or misinterpret complex syntax, comments that mimic keywords, or non-standard module systems not covered by its current regex definitions.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function extractExports(code) {
  const exports = [];
  let m;

  const fnRe = /export\s+(?:async\s+)?function\s+(\w+)/g;
  while ((m = fnRe.exec(code)) !== null) exports.push({ name: m[1], type: 'function' });

  const classRe = /export\s+class\s+(\w+)/g;
  while ((m = classRe.exec(code)) !== null) exports.push({ name: m[1], type: 'class' });

  const constRe = /export\s+(?:const|let|var)\s+(\w+)/g;
  while ((m = constRe.exec(code)) !== null) exports.push({ name: m[1], type: 'const' });

  const defaultRe = /export\s+default\s+(?:function\s+)?(\w+)?/g;
  while ((m = defaultRe.exec(code)) !== null) exports.push({ name: m[1] || 'default', type: 'default' });

  const namedRe = /export\s+\{([^}]+)\}/g;
```

#### `extractSignatures(code)` — function, line 570

* **Purpose:** Extracts structured signatures (functions, classes, and methods) from a given JavaScript source code string.
* **Inputs:**
    * `code` (string): The source code content to analyze for declarations.
* **Outputs:** Array of objects, where each object represents a detected signature and contains:
    * `name` (string): The name of the declared entity.
    * `type` (string): The type of declaration ('function', 'class', or 'method').
    * `params` (string): The parameters list for the signature.
    * `line` (number): The 1-based line number where the signature starts.
    * `body` (any): The extracted body content associated with the signature.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * It processes the code line by line, using multiple regular expressions to capture different declaration styles (e.g., standard function declarations, const/let assignments, class definitions, and indented methods).
    * Detection of signatures depends on external helper functions `shouldSkip(name)` and `extractBody(lines, i)`.
    * The method detection regex specifically looks for lines starting with two or more spaces (`^\s{2,}`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function extractSignatures(code) {
  const sigs = [];
  const lines = code.split('\n');

  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
    const line = lines[i];
    let m;

    // function name(params) or async function name(params)
    m = line.match(/(?:export\s+)?(?:async\s+)?function\s+(\w+)\s*\(([^)]*)\)/);
    if (m && !shouldSkip(m[1])) {
      sigs.push({ name: m[1], type: 'function', params: m[2].trim(), line: i + 1, body: extractBody(lines, i) });
      continue;
    }

    // const name = (params) => or const name = function(params)
    m = line.match(/(?:export\s+)?(?:const|let|var)\s+(\w+)\s*=\s*(?:async\s+)?(?:\(([^)]*)\)\s*=>|function\s*\(([^)]*)\))/);
    if (m && !shouldSkip(m[1])) {
      sigs.push({ name: m[1], type: 'function', params: (m[2] || m[3] || '').trim(), line: i + 1, body: extractBody(lines, i) });
      continue;
    }

    // class Name
    m = line.match(/(?:export\s+)?class\s+(\w+)(?:\s+extends\s+(\w+))?/);
    if (m) {
      sigs.push({ name: m[1], type: 'class', params: m[2] ? 'extends ' + m[2] : '', line: i + 1, body: extractBody(lines, i) });
      continue;
    }

    // Method: name(params) { (indented, inside class/object)
    m = line.match(/^\s{2,}(?:async\s+)?(\w+)\s*\(([^)]*)\)\s*\{/);
    if (m && !shouldSkip(m[1])) {
      sigs.push({ name: m[1], type: 'method', params: m[2].trim(), line: i + 1, body: extractBody(lines, i) });
    }
  }

  return sigs;
}

#### `extractBody(lines, startLine)` — function, line 613

* **Purpose:** Extracts a code block or function body from an array of source code lines, respecting nested curly braces and applying truncation limits.
* **Inputs:**
    * `lines` (Array<string>): The full array of source code lines to search through.
    * `startLine` (number): The index of the line where the extraction should begin.
* **Outputs:** string: A single string containing the extracted body, with lines joined by newline characters.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Extraction is limited to a maximum of 60 lines starting from `startLine`.
    * It tracks nesting depth using curly braces (`{` increments depth, `}` decrements depth). The extraction continues until the initial block's depth returns to zero (if it started with an opening brace).
    * If the extracted body reaches 40 lines, the function stops and appends a truncation comment (`// ... (truncated)`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function extractBody(lines, startLine) {
  let depth = 0;
  let started = false;
  const body = [];

  for (let i = startLine; i < Math.min(lines.length, startLine + 60); i++) {
    for (const ch of lines[i]) {
      if (ch === '{') { depth++; started = true; }
      if (ch === '}') depth--;
    }
    body.push(lines[i]);
    if (started && depth === 0) break;
    if (body.length >= 40) { body.push('  // ... (truncated)'); break; }
  }

  return body.join('\n');
}
```

</details>

#### `buildDependencyMap(analyzedFiles)` — function, line 635

* **Purpose:** Builds and returns a comprehensive dependency map detailing which files depend on others and which files are imported by others within the analyzed set of source files.
* **Inputs:**
    * `analyzedFiles`: Array of objects, where each object represents an analyzed file and must contain at least `relPath` (string) and `imports` (array of import objects).
* **Outputs:** Map<string, { importedBy: string[], dependsOn: string[] }> A map where keys are the relative paths (`file.relPath`) of files, and values are objects tracking an array of file paths that depend on this file (`importedBy`) and an array of file paths that this file depends on (`dependsOn`).
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function relies on external helper functions `resolveRelative` and `findFile` to correctly resolve relative import paths into existing analyzed files. It only processes imports that are explicitly relative (i.e., start with a dot, `.`). Dependencies are added uniquely; duplicate entries for `dependsOn` or `importedBy` are prevented.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function buildDependencyMap(analyzedFiles) {
  const map = new Map();
  for (const file of analyzedFiles) {
    map.set(file.relPath, { importedBy: [], dependsOn: [] });
  }

  for (const file of analyzedFiles) {
    for (const imp of file.imports) {
      if (!imp.source.startsWith('.')) continue;
      const dir = file.relPath.includes('/') ? file.relPath.replace(/\/[^/]+$/, '') : '';
      const resolved = resolveRelative(dir, imp.source);
      const match = findFile(analyzedFiles, resolved);
      if (match) {
        const entry = map.get(file.relPath);
        if (entry && !entry.dependsOn.includes(match)) entry.dependsOn.push(match);
        const target = map.get(match);
        if (target && !target.importedBy.includes(file.relPath)) target.importedBy.push(file.relPath);
      }
    }
  }

  return map;
}
```

</details>

#### `resolveRelative(fromDir, importPath)` — function, line 659

* **Purpose:** Resolves a relative file path by processing directory navigation components (`.` and `..`) against a specified base directory.
* **Inputs:**
    * `fromDir` (string | null): The starting directory used as the base for resolving the relative path.
    * `importPath` (string): The relative path string that needs to be resolved.
* **Outputs:** string: The fully resolved absolute-like path string, using `/` as a separator.
* **Behavioral Notes:** If `fromDir` is null or undefined, it initializes the base parts array as empty. It correctly handles traversing up directories (`..`) by popping elements from the base path components.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function resolveRelative(fromDir, importPath) {
  const parts = fromDir ? fromDir.split('/') : [];
  for (const p of importPath.split('/')) {
    if (p === '.') continue;
    if (p === '..') parts.pop();
    else parts.push(p);
  }
  return parts.join('/');
}
```

</details>

#### `findFile(files, resolved)` — function, line 669

* **Purpose:** Attempts to locate the full relative path of a module or file given a base resolved path by checking for common extensions and index files.
* **Inputs:**
    * `files`: Array of objects, where each object must contain a `relPath` property representing the file's relative path.
    * `resolved`: String, the base path or name of the file being searched for.
* **Outputs:** String | null, The full relative path to the found file (e.g., "path/to/file.js"), or `null` if no matching file is found.
* **Behavioral Notes:** It checks for three types of matches in order: 1) Exact match (`resolved`). 2) Match with common extensions appended (`resolved + ext`). 3) Match as an index file (`resolved + '/index' + ext`). The function returns immediately upon finding the first successful match.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function findFile(files, resolved) {
  if (files.find(f => f.relPath === resolved)) return resolved;
  const exts = ['.js', '.ts', '.jsx', '.tsx', '.mjs'];
  for (const ext of exts) {
    if (files.find(f => f.relPath === resolved + ext)) return resolved + ext;
  }
  for (const ext of exts) {
    if (files.find(f => f.relPath === resolved + '/index' + ext)) return resolved + '/index' + ext;
  }
  return null;
}
```

</details>

## `src/extensions/ephemeral-mind.js`

**211 lines** | 3 functions/classes

This module implements the "Ephemeral Mind," a sophisticated internal reasoning scratchpad designed to force deep, adversarial, and structured self-criticism within a larger AI system.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to prevent superficial or incomplete answers by forcing the model to explicitly identify unresolved tensions, structural flaws, and edge cases in its own logic. It simulates a rigorous, multi-step thinking process that goes beyond simple generation.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Guides Reasoning (System Prompt/Few-Shot):** It provides highly structured system prompts and few-shot examples that mandate the model's output be a strict JSON payload containing four key fields: `thought_block` (raw analysis), `resolved_nodes` (summary of findings), `unresolved_tension` (the specific gap or contradiction found), and `requires_further_recursion` (a boolean gate for continuing the thought process).
2.  **Parses Output (`parseEphemeralPayload`):** It contains robust logic to parse the model's raw, potentially malformed output, handling JSON extraction, markdown stripping, and providing fallbacks if parsing fails, ensuring the core structured data can always be retrieved.
3.  **Validates Tension (`validateTension`):** It includes a validation function that checks if an identified "unresolved tension" is meaningful—requiring minimum length and overlap with key concepts from the original query—to prevent the model from generating arbitrary gaps just to loop.
4.  **Exposes Functionality (`createEphemeralMindTool`):** It wraps this entire process into a callable tool, making deep reasoning available for complex tasks like comparisons, technical analysis, or multi-step design decisions.

**Role in the Project:**
The Ephemeral Mind acts as a critical **self-correction and refinement loop**. Instead of simply generating an answer, the system uses this module to iteratively refine its understanding. By forcing the model to articulate what it *doesn't* know (`unresolved_tension`), the project ensures that complex problems are thoroughly analyzed until all logical gaps are closed or explicitly acknowledged, leading to higher quality and more robust final outputs.

### Exports

- `parseEphemeralPayload` (function)
- `validateTension` (function)
- `createEphemeralMindTool` (function)
- `EPHEMERAL_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (const)

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/tools/base.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `parseEphemeralPayload(rawOutput)` — function, line 67

* **Purpose:** Safely extracts, cleans, and parses structured JSON data from a raw string output that may contain surrounding text or markdown fences.
* **Inputs:**
    * `rawOutput` (string): The raw textual output containing the payload to be parsed.
* **Outputs:** Object: A standardized object containing extracted fields (`thought_block`, `resolved_nodes`, etc.), using default values if parsing fails.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If `rawOutput` is null, undefined, or empty/whitespace, it returns a predefined fallback structure immediately.
    * It automatically strips common markdown JSON fences (e.g., ` ```json `, ` ``` `) from the input string.
    * It uses brace-depth matching to accurately locate and extract the core JSON object within the raw text, ignoring surrounding non-JSON content.
    * If the extracted content is not valid JSON or if no JSON structure can be found, it returns a fallback object initialized with default values.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export function parseEphemeralPayload(rawOutput) {
  const fallback = {
    thought_block: rawOutput || '',
    resolved_nodes: [],
    unresolved_tension: '',
    requires_further_recursion: false
  };

  if (!rawOutput || rawOutput.trim().length === 0) return fallback;

  // Strip markdown fences if present
  let cleaned = rawOutput
    .replace(/^```json\s*/i, '')
    .replace(/^```\s*/i, '')
    .replace(/\s*```$/i, '')
    .trim();

  // Try to find JSON by brace-depth matching
  const openIdx = cleaned.indexOf('{');
  if (openIdx === -1) return fallback;

  let depth = 0;
  let closeIdx = -1;
  for (let i = openIdx; i < cleaned.length; i++) {
    if (cleaned[i] === '{') depth++;
    else if (cleaned[i] === '}') {
      depth--;
      if (depth === 0) { closeIdx = i + 1; break; }
    }
  }

  if (closeIdx <= openIdx) return fallback;

  const jsonStr = cleaned.slice(openIdx, closeIdx);

  try {
    const parsed = JSON.parse(jsonStr);
    return {
      thought_block: parsed.thought_block || parsed.analysis || parsed.thought || rawOutput,
      resolved_nodes: Array.isArray(parsed.resolved_nodes) ? parsed.resolved_nodes : [],
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `validateTension(tension, originalQuery)` — function, line 144

* **Purpose:** Validates if a given tension string is sufficiently long and shares at least one common word (4 characters or longer) with an original query string.
* **Inputs:**
    * `tension` (string): The text segment being validated for overlap and length.
    * `originalQuery` (string): The source text used to determine required vocabulary overlap.
* **Outputs:** boolean: Returns `true` if the tension meets both the minimum length requirement and the word overlap requirement; otherwise, it returns `false`.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function requires the `tension` string itself to be at least 15 characters long to pass validation.
    * Word extraction for both inputs filters out any words shorter than four characters (`w.length >= 4`).
    * Validation only passes if there is a minimum of one overlapping word between the processed `tension` and the processed `originalQuery`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export function validateTension(tension, originalQuery) {
  if (!tension || tension.length < 15) return false;

  // Extract meaningful words (4+ chars) from the original query
  const queryWords = new Set(
    originalQuery.toLowerCase().split(/\W+/).filter(w => w.length >= 4)
  );

  // Extract words from the tension
  const tensionWords = tension.toLowerCase().split(/\W+/).filter(w => w.length >= 4);

  // Require at least one domain noun overlap
  const overlap = tensionWords.filter(w => queryWords.has(w));
  return overlap.length >= 1;
}
```

</details>

#### `createEphemeralMindTool({ transport, memoryConfig })` — function, line 167

* **Purpose:** Creates and returns a specialized tool designed to facilitate deep, step-by-step reasoning or complex analysis by an LLM before generating a final response.
* **Inputs:**
    * `transport` (object): An object containing methods necessary for making API calls (e.g., `complete`).
    * `memoryConfig` (object): Configuration object used to manage the tool's interaction with the system memory state, specifically tracking whether thinking is enabled.
* **Outputs:** A configured Tool instance that can be utilized by the calling system.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The `execute` function temporarily modifies the global `memoryConfig.thinking` state (setting it to `false`) before making the API call and ensures this state is restored in a `finally` block, regardless of success or failure.
    * If the required `query` parameter is missing during execution, an error is thrown.
    * The tool uses predefined system prompts (`EPHEMERAL_SYSTEM_PROMPT`) to guide the LLM's internal reasoning process.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export function createEphemeralMindTool({ transport, memoryConfig }) {
  return new Tool({
    name: "ephemeral_mind",
    discovery: "llm",
    description: "Use this tool when you need to reason through a complex problem, work out logic step-by-step, analyze tradeoffs, deliberate between options, or think deeply before responding. Call it to get a focused analysis back that you can then use in your final answer to the user. You SHOULD use this for any question involving: comparisons, multi-step reasoning, technical analysis, design decisions, debugging logic, or synthesizing multiple pieces of information.",
    parameters: {
      type: "object",
      properties: {
        query: {
          type: "string",
          description: "What to think about, reason through, or work out internally"
        }
      },
      required: ["query"]
    },
    execute: async function (params, context) {
      const query = params.query;
      if (!query) throw new Error("ephemeral_mind requires a query");

      // Snapshot thinking state, disable for ephemeral
      const wasThinking = memoryConfig.thinking;
      memoryConfig.thinking = false;

      try {
        const messages = [
          { role: "system", content: EPHEMERAL_SYSTEM_PROMPT },
          { role: "user", content: query }
        ];

        const { text } = await transport.complete(messages);
        const payload = parseEphemeralPayload(text);

        return {
          result: payload,
          formatted: payload.thought_block
        };
      } finally {
        memoryConfig.thinking = wasThinking;
      }
    }
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

</details>

## `src/extensions/ingest-file.js`

**296 lines** | 8 functions/classes

This file provides a comprehensive utility for ingesting diverse document formats—including PDF, Markdown, plain text, and various code files—and preparing them into structured, manageable chunks suitable for indexing or retrieval.

**Purpose:**
The primary purpose is to standardize the ingestion process by reliably extracting raw text from complex sources (like PDFs) and then intelligently segmenting that text into meaningful, context-rich chunks based on the document's detected format.

**What it Does:**
1.  **PDF Text Extraction (`extractPdfText`):** It employs a multi-layered strategy to maximize extraction success:
    *   It first attempts modern PDF parsing using `unpdf`.
    *   If that fails, it falls back to the robust `pdf-parse` library.
    *   As a final fallback for scanned or image-heavy PDFs, it performs Optical Character Recognition (OCR) by rendering the document in a headless browser (Playwright) and processing screenshots with Tesseract.js.
2.  **Document Chunking (`chunkDocument`):** It automatically detects the file type or content structure to apply the correct chunking logic:
    *   **Markdown/RST:** Chunks content by identifying headings (`#`, `##`, etc.), ensuring that each section is treated as a distinct unit with an associated heading.
    *   **Code Files:** Splits code into chunks based on defined boundaries (e.g., empty lines) or maximum size limits, preserving logical code blocks.
    *   **Plain Text:** Divides the text by paragraphs and groups them into larger chunks to maintain contextual flow while respecting size constraints.

**Role in the Project:**
This module acts as a critical preprocessing layer for any knowledge retrieval system (e.g., RAG pipeline). By handling format variability, it ensures that raw documents are converted into high-quality, structured data chunks—each chunk retaining its original context (via headings or code boundaries)—making the resulting index highly accurate and effective for downstream querying.

### Exports

- `extractPdfText` (function)
- `chunkDocument` (function)

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/extensions/ingest-file.js`

**Imported by:** `serve.js`, `src/extensions/ingest-file.js`, `src/extensions/ingest-repo.js`

#### `extractPdfText(buffer, opts = {})` — function, line 27

* **Purpose:** Extracts text content from a PDF file provided as a buffer by attempting multiple extraction strategies sequentially, including standard parsing libraries and Optical Character Recognition (OCR).
* **Inputs:**
    * `buffer` (`Buffer`/`Uint8Array`): The raw binary data of the PDF file.
    * `opts` (`Object`, optional): Configuration options for advanced extraction methods, such as specifying a directory path for OCR processing (`dataDir`).
* **Outputs:** Promise<string> A promise that resolves to the extracted text content from the PDF.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function employs a cascading fallback mechanism: it first attempts `unpdf`, then `pdf-parse`. If both fail or return insufficient text (less than 100 characters), it proceeds to attempt OCR using Playwright/tesseract if `opts.dataDir` is provided. All attempts log detailed status messages (success, failure, page count) to the console.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export async function extractPdfText(buffer, opts = {}) {
  let text = '';

  // Strategy 1: unpdf
  try {
    const { extractText } = await import('unpdf');
    const result = await extractText(new Uint8Array(buffer));
    // unpdf returns { text: string | string[], totalPages: number }
    let extracted = '';
    if (Array.isArray(result.text)) {
      extracted = result.text.join('\n\n');
    } else if (typeof result.text === 'string') {
      extracted = result.text;
    }
    console.log('[PDF:unpdf] Pages:', result.totalPages, '| Text length:', extracted.length);
    if (extracted.trim().length > 100) return extracted;
  } catch (err) {
    console.log('[PDF:unpdf] Failed:', err.message);
  }

  // Strategy 2: pdf-parse
  try {
    const { createRequire } = await import('node:module');
    const require = createRequire(import.meta.url);
    const pdfParseFn = require("pdf-parse");
    const data = await pdfParseFn(buffer);
    text = data.text || '';
    console.log('[PDF:pdf-parse] Pages:', data.numpages, '| Text length:', text.length);
    if (text.trim().length > 100) return text;
  } catch (err) {
    console.log('[PDF:pdf-parse] Failed:', err.message);
  }

  // Strategy 3: OCR all pages via Playwright + tesseract.js
  if (opts.dataDir) {
    console.log('[PDF:OCR] Text extractors failed, attempting OCR on all pages...');
    try {
      text = await ocrAllPages(buffer, opts);
      if (text.trim().length > 50) return text;
    } catch (err) {
  // ... (truncated)

#### `ocrAllPages(buffer, opts)` — function, line 79

* **Purpose:** Performs Optical Character Recognition (OCR) on all pages of a provided PDF file by rendering it in a headless Chrome browser and processing screenshots using Tesseract.js.
* **Inputs:**

    * `opts` (`object`): Configuration options for the OCR process (e.g., language, page limits).
* **Outputs:** `Array<string>`: An array containing the extracted and cleaned text from each successfully scanned page.

    * It launches a headless Chromium instance, navigates it to the local PDF URL, and waits for rendering (including an explicit 3-second delay).
    * OCR processing is resource-intensive and involves multiple asynchronous steps (browser launch, page navigation, screenshot capture, Tesseract recognition).
    * The function relies on external libraries (`playwright`, `tesseract.js`) which must be installed.
    * **Cleanup:** Although not visible in the provided snippet, proper cleanup of the temporary HTTP server, browser instance, and OCR worker is critical to prevent resource leaks.

async function ocrAllPages(buffer, opts) {
  const { chromium } = await import('playwright');
  const { createWorker } = await import('tesseract.js');
  const { createServer } = await import('node:http');

  // Spin up a tiny temp HTTP server to serve the PDF to Chrome
  const pdfServer = createServer((req, res) => {
    res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'application/pdf', 'Content-Length': buffer.length });
    res.end(buffer);
  });

  const pdfPort = pdfServer.address().port;

  const allText = [];

  try {
    browser = await chromium.launch({ headless: true, args: ['--disable-gpu'] });
    const page = await browser.newPage();
    page.setDefaultTimeout(15000);

    await page.goto(pdfUrl, { waitUntil: 'networkidle', timeout: 20000 });
    await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 3000));

    console.log('[PDF:OCR] Scanning pages...');

    const worker = await createWorker('eng');
    let emptyStreak = 0;

    for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {

      const { data: { text } } = await worker.recognize(screenshot);

        allText.push(text.trim());

        emptyStreak++;

#### `chunkDocument(text, filename = '')` — function, line 147

* **Purpose:** Determines the appropriate chunking strategy for a document based on its file extension or content structure and calls the corresponding specialized function.
* **Inputs:**
    * `text` (string): The full text content of the document to be processed.
    * `filename` (string, optional): The name of the file containing the text; used to infer the file type/extension.
* **Outputs:** A chunked representation of the document (type inferred from internal calls like `chunkMarkdown`, etc.).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If the text contains Markdown headings (`#`, `##`, or `###`) or if the file extension is `.md`, `.txt`, or `.rst`, it uses markdown/text chunking logic.
    * If the file extension matches a code type (determined by `isCodeExt`), it uses specialized code chunking.
    * Otherwise, it defaults to plain text chunking.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export function chunkDocument(text, filename = '') {
  const ext = extname(filename).toLowerCase();
  const hasHeadings = /^#{1,3}\s+/m.test(text);

  if (hasHeadings || ext === '.md' || ext === '.txt' || ext === '.rst') {
    return chunkMarkdown(text, filename);
  } else if (isCodeExt(ext)) {
    return chunkCode(text, filename);
  } else {
    return chunkPlainText(text, filename);
  }
}
```

</details>

function isCodeExt(ext) {

    '.scala', '.zig', '.css', '.scss', '.html', '.svelte', '.vue', '.astro',
    '.sh', '.bash', '.sql', '.graphql', '.proto', '.yaml', '.yml', '.toml',
    '.json'].includes(ext);
}

#### `chunkMarkdown(text, filename)` — function, line 168

* **Purpose:** Splits a large block of markdown text into smaller, manageable sections based on markdown headings (H1-H3).
* **Inputs:**
    * `text` (string): The raw markdown content to be chunked.
    * `filename` (string, optional): An alternative name or initial heading used if the document does not start with a proper markdown header.
* **Outputs:** Array of objects: An array where each object represents a section and contains a `heading` (string) and its associated `content` (string). The structure is further processed by an internal function call (`subChunkSections`).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Sections are delimited by markdown headings (`#`, `##`, or `###`).
    * A section will only be included in the output if its content length exceeds 30 characters.
    * If no explicit heading is provided, the function uses the value of `filename` as the initial heading.
    * The final returned array is passed through an external function, `subChunkSections`, which modifies or processes the structure of the sections before returning them.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function chunkMarkdown(text, filename) {
  const lines = text.split('\n');
  const sections = [];
  let currentHeading = filename || 'Introduction';
  let currentContent = [];

  for (const line of lines) {
    if (/^#{1,3}\s+/.test(line)) {
      if (currentContent.length > 0) {
        const content = currentContent.join('\n').trim();
        if (content.length > 30) {
          sections.push({ heading: currentHeading, content });
        }
      }
      currentHeading = line.replace(/^#+\s*/, '');
      currentContent = [];
    } else {
      currentContent.push(line);
    }
  }

  if (currentContent.length > 0) {
    const content = currentContent.join('\n').trim();
    if (content.length > 30) {
      sections.push({ heading: currentHeading, content });
    }
  }

  return subChunkSections(sections);
}
```

</details>

#### `chunkCode(text, filename)` — function, line 199

* **Purpose:** Splits a large block of text into smaller, structured chunks suitable for processing or ingestion, managing chunk boundaries based on line count and empty lines.
* **Inputs:**
    * `text` (string): The raw content that needs to be split into chunks.
    * `filename` (string): The base name used to label the resulting chunks.
* **Outputs:** Array of objects `{ heading: string, content: string }`, where each object represents a distinct chunk of text and its associated file part name.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Chunking is triggered when one of three conditions is met: encountering an empty line (if the current chunk has 40+ lines), reaching a maximum of 80 lines, or reaching the end of the input text.
    * Chunks with content length less than or equal to 30 characters are discarded.
    * The `heading` for subsequent chunks is automatically appended with `(part N)` where N is the chunk index (starting from 1).
    * If the entire input text is very short, it may result in only one chunk or zero chunks if the content length threshold is not met.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function chunkCode(text, filename) {
  const lines = text.split('\n');
  const chunks = [];
  let currentChunk = [];
  let chunkIdx = 0;

  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
    currentChunk.push(lines[i]);

    const atBoundary = lines[i].trim() === '' && currentChunk.length >= 40;
    const atCap = currentChunk.length >= 80;

    if (atBoundary || atCap || i === lines.length - 1) {
      const content = currentChunk.join('\n').trim();
      if (content.length > 30) {
        chunkIdx++;
        chunks.push({
          heading: filename + (chunkIdx > 1 ? ' (part ' + chunkIdx + ')' : ''),
          content: content
        });
      }
      currentChunk = [];
    }
  }

  return chunks;
}
```

</details>

#### `chunkPlainText(text, filename)` — function, line 227

* **Purpose:** Splits a large block of plain text into smaller, manageable chunks based on character limits and paragraph structure.
* **Inputs:**
    * `text` (string): The raw textual content that needs to be chunked.
    * `filename` (string): The base filename used to generate the heading for each resulting chunk.
* **Outputs:** Array of objects, where each object contains a `heading` (string) and the `content` (string) of one text chunk.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Chunking is triggered when adding a new paragraph would exceed 1500 characters AND the current accumulated chunk content already exceeds 100 characters.
    * The function preserves paragraphs by joining them with double line breaks (`\n\n`).
    * Any remaining content in the final `currentChunk` must be longer than 30 characters to be included as a final chunk.
    * Chunk headings are automatically appended with part numbers (e.g., `filename (part 2)`) if more than one chunk is created.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function chunkPlainText(text, filename) {
  const paragraphs = text.split(/\n\s*\n+/);
  const chunks = [];
  let currentChunk = '';
  let chunkIdx = 0;

  for (const para of paragraphs) {
    const trimmed = para.trim();
    if (!trimmed) continue;

    if (currentChunk.length + trimmed.length > 1500 && currentChunk.length > 100) {
      chunkIdx++;
      chunks.push({
        heading: filename + (chunkIdx > 1 ? ' (part ' + chunkIdx + ')' : ''),
        content: currentChunk.trim()
      });
      currentChunk = trimmed;
    } else {
      currentChunk += (currentChunk ? '\n\n' : '') + trimmed;
    }
  }

  if (currentChunk.trim().length > 30) {
    chunkIdx++;
    chunks.push({
      heading: filename + (chunkIdx > 1 ? ' (part ' + chunkIdx + ')' : ''),
      content: currentChunk.trim()
    });
  }

  return chunks;
}
```

</details>

#### `subChunkSections(sections)` — function, line 260

* **Purpose:** Processes an array of document sections, splitting any section whose content exceeds 2000 characters into multiple smaller chunks while preserving structural integrity.
* **Inputs:** `sections` - An array of objects, where each object must contain a `heading` (string) and `content` (string).
* **Outputs:** Array of objects, where each object represents a chunk with a limited size. Each chunk object contains a `heading` (string) and the truncated `content` (string).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Chunks are created to ensure no single chunk exceeds 2000 characters in length.
    * Chunking only occurs if the original section content is greater than 2000 characters.
    * When splitting, a current chunk must be at least 100 characters long before it can be split off (to prevent excessive fragmentation).
    * The heading of any resulting chunk will be appended with `(part X)` to indicate its sequence within the original section.
    * If the final remaining content after processing is over 30 characters, it is pushed as a final chunk.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function subChunkSections(sections) {
  const chunks = [];

  for (const section of sections) {
    if (section.content.length <= 2000) {
      chunks.push(section);
    } else {
      const paragraphs = section.content.split(/\n\n+/);
      let currentChunk = '';
      let chunkIdx = 0;

      for (const para of paragraphs) {
        if (currentChunk.length + para.length > 2000 && currentChunk.length > 100) {
          chunkIdx++;
          chunks.push({
            heading: section.heading + (chunkIdx > 1 ? ' (part ' + chunkIdx + ')' : ''),
            content: currentChunk.trim()
          });
          currentChunk = para;
        } else {
          currentChunk += (currentChunk ? '\n\n' : '') + para;
        }
      }

      if (currentChunk.trim().length > 30) {
        chunkIdx++;
        chunks.push({
          heading: section.heading + (chunkIdx > 1 ? ' (part ' + chunkIdx + ')' : ''),
          content: currentChunk.trim()
        });
      }
    }
  }

  return chunks;
}
```

</details>

</details>

## `src/extensions/ingest-repo.js`

**111 lines** | 2 functions/classes

This module provides the core functionality for ingesting code from external Git repositories.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to automate the process of fetching a public source code repository, extracting relevant content files, structuring that content into discrete data nodes, and ensuring a clean execution environment afterward.

**What it Does:**
The main function, `ingestRepo`, performs several sequential tasks:
1. **Cloning:** It shallowly clones a specified public Git URL into a temporary directory.
2. **Filtering & Traversal:** It recursively walks the cloned repository structure while applying comprehensive ignore lists (including common build artifacts like `node_modules` and `.git`) and filtering files based on predefined valid extensions (e.g., `.js`, `.py`, `.md`).
3. **Processing:** For each valid file, it reads the content, checks size constraints, and uses an internal utility to "chunk" the document into structured nodes suitable for indexing or analysis.
4. **Cleanup:** Upon completion, it reliably deletes the entire temporary directory used during the ingestion process.

**Role in the Project:**
This module serves as the critical data pipeline component responsible for transforming raw source code repositories into a standardized, usable format (an array of content nodes). It acts as the entry point for any feature requiring analysis or indexing of external codebase contents.

### Exports

- `ingestRepo` (function)

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/extensions/ingest-repo.js`, `src/extensions/ingest-file.js`

**Imported by:** `src/extensions/ingest-repo.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `ingestRepo(repoUrl, dataDir, customIgnores = [])` — function, line 49

* **Purpose:** Clones a Git repository to a temporary directory and recursively walks through its files, processing them while respecting default and custom ignore patterns.
* **Inputs:**
    * `repoUrl` (string): The URL of the Git repository to clone.
    * `dataDir` (string): The base directory where the temporary clone will be placed.
    * `customIgnores` (string[]): An optional array of additional file or directory names that should be ignored during traversal.
* **Outputs:** Promise resolving to an object `{ nodes: Array<any>, error?: string }`, representing either the processed data chunks (`nodes`) or an error message if cloning fails.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Creates a temporary subdirectory within `dataDir` using a unique timestamp for the clone operation.
    * Uses `git clone --depth 1 --single-branch` to perform a shallow clone, which is resource-intensive and time-limited (90 seconds timeout).
    * If the git clone fails, it cleans up the temporary directory and returns an error object immediately.
    * The file traversal function (`walk`) skips files/directories starting with `.` (except `.env.example`) and any entry name present in the combined ignore set.
    * It performs side effects by creating a temporary directory structure on the filesystem and executing external shell commands (`git`).

export async function ingestRepo(repoUrl, dataDir, customIgnores = []) {
  const tempDir = join(dataDir, '_temp_repo_' + Date.now());
  await mkdir(tempDir, { recursive: true });

  // Build ignore set
  const ignores = new Set(DEFAULT_IGNORES);
  customIgnores.forEach(i => ignores.add(i));

  // Clone (shallow, single branch)
  const { execSync } = await import("node:child_process");
  try {
    execSync(`git clone --depth 1 --single-branch "${repoUrl}" "${tempDir}"`, {
      timeout: 90000,
      stdio: 'pipe'
    });
  } catch (err) {
    await rm(tempDir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
    return { nodes: [], error: 'Git clone failed: ' + err.message };

      const rel = relPath ? relPath + '/' + entry.name : entry.name;

        await walk(fullPath, rel);

#### `walk(dir, relPath = '')` — function, line 73

* **Purpose:** Recursively traverses a directory structure, reading and processing the content of valid source files into an external collection of document chunks.
* **Inputs:**
    * `dir` (string): The starting directory path for the traversal.
    * `relPath` (string, optional): The relative path prefix used when calculating the relative path for files found in this directory.
* **Outputs:** None (It modifies the external array `allChunks`).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This function relies on several external variables/constants (`ignores`, `VALID_EXTS`, `allChunks`, `join`, `readdir`, `readFile`, `chunkDocument`) which must be defined in the scope.
    * It skips files starting with a dot (`.`), unless the file is `.env.example`.
    * Files are skipped if their name is present in the global `ignores` set.
    * Only files matching extensions defined in `VALID_EXTS` are processed.
    * File content must be between 30 and 50,000 characters (inclusive) to be processed; otherwise, it is skipped.
    * The function accumulates results by pushing chunks into the external array `allChunks`.
    * Errors during file reading or processing are silently caught and ignored (`try...catch`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async function walk(dir, relPath = '') {
    let entries;
    try { entries = await readdir(dir, { withFileTypes: true }); }
    catch { return; }

    for (const entry of entries) {
      if (entry.name.startsWith('.') && entry.name !== '.env.example') continue;
      if (ignores.has(entry.name)) continue;

      const fullPath = join(dir, entry.name);
      const rel = relPath ? relPath + '/' + entry.name : entry.name;

      if (entry.isDirectory()) {
        await walk(fullPath, rel);
      } else {
        const ext = extname(entry.name).toLowerCase();
        if (!VALID_EXTS.has(ext)) continue;

        try {
          const content = await readFile(fullPath, 'utf8');
          if (content.length < 30) continue;
          if (content.length > 50000) continue; // Skip huge generated files

          const fileChunks = chunkDocument(content, rel);
          allChunks.push(...fileChunks);
        } catch { /* skip binary/unreadable */ }
      }
    }
  }
```

</details>

</details>

## `src/extensions/web-search.js`

**147 lines** | 1 functions/classes

This file defines and exports the `web_search` tool, designed to provide the system with up-to-date external information from the internet.

**Purpose:**
The primary purpose of this tool is to allow the AI agent to search for current facts, documentation, tutorials, or technical answers that are not contained within its existing knowledge base (memory). It acts as a mechanism for grounding responses in real-time web data.

**Functionality:**
When executed with a keyword query, the tool performs a multi-step process:
1. **Search:** It first queries Google via a dedicated server API endpoint (`/api/websearch`) using Playwright (headless Chrome) to retrieve initial search results and snippets.
2. **Relevance Scoring:** It scores these initial results based on keyword overlap with the original query, filtering for the most relevant sources.
3. **Deep Fetching:** For the top relevant results, it fetches the full content of the corresponding web pages via another server API endpoint (`/api/fetch`). This process includes respectful delays to avoid overloading external sites.
4. **Processing & Output:** The fetched HTML content is cleaned and converted into Markdown format (server-side). Finally, the tool structures this information—including titles, URLs, and truncated content—into a comprehensive output that can be used both for prompt injection (providing context to the LLM) and for persistent node storage in the project's memory.

**Role in the Project:**
The `web_search` tool is critical for maintaining the system's relevance and accuracy. It serves as an external knowledge retrieval mechanism, ensuring that the AI can provide timely, verifiable information when required by the user query. The client-side code handles coordination (displaying a loading overlay), while all resource-intensive tasks (scraping, HTML parsing) are delegated to robust server-side APIs.

### Exports

- `createWebSearchTool` (function)

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/tools/base.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `createWebSearchTool(config = {})` — function, line 23

* **Purpose:** Creates and returns a structured tool object designed to search external web sources for current information using an API endpoint.
* **Inputs:**
    * **config** (`Object`, optional): Configuration options for the web search tool.
        * **apiBase** (`string`, optional): The base URL used to construct the full search API endpoint. Defaults to an empty string.
        * **maxResults** (`number`, optional): The maximum number of results expected from the search (defaults to 3).
        * **memoryConfig** (`Object`, optional): A configuration object that may contain a boolean flag, `webSearchEnabled`, used as a kill switch for the tool.
* **Outputs:** An instance of a `Tool` object containing metadata, parameter definitions, and an asynchronous `execute` method.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The returned tool requires a non-empty `query` string when executed.
    * If `memoryConfig.webSearchEnabled` is explicitly set to `false`, the execution function immediately returns a disabled result object without making any API calls.
    * The `execute` method performs a significant side effect by manipulating the DOM, adding a full-screen overlay (`#web-search-overlay`) to the document body while the search is in progress to inform the user.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export function createWebSearchTool(config = {}) {
  const apiBase = config.apiBase || '';
  const maxResults = config.maxResults || 3;
  const memoryConfig = config.memoryConfig || null;

  return new Tool({
    name: "web_search",
    discovery: "bm25",
    description: "Search the web for current information, documentation, tutorials, code examples, or answers to technical questions. Use when the conversation requires up-to-date facts, external references, or information not available in memory.",
    parameters: {
      type: "object",
      properties: {
        query: {
          type: "string",
          description: "A short Google search query, 3-6 words, like a human would type into a search bar. No full sentences. No questions. Just keywords."
        }
      },
      required: ["query"]
    },
    execute: async function (params) {
      const query = params.query;
      if (!query) throw new Error("web_search requires a query");

      // Kill switch — check config flag
      if (memoryConfig && memoryConfig.webSearchEnabled === false) {
        console.log('[WebSearch] Disabled (webSearchEnabled: false)');
        return { result: { results: [], query }, formatted: "Web search is currently disabled." };
      }
      // Show searching modal so user knows not to interact
      const overlay = document.createElement('div');
      overlay.id = 'web-search-overlay';
      overlay.style.cssText = 'position:fixed;inset:0;background:rgba(0,0,0,0.75);display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;z-index:9999;';
      overlay.innerHTML = '<div style="background:#1a1a2e;border:1px solid #333;border-radius:12px;padding:40px 60px;text-align:center;font-family:inherit;"><div style="font-size:1.4rem;color:#e0e0e0;margin-bottom:12px;">🔍 AI is searching the web</div><div style="font-size:0.95rem;color:#888;margin-bottom:8px;">Do not make adjustments</div><div style="font-size:0.85rem;color:#555;font-style:italic;">"' + query + '"</div></div>';
      document.body.appendChild(overlay);

      try {
        // Step 1: Search via Playwright (visible Chrome window)
        const searchRes = await fetch(`${apiBase}/api/websearch?q=${encodeURIComponent(query)}`);
        if (!searchRes.ok) throw new Error("Search failed: " + searchRes.status);
        const searchData = await searchRes.json();
  // ... (truncated)
```

## `src/index.js`

**500 lines** | 23 functions/classes

The `LuminalMemory` class serves as the main entry point and comprehensive API for managing persistent, context-aware memory for Large Language Models (LLMs). Its core purpose is to facilitate complex conversational cycles by intelligently handling conversation history, external tool usage, and efficient knowledge retrieval.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Manages Context:** It orchestrates the entire chat cycle: taking a user message, appending it to a historical chain, retrieving relevant past information, building a comprehensive prompt, calling an LLM, and finally saving the assistant's response.
2.  **Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG):** It implements advanced retrieval mechanisms using multiple indexing techniques (BM25, Bloom Filter, TF-IDF) combined with a sliding window to fetch highly relevant historical nodes from memory.
3.  **Tool Orchestration:** It manages an integrated tool system. When a query is received, it first identifies potential tools, uses the LLM to determine if and how those tools should be used (tool routing), executes the necessary functions, and injects the results back into the prompt context for the final response generation.
4.  **Memory Persistence:** It handles memory storage and management using dedicated components for archiving and ensuring that the system does not exceed defined memory limits.

**Role in the Project:**
`LuminalMemory` acts as the central brain of the application. By integrating conversation history, sophisticated retrieval algorithms, and dynamic tool execution into a single workflow, it ensures that LLM interactions are grounded, contextually rich, and capable of performing complex tasks beyond simple chat responses. It abstracts away the complexity of memory management from the user, providing a robust, zero-dependency framework for building advanced AI applications.

#### `LuminalMemory()` — class, line 24

* **Purpose:** Manages and orchestrates all components required for a sophisticated, persistent memory system used by large language models (LLMs).
* **Inputs:**
    * `userConfig` (`Object`): Configuration object containing settings for various sub-components like BM25, BloomFilter, and general operational parameters.
* **Outputs:**
    * `Promise<LuminalMemory>`: A promise that resolves to the initialized instance of `LuminalMemory`, indicating successful setup of all memory components.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The constructor initializes numerous complex internal systems (e.g., `Chain`, `BM25`, `Retrieval`, `Archive`) using the provided configuration, making it a heavy setup process.
    * The `init()` method is asynchronous and must be called explicitly to set up persistent storage (like IndexedDB) via `this.archive.init()`.
    * It sets an internal state flag (`_initialized`) upon successful initialization.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class LuminalMemory {
  constructor(userConfig = {}) {
    this.config = createConfig(userConfig);

    // Core components
    this.chain = new Chain();
    this.window = new Window(this.config);
    this.conversationManager = new ConversationManager(this.config);
    this.bm25 = new BM25(this.config.bm25);
    this.bloom = new BloomFilter(this.config.bloom);
    this.tfidf = new TfIdf();
    this.archive = new Archive();
    this.memoryManager = new MemoryManager(this.chain, this.config);
    this.transport = new LLMTransport(this.config);
    this.compaction = new Compaction(
      this.chain, this.bm25, this.bloom, this.tfidf, this.archive, this.config
    );
    this.retrieval = new Retrieval(
      this.chain, this.bm25, this.bloom, this.tfidf, this.compaction, this.archive, this.transport, this.config
    );

    // Tool system
    this.toolRegistry = new ToolRegistry(this.config);

    // Pocket — parallel instruction queue
    this.pocket = new Pocket();

    // Settings — runtime config management with schema
    this.settings = new Settings(this.config, this.transport);

    this._initialized = false;
  }

  /**
   * Initialize the memory system (sets up IndexedDB if available).
   */
  async init() {
    await this.archive.init();
    this._initialized = true;
    return this;
  // ... (truncated)

#### `chat(message)` — method, line 71

* **Purpose:** Processes a user message by managing memory, appending the message to the chat history, identifying and executing relevant external tools, and preparing the context for an LLM response.
* **Inputs:**
    * `message` (`string`): The text content of the user's query or message.
* **Outputs:** Promise (The final result object from the interaction, though the return value is truncated in the provided snippet.)
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * **Initialization:** Ensures the chat system is initialized via `this.init()` if it hasn't been already.
    * **Memory Check:** Throws an error immediately if the current memory usage exceeds the configured limit (`this.config.memoryLimitMB`).
    * **State Mutation:** Appends the user message to the internal chain history and updates the BM25 index with the new node.
    * **Tool Execution:** If relevant tools are found, it uses an LLM call (`_askToolDecision`) to determine tool usage, executes the chosen tool(s) via `this.toolRegistry`, and logs success/failure messages for each execution.

<details>

  async chat(message) {
    if (!this._initialized) await this.init();

    // Check memory before appending
    const memCheck = this.memoryManager.check();
    if (!memCheck.ok) {
      throw new Error(
        `Memory limit reached (${memCheck.usageMB.toFixed(1)}MB / ${this.config.memoryLimitMB}MB). ` +
        `Please trim or branch to free memory.`
      );
    }

    // 1. Append user message as new node
    const userNode = this.chain.append("user", message);
    this.bm25.add(userNode);

    // 2. Discover relevant tools for this query
    const matchedTools = this.toolRegistry.retrieve(message);
    const toolsUsed = [];

    // 3. If tools matched, ask the LLM to decide whether to use them
    let toolResults = [];
    if (matchedTools.length > 0) {
      const toolDecision = await this._askToolDecision(message, matchedTools);
      
      if (toolDecision) {
        // Execute the tool
        const result = await this.toolRegistry.execute(toolDecision.name, toolDecision.params, {
          query: message,
          chain: this.chain,
          config: this.config
        });
        
        if (result.success) {
          toolResults.push({ name: toolDecision.name, result: result.result });
          toolsUsed.push({ name: toolDecision.name, params: toolDecision.params, result: result.result, elapsed: result.elapsed });
          console.log(`[Chat] Tool "${toolDecision.name}" executed successfully in ${result.elapsed}ms`);
        } else {
          console.warn(`[Chat] Tool "${toolDecision.name}" failed: ${result.error}`);
        }

#### `addPocketNote(nodeId, note)` — method, line 262

* **Purpose:** Adds a pocket note to a specified node and ensures that the new content is immediately searchable by updating the internal search index.
* **Inputs:**
    * `nodeId` (string | number): The unique identifier of the node where the note should be added.
    * `note` (string): The text content of the pocket note to be added.
* **Outputs:** Object: The resulting node object after the pocket note has been successfully attached.
* **Behavioral Notes:** This method performs a side effect by modifying the internal search index (`this.bm25`), first removing the old entry for `nodeId` and then adding the newly created/updated node to ensure data consistency for searching. If the underlying chain operation fails (returns falsy), no indexing updates occur.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  addPocketNote(nodeId, note) {
    const node = this.chain.addPocketNote(nodeId, note);
    if (node) {
      // Re-index so the correction terms are searchable
      this.bm25.remove(nodeId);
      this.bm25.add(node);
    }
    return node;
  }
```

</details>

  append(role, content) {

    if (role !== "compaction") {

#### `trim({ from, to }, summary = null)` — method, line 343

* **Purpose:** Trims the data within a specified range (`from` to `to`), optionally generating or utilizing a pre-calculated summary for compaction.
* **Inputs:**
    * `{ from, to }`: Object containing the start and end points (ranges) for the trimming operation.
    * `summary = null`: Optional summary object used by the compaction process; if null, it will be generated internally.
* **Outputs:** Promise resolving to the result of the compaction trim operation.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The method ensures internal initialization (`this.init()`) before proceeding.
    * If `summary` is not provided, it automatically generates a summary by retrieving all nodes in the range using `this.chain.range()` and passing them to `this.transport.generateSummary()`.
    * It relies on external components: `this.chain`, `this.transport`, and `this.compaction`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async trim({ from, to }, summary = null) {
    if (!this._initialized) await this.init();

    if (!summary) {
      const nodes = this.chain.range(from, to);
      summary = await this.transport.generateSummary(nodes);
    }

    return this.compaction.trim(from, to, summary);
  }
```

</details>

#### `trimFromHere(summary = null)` — method, line 382

* **Purpose:** Trims data nodes from the chain, ensuring that any nodes outside the current processing window are summarized and compacted before returning the result.
* **Inputs:** summary (Optional[any]): A pre-generated summary of nodes to be trimmed; if null, the method calculates this summary internally based on the current window context.
* **Outputs:** Promise<any>: The result of the compaction process after trimming data outside the defined window size.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The method ensures internal initialization by calling `this.init()` if `this._initialized` is false.
    * If no `summary` is provided, it identifies all nodes in the chain and filters out those that fall within the current processing window (`this.window`). These external nodes are then passed to `this.transport.generateSummary()`.
    * The final result is determined by calling `this.compaction.trimBeforeWindow()` using the configured window size and the calculated/provided summary.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async trimFromHere(summary = null) {
    if (!this._initialized) await this.init();

    if (!summary) {
      const windowNodes = this.window.select(this.chain);
      const allNodes = this.chain.all();
      const windowIds = new Set(windowNodes.map(n => n.id));
      const nodesToTrim = allNodes.filter(n => !windowIds.has(n.id));
      if (nodesToTrim.length > 0) {
        summary = await this.transport.generateSummary(nodesToTrim);
      }
    }

    return this.compaction.trimBeforeWindow(this.config.windowSize, summary);
  }
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async branch(summary = null) {
    if (!this._initialized) await this.init();

    if (!summary) {
      const allNodes = this.chain.all();
      if (allNodes.length > 0) {
        summary = await this.transport.generateSummary(allNodes.slice(-50));
      }
    }

    return this.compaction.branch(summary);
  }
```
