## `src/search/bloom.js`

**191 lines** | 14 functions/classes

This source file implements an advanced data structure called a **Bloom Filter** designed for efficient membership testing of large sets of terms.

**Purpose:**
The primary purpose is to track whether specific terms (like words or tokens) are present in a collection of archived blocks or texts while minimizing memory usage and avoiding false negatives. It is specifically engineered to support insertion, membership testing, *and* deletion of terms.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Initialization:** It calculates optimal parameters (size and hash count) based on the expected number of items and the desired false positive rate.
2.  **Data Structure:** Instead of a single bit array, it uses two sub-tables (`left` and `right`) with counters to implement the "d-Left" hashing strategy. This technique improves accuracy by directing an item's count increment to the less-loaded table.
3.  **Operations:**
    *   **`add(term)`:** Inserts a term by incrementing the counter in the designated sub-table.
    *   **`test(term)`:** Checks if a term *might* exist (returns `true`) or is definitely absent (returns `false`).
    *   **`remove(term)`:** Decrements the counters, allowing the filter to track changes when blocks are restored or removed.
4.  **Utility:** It includes helper functions (`uniqueTerms`, `fnv1a`) to preprocess text into unique terms and generate stable hash pairs for reliable indexing.

**Role in the Project:**
This class serves as a core vocabulary tracker, acting as an efficient index of all unique terms encountered across multiple data blocks or archives. Its ability to support deletion is crucial for scenarios where the underlying dataset changes (e.g., restoring or removing archived content), making it more robust than a standard Bloom Filter implementation.

### Exports

- `BloomFilter` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`, `tests/bloom.test.js`, `tests/compaction.test.js`, `tests/retrieval.test.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `BloomFilter()` — class, line 9

* **Purpose:** Implements a space-efficient probabilistic data structure to test for the potential existence of terms without storing them explicitly.
* **Inputs:**
    * `config` (`object`, optional): Configuration object used to set initial parameters.
        * `expectedItems` (`number`, default 100000): The estimated number of unique items expected to be added to the filter.
        * `falsePositiveRate` (`number`, default 0.01): The maximum acceptable probability that a non-existent item will incorrectly appear as existing (False Positive Rate).
* **Outputs:** None (The class manages internal state).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The constructor calculates the optimal bit array size and hash count based on the provided expected items and false positive rate.
    * It uses a specialized "d-left" hashing strategy, maintaining two separate counter arrays (`left` and `right`) to track load balance when adding terms.
    * Adding an item increments one of the counters (either `left` or `right`) at the calculated index, ensuring that the side with the lower current load is chosen first.
    * The structure uses `Uint8Array`s for counter storage, meaning each individual counter can only track up to 255 additions before potential overflow/saturation occurs.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class BloomFilter {
  constructor(config = {}) {
    const expectedItems = config.expectedItems || 100000;
    const fpRate = config.falsePositiveRate || 0.01;

    // Calculate optimal size
    this.size = Math.ceil(-(expectedItems * Math.log(fpRate)) / (Math.log(2) ** 2));
    this.hashCount = Math.ceil((this.size / expectedItems) * Math.log(2));

    // Two sub-tables for d-left hashing, each with counters
    this.tableSize = Math.ceil(this.size / 2);
    this.left = new Uint8Array(this.tableSize);   // 4-bit counters packed in bytes
    this.right = new Uint8Array(this.tableSize);

    this.itemCount = 0;
  }

  /**
   * Add a term associated with a block.
   * @param {string} term
   */
  add(term) {
    const h = this._hashes(term);
    const leftLoad = this.left[h.left % this.tableSize];
    const rightLoad = this.right[h.right % this.tableSize];

    // Insert into less-loaded side (d-left strategy)
    if (leftLoad <= rightLoad) {
      const idx = h.left % this.tableSize;
      if (this.left[idx] < 255) this.left[idx]++;
    } else {
      const idx = h.right % this.tableSize;
      if (this.right[idx] < 255) this.right[idx]++;
    }
    this.itemCount++;
  }

  /**
   * Test if a term might exist in any archived block.
   * @param {string} term
  // ... (truncated)
```

```javascript
  constructor(config = {}) {
    const expectedItems = config.expectedItems || 100000;
    const fpRate = config.falsePositiveRate || 0.01;

    // Calculate optimal size
    this.size = Math.ceil(-(expectedItems * Math.log(fpRate)) / (Math.log(2) ** 2));
    this.hashCount = Math.ceil((this.size / expectedItems) * Math.log(2));

    // Two sub-tables for d-left hashing, each with counters
    this.tableSize = Math.ceil(this.size / 2);
    this.left = new Uint8Array(this.tableSize);   // 4-bit counters packed in bytes
```

#### `add(term)` — method, line 30

* **Purpose:** Adds a term to the Bloom filter by calculating multiple hashes and incrementing the counter at the index corresponding to the side with the lowest current load, implementing a "d-left" distribution strategy.
* **Inputs:**
    * `term` (string): The item or word to be added to the set represented by the Bloom filter.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The method modifies the internal state of the Bloom filter instance (`this.left`, `this.right`, and `this.itemCount`).
    * It uses a load-balancing strategy, always incrementing the counter on the side (left or right) that currently has fewer set bits/counts.
    * Counter saturation is implemented: individual counters in `this.left` and `this.right` cannot exceed 255; if they do, no further increment occurs for that index.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  add(term) {
    const h = this._hashes(term);
    const leftLoad = this.left[h.left % this.tableSize];
    const rightLoad = this.right[h.right % this.tableSize];

    // Insert into less-loaded side (d-left strategy)
    if (leftLoad <= rightLoad) {
      const idx = h.left % this.tableSize;
      if (this.left[idx] < 255) this.left[idx]++;
    } else {
      const idx = h.right % this.tableSize;
      if (this.right[idx] < 255) this.right[idx]++;
    }
    this.itemCount++;
  }
```

</details>

#### `test(term)` — method, line 51

* **Purpose:** Checks if a given term might be present in the set represented by the Bloom filter structure.
* **Inputs:**
    * `term` (string): The item whose potential membership is being tested.
* **Outputs:** boolean - Returns `true` if the term *might* be in the set (potential false positive); returns `false` if the term is definitely not in the set.
* **Behavioral Notes:** This method implements a probabilistic check; therefore, it can return `true` even if the item was never added to the filter (a false positive). It relies on internal state properties (`this.left`, `this.right`, `this.tableSize`) and the existence of a private hashing method (`this._hashes`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  test(term) {
    const h = this._hashes(term);
    const leftVal = this.left[h.left % this.tableSize];
    const rightVal = this.right[h.right % this.tableSize];
    return leftVal > 0 || rightVal > 0;
  }
```

</details>

#### `remove(term)` — method, line 62

* **Purpose:** Removes a term's presence by decrementing corresponding counters in the internal hash tables (`left` or `right`) and updating the total item count.
* **Inputs:**
    * `term` (string): The specific term that needs to be removed from the structure.
* **Outputs:** void: No value is returned; the method modifies the object's state by decrementing internal counters (`this.left`, `this.right`) and updating `this.itemCount`.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The removal process calculates two indices, one for the left structure and one for the right structure.
    * It attempts to safely decrement the counter at the left index first. If that counter is zero, it then attempts to decrement the counter at the right index.
    * `this.itemCount` is decremented only if a successful removal (decrement) occurs on either side.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  remove(term) {
    const h = this._hashes(term);
    const leftIdx = h.left % this.tableSize;
    const rightIdx = h.right % this.tableSize;

    // Decrement from both sides if non-zero (safe approach)
    if (this.left[leftIdx] > 0) {
      this.left[leftIdx]--;
      this.itemCount--;
    } else if (this.right[rightIdx] > 0) {
      this.right[rightIdx]--;
      this.itemCount--;
    }
  }
```

</details>

#### `fnv1a(str, seed)` — function, line 168

* **Purpose:** Calculates a hash value for a given string using the FNV-1a hashing algorithm.
* **Inputs:**
    * `str` (string): The input string to be hashed.
    * `seed` (number): The initial seed value used for the hash calculation.
* **Outputs:** number: A positive integer representing the calculated 31-bit FNV-1a hash.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function uses bitwise operations and `Math.imul` to simulate 32-bit unsigned integer arithmetic, ensuring the final result is constrained to a positive range (modulo $2^{31}-1$).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function fnv1a(str, seed) {
  let hash = seed;
  for (let i = 0; i < str.length; i++) {
    hash ^= str.charCodeAt(i);
    hash = Math.imul(hash, 0x01000193);
  }
  return (hash >>> 0) % 0x7FFFFFFF;
}
```

</details>

#### `uniqueTerms(text)` — function, line 182

* **Purpose:** Extracts all unique, lowercase terms from a given string after normalizing case, removing punctuation, and filtering out tokens shorter than two characters.
* **Inputs:** text (string) - The input block of text to process.
* **Outputs:** Array of Strings (`string[]`) - An array containing all unique, cleaned words found in the input text.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function converts all text to lowercase and removes non-word/non-whitespace characters (punctuation).
    * It filters out any resulting tokens that are only one character long or empty strings.
    * Duplicate terms are automatically removed using a `Set`.
    * If the input `text` is falsy (null, undefined, or empty string), it returns an empty array immediately.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function uniqueTerms(text) {
  if (!text) return [];
  const terms = text
    .toLowerCase()
    .replace(/[^\w\s]/g, " ")
    .split(/\s+/)
    .filter(t => t.length > 1);
  return [...new Set(terms)];
}
```

</details>

</details>

## `src/search/bm25.js`

**176 lines** | 11 functions/classes

This file provides a pure JavaScript implementation of the **BM25 (Best Matching 25) ranking algorithm**, which is used for calculating relevance scores in information retrieval systems.

**Purpose:**
The primary purpose of this module is to build, manage, and query an inverted index structure to determine how relevant a set of documents (nodes) are to a given search query.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Indexing (`add`, `remove`):** It maintains an in-memory inverted index that maps every unique term found across all documents to a list of document IDs and the frequency of that term within those documents. It also tracks document lengths and total document counts.
2.  **Searching (`search`):** Given a query string, it calculates a composite relevance score for every indexed document using the BM25 formula. This score incorporates factors like Inverse Document Frequency (IDF), Term Frequency (TF), and document length normalization.
3.  **Utility:** It includes methods to rebuild the entire index from scratch (`rebuild`), save/load the index state (`export`/`import`), and calculate the best possible score for a query (`bestScore`).
4.  **Preprocessing:** Helper functions handle tokenization (converting text into clean, lowercase terms) and counting term frequencies within documents.

**Role in the Project:**
This module serves as the core ranking engine for any search or retrieval component. It is responsible for taking raw content and a user query, processing them through its index, and outputting an ordered list of results ranked by their calculated relevance score.

### Exports

- `tokenize` (function)
- `BM25` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`, `src/tools/registry.js`, `tests/bm25.test.js`, `tests/compaction.test.js`, `tests/conversation.test.js`, `tests/retrieval.test.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `BM25()` — class, line 6

* **Purpose:** Implements the BM25 ranking algorithm for information retrieval by managing an inverted index of documents and calculating relevance scores.
* **Inputs:**
    * `config` (`object`, optional): Configuration parameters for the BM25 formula, including `k1` (default: 1.2) and `b` (default: 0.4).
    * `node` (`object`): A document to be added to the index, requiring at least an `id` (unique identifier) and `content` (the text content).
    * `nodeId` (`number`): The unique ID of a document that needs to be removed from the index.
* **Outputs:** None (The class manages state internally).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * **State Mutation:** The class maintains internal state tracking the inverted index (`this.index`), document lengths (`this.docLengths`), total document count (`this.docCount`), and the sum of all document lengths (`this.totalLength`).
    * **`add(node)`:** Updates `docLengths`, increments `docCount`, updates `totalLength`, and populates/updates the term frequencies in `this.index`.
    * **`remove(nodeId)`:** (Inferred) Must correctly decrement `docCount`, adjust `totalLength`, remove entries from `docLengths`, and clean up all associated term postings within `this.index` for the given `nodeId`. Failure to fully clean up state will lead to incorrect scoring.

```javascript
export class BM25 {
  constructor(config = {}) {
    this.k1 = config.k1 || 1.2;
    this.b = config.b || 0.4;

    // Inverted index: term -> [{ nodeId, freq }]
    this.index = new Map();
    // Document lengths: nodeId -> wordCount
    this.docLengths = new Map();
    // Total documents indexed
    this.docCount = 0;
    // Sum of all document lengths (for avgdl)
    this.totalLength = 0;
  }

  /**
   * Add a node to the index.
   * @param {object} node
   */
  add(node) {
    const terms = tokenize(node.content);
    const termFreqs = countTerms(terms);

    this.docLengths.set(node.id, terms.length);
    this.docCount++;
    this.totalLength += terms.length;

    for (const [term, freq] of termFreqs) {
      if (!this.index.has(term)) {
        this.index.set(term, []);
      }
      this.index.get(term).push({ nodeId: node.id, freq });
    }
  }
```

#### `remove(nodeId)` — method, line 45

* **Purpose:** Removes all data associated with a specific document node ID (`nodeId`) from the search index, updating internal counts and cleaning up the inverted index accordingly.
* **Inputs:** nodeId (string | number): The unique identifier of the document/node to be removed from the index.
* **Outputs:** void
* **Behavioral Notes:** If `nodeId` does not exist in `this.docLengths`, the method exits immediately without making changes. It updates `this.docLengths`, decrements `this.docCount`, and reduces `this.totalLength`. The method iterates through every term in the index, filtering out any posting list entries that match the removed `nodeId`; if a term's postings become empty after removal, the entire term is deleted from the index.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  remove(nodeId) {
    const docLen = this.docLengths.get(nodeId);
    if (docLen === undefined) return;

    this.docLengths.delete(nodeId);
    this.docCount--;
    this.totalLength -= docLen;

    // Remove from inverted index
    for (const [term, postings] of this.index) {
      const filtered = postings.filter(p => p.nodeId !== nodeId);
      if (filtered.length === 0) {
        this.index.delete(term);
      } else {
        this.index.set(term, filtered);
      }
    }
  }
```

</details>

#### `search(query, topK = 10)` — method, line 70

* **Purpose:** Calculates and returns the top K ranked results (document IDs) based on the BM25 ranking algorithm given a search query.
* **Inputs:**
    * `query` (string): The text string to be searched against the index.
    * `topK` (number, optional): The maximum number of top results to return; defaults to 10.
* **Outputs:** Array of objects `{ nodeId: number, score: number }`, representing the top K document IDs sorted by their calculated BM25 relevance score in descending order.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If the input `query` is empty or tokenization results in no terms, an empty array is returned immediately.
    * The calculation relies on internal state variables (`this.docCount`, `this.totalLength`, `this.index`, `this.docLengths`, `this.k1`, `this.b`) which must be correctly initialized for BM25 scoring to function.
    * If no documents match any terms in the query, an empty array is returned.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  search(query, topK = 10) {
    const queryTerms = tokenize(query);
    if (queryTerms.length === 0) return [];

    const avgdl = this.docCount > 0 ? this.totalLength / this.docCount : 1;
    const scores = new Map();

    for (const term of queryTerms) {
      const postings = this.index.get(term);
      if (!postings) continue;

      const df = postings.length;
      const idf = Math.log(1 + (this.docCount - df + 0.5) / (df + 0.5));

      for (const { nodeId, freq } of postings) {
        const dl = this.docLengths.get(nodeId) || 1;
        const numerator = freq * (this.k1 + 1);
        const denominator = freq + this.k1 * (1 - this.b + this.b * (dl / avgdl));
        const termScore = idf * (numerator / denominator);

        scores.set(nodeId, (scores.get(nodeId) || 0) + termScore);
      }
    }

    // Sort by score descending, return top K
    return Array.from(scores.entries())
      .map(([nodeId, score]) => ({ nodeId, score }))
      .sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score)
      .slice(0, topK);
  }
```

</details>

<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  rebuild(nodes) {
    this.index.clear();
    this.docLengths.clear();
    this.docCount = 0;
    this.totalLength = 0;
    for (const node of nodes) {
      if (node.role !== "compaction") {
        this.add(node);
      }
    }
  }
```

```javascript
export function tokenize(text) {
  if (!text) return [];
  return text
    .toLowerCase()
    .replace(/[^\w\s]/g, " ")
    .split(/\s+/)
    .filter(t => t.length > 0);
}
```

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function countTerms(terms) {
  const counts = new Map();
  for (const term of terms) {
    counts.set(term, (counts.get(term) || 0) + 1);
  }
  return counts;
}
```

## `src/search/tfidf.js`

**173 lines** | 10 functions/classes

This module implements a **TF-IDF Vector Engine** designed for indexing text data, calculating relevance scores, and ranking archived content.

**Purpose:**
The primary purpose of the `TfIdf` class is to transform raw blocks of text into sparse numerical vectors using the Term Frequency-Inverse Document Frequency (TF-IDF) weighting scheme. It facilitates semantic search by quantifying how important a term is within a specific block relative to the entire corpus.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Vector Computation (`computeVector`):** Calculates the TF-IDF vector for a given text block during the compaction phase, using global document frequency statistics.
2.  **State Management:** It maintains and updates two critical pieces of global state: `documentFrequency` (tracking how many blocks contain each unique term) and `blockCount` (the total number of indexed blocks). Methods are provided to register and unregister blocks accordingly.
3.  **Similarity Scoring (`cosineSimilarity`):** Computes the cosine similarity between a query vector and stored block vectors, providing a measure of angular distance (relevance) between them.
4.  **Ranking:** It provides a high-level function (`rankBlocks`) that takes a user query and a list of archived blocks, computes the relevance score for each block against the query, and returns them sorted by descending score.

**Role in the Project:**
This engine acts as the core indexing and retrieval component for an archival or search system. It is responsible for:
*   **Indexing/Compaction Time:** Building the necessary statistical metadata (document frequency) and generating the feature vectors for all stored data blocks.
*   **Query Time:** Receiving a user query, converting it into a vector, and efficiently comparing that query vector against the pre-computed vectors of archived blocks to determine relevance and provide ranked search results.

### Exports

- `TfIdf` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`, `tests/compaction.test.js`, `tests/retrieval.test.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `TfIdf()` — class, line 7

* **Purpose:** Computes the Term Frequency-Inverse Document Frequency (TF-IDF) vector for a given block of text based on previously indexed document statistics.
* **Inputs:**
    * `text` (`string`): The concatenated text content of the block for which the vector needs to be computed.
* **Outputs:**
    * `Map<string, number>`: A sparse vector where keys are terms and values are their calculated TF-IDF scores.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This class maintains internal state (`documentFrequency` and `blockCount`) which must be populated by other methods (not shown) before `computeVector` is called accurately.
    * The IDF calculation uses a smoothed formula: $\ln((\text{blockCount} + 1) / (\text{df} + 1)) + 1$.
    * If the input text is empty or contains no tokens, an empty Map is returned.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class TfIdf {
  constructor() {
    // Global document frequency: term -> number of blocks containing it
    this.documentFrequency = new Map();
    // Total number of blocks indexed
    this.blockCount = 0;
  }

  /**
   * Compute a TF-IDF vector for a block of text.
   * Call this at compaction time.
   * @param {string} text - concatenated text of all nodes in the block
   * @returns {Map<string, number>} sparse vector (term -> tfidf score)
   */
  computeVector(text) {
    const terms = tokenize(text);
    if (terms.length === 0) return new Map();

    // Term frequency in this block
    const tf = new Map();
    for (const term of terms) {
      tf.set(term, (tf.get(term) || 0) + 1);
    }

    // Normalize TF by total terms
    const totalTerms = terms.length;
    const vector = new Map();

    for (const [term, count] of tf) {
      const normalizedTf = count / totalTerms;
      const df = this.documentFrequency.get(term) || 0;
      // Use smoothed IDF: ln((blockCount + 1) / (df + 1)) + 1
      const idf = Math.log((this.blockCount + 1) / (df + 1)) + 1;
      vector.set(term, normalizedTf * idf);
    }

    return vector;
  }
```

#### `computeVector(text)` — method, line 21

* **Purpose:** Computes the TF-IDF vector for a given block of text based on pre-calculated document and corpus statistics.
* **Inputs:**
    * `text` (`string`): The input text block to be vectorized.
* **Outputs:** `Map<string, number>`: A map where keys are unique terms found in the text, and values are their corresponding TF-IDF weights.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If the input text is empty or contains no tokens, an empty Map is returned immediately.
    * The calculation uses a normalized Term Frequency (TF) of `count / totalTerms`.
    * It employs a smoothed Inverse Document Frequency (IDF) formula: $\ln((\text{blockCount} + 1) / (\text{df} + 1)) + 1$.
    * This method relies on the class having initialized properties `this.documentFrequency` and `this.blockCount`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  computeVector(text) {
    const terms = tokenize(text);
    if (terms.length === 0) return new Map();

    // Term frequency in this block
    const tf = new Map();
    for (const term of terms) {
      tf.set(term, (tf.get(term) || 0) + 1);
    }

    // Normalize TF by total terms
    const totalTerms = terms.length;
    const vector = new Map();

    for (const [term, count] of tf) {
      const normalizedTf = count / totalTerms;
      const df = this.documentFrequency.get(term) || 0;
      // Use smoothed IDF: ln((blockCount + 1) / (df + 1)) + 1
      const idf = Math.log((this.blockCount + 1) / (df + 1)) + 1;
      vector.set(term, normalizedTf * idf);
    }

    return vector;
  }
```

</details>

* **Inputs:**

* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** Modifies internal state by incrementing `this.documentFrequency` for every unique term found in the input text, and increments `this.blockCount`. It relies on an external `tokenize(text)` function to extract tokens from the input string.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  registerBlock(text) {
    const uniqueTermsSet = new Set(tokenize(text));
    for (const term of uniqueTermsSet) {
      this.documentFrequency.set(term, (this.documentFrequency.get(term) || 0) + 1);
    }
    this.blockCount++;
```

#### `unregisterBlock(text)` — method, line 63

* **Purpose:** Decrements the document frequency counts for all unique terms found in the provided text block and reduces the internal block counter.
* **Inputs:**
    * `text` (string): The text content of the block being unregistered, used to calculate term frequencies.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * This method mutates the instance state: it modifies `this.documentFrequency` and `this.blockCount`.
    * For every unique term in the input `text`, its count in `this.documentFrequency` is decremented by 1.
    * If a term's count drops to 0 or less, it is removed entirely from `this.documentFrequency`. (Note: The logic specifically checks if `count <= 1` before deletion/decrementing, ensuring that terms with an initial count of 1 are deleted.)
    * `this.blockCount` is decremented by 1, ensuring it does not fall below zero using `Math.max(0, ...)`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  unregisterBlock(text) {
    const uniqueTermsSet = new Set(tokenize(text));
    for (const term of uniqueTermsSet) {
      const count = this.documentFrequency.get(term) || 0;
      if (count <= 1) {
        this.documentFrequency.delete(term);
      } else {
        this.documentFrequency.set(term, count - 1);
      }
    }
    this.blockCount = Math.max(0, this.blockCount - 1);
  }
```

</details>

#### `cosineSimilarity(queryVec, blockVec)` — method, line 82

* **Purpose:** Calculates the cosine similarity between two term frequency vectors (query and block) to determine how related they are.
* **Inputs:**
    * `queryVec`: Map<string, number> - The vector representing the query terms and their associated scores.
    * `blockVec`: Map<string, number> - The vector representing the document/block terms and their associated scores.
* **Outputs:** Number - A value between 0 and 1 representing the cosine similarity score; a perfect match returns 1, and no relation returns 0.
* **Behavioral Notes:** If either input vector has zero magnitude (i.e., all scores are zero), the function safely returns 0 to prevent division by zero.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  cosineSimilarity(queryVec, blockVec) {
    let dotProduct = 0;
    let queryMag = 0;
    let blockMag = 0;

    for (const [term, score] of queryVec) {
      queryMag += score * score;
      if (blockVec.has(term)) {
        dotProduct += score * blockVec.get(term);
      }
    }

    for (const [, score] of blockVec) {
      blockMag += score * score;
    }

    queryMag = Math.sqrt(queryMag);
    blockMag = Math.sqrt(blockMag);

    if (queryMag === 0 || blockMag === 0) return 0;
    return dotProduct / (queryMag * blockMag);
  }
```

</details>

#### `rankBlocks(query, blocks)` — method, line 111

* **Purpose:** Calculates the relevance score of multiple data blocks against a given query vector using cosine similarity, returning them sorted from most relevant to least relevant.
* **Inputs:**
    * `query`: String - The search query used to generate the comparison vector.
    * `blocks`: Array<Object> - An array of document blocks, where each block must contain at least a unique identifier (`key`) and a pre-computed vector representation (`vector`).
* **Outputs:** Array<Object> - A list of objects, sorted in descending order by score. Each object contains the original block's `key` and its calculated relevance `score`.
* **Behavioral Notes:** If the query cannot be converted into a vector (e.g., if it is empty), an empty array is returned immediately without processing the blocks. The ranking relies on the internal `this.cosineSimilarity` method.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  rankBlocks(query, blocks) {
    const queryVec = this.computeVector(query);
    if (queryVec.size === 0) return [];

    const ranked = blocks.map(block => ({
      key: block.key,
      score: this.cosineSimilarity(queryVec, block.vector)
    }));

    return ranked.sort((a, b) => b.score - a.score);
  }
```

</details>

#### `tokenize(text)` — function, line 165

* **Purpose:** Converts a raw string of text into an array of cleaned, lowercase tokens suitable for indexing or searching.
* **Inputs:** `text` (string): The input text to be tokenized.
* **Outputs:** Array<string>: An array containing the filtered and processed words from the input text.
* **Behavioral Notes:** If the input `text` is falsy (null, undefined, or empty string), an empty array is returned immediately. The function converts all characters to lowercase, removes punctuation/special characters, splits by whitespace, and filters out any resulting tokens that are only one character long.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
function tokenize(text) {
  if (!text) return [];
  return text
    .toLowerCase()
    .replace(/[^\w\s]/g, " ")
    .split(/\s+/)
    .filter(t => t.length > 1);
}
```

</details>

## `src/storage/archive.js`

**204 lines** | 11 functions/classes

The `Archive` class serves as a dedicated cold storage manager for data that results from explicit user actions like trimming or branching within the application. Its primary purpose is to persist and manage historical, compressed node data outside of the main working memory state.

**What it Does:**
The class provides comprehensive CRUD (Create, Read, Update, Delete) functionality for archived blocks, identified by unique keys. It handles the entire lifecycle of stored data:
1.  **Storage:** Accepts arrays of nodes, serializes them, compresses the resulting JSON string, and stores this compressed block along with metadata (key, size, count).
2.  **Retrieval:** Fetches a block by key, decompresses the data, and returns the original array of node objects.
3.  **Persistence:** It is designed to be highly portable, supporting two distinct storage backends: **IndexedDB** in web browsers (for robust persistence) or an **in-memory `Map` fallback** for Node.js environments or testing.
4.  **State Management:** It includes methods to list all stored keys, export the entire archive state, and import data from external sources.

**Role in the Project:**
The `Archive` acts as a reliable, persistent repository for non-active, historical application state. By separating trimmed or branched data into this cold storage layer, it prevents the main working memory from becoming bloated while ensuring that users can later retrieve and restore these archived states when needed.

### Exports

- `Archive` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`, `tests/compaction.test.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `Archive()` — class, line 6

* **Purpose:** Manages persistent storage for compressed archive blocks, utilizing IndexedDB in browsers or falling back to an in-memory Map otherwise.
* **Inputs:** None
* **Outputs:** Promise<void> - Resolves when the underlying database connection is successfully established (or fallback occurs).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The class attempts to initialize using `indexedDB` upon calling `init()`.
    * If IndexedDB fails or is unavailable, it automatically switches to using an in-memory `Map` (`fallbackStore`) and sets `this.useIndexedDB` to `false`, logging a warning.
    * The internal state (`this.db`) holds the active database connection object if successful.
    * The `storeBlock()` method (inferred) is responsible for persisting data using either IndexedDB transactions or direct Map operations, depending on initialization success.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class Archive {
  constructor() {
    this.db = null;
    this.fallbackStore = new Map(); // In-memory fallback for non-browser environments
    this.useIndexedDB = typeof indexedDB !== "undefined";
  }

  /**
   * Initialize the archive store.
   */
  async init() {
    if (!this.useIndexedDB) return;

    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
      const request = indexedDB.open("luminal-memory-archive", 1);

      request.onupgradeneeded = (event) => {
        const db = event.target.result;
        if (!db.objectStoreNames.contains("blocks")) {
          db.createObjectStore("blocks", { keyPath: "key" });
        }
      };

      request.onsuccess = (event) => {
        this.db = event.target.result;
        resolve();
      };

      request.onerror = (event) => {
        console.warn("IndexedDB unavailable, using in-memory fallback");
        this.useIndexedDB = false;
        resolve();
      };
    });
  }

  /**
   * Store a compressed archive block.
   * @param {string} key - archive identifier (e.g., "archive_501_1500")
   * @param {object[]} nodes - array of node objects to archive
  // ... (truncated)
```

* **Behavioral Notes:** If `this.useIndexedDB` is false, the method exits immediately without attempting database initialization. On successful setup, it stores the resulting database object in `this.db`. If IndexedDB fails (e.g., due to browser restrictions), it logs a warning, sets `this.useIndexedDB` to false, and resolves gracefully, indicating that an in-memory fallback will be used instead of persistent storage. It ensures the existence of an object store named "blocks" with "key" as its primary key path during version upgrades.

```javascript
  async init() {
    if (!this.useIndexedDB) return;

    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
      const request = indexedDB.open("luminal-memory-archive", 1);

      request.onupgradeneeded = (event) => {
        const db = event.target.result;
        if (!db.objectStoreNames.contains("blocks")) {
          db.createObjectStore("blocks", { keyPath: "key" });
        }
      };

      request.onsuccess = (event) => {
        this.db = event.target.result;
        resolve();
      };

      request.onerror = (event) => {
        console.warn("IndexedDB unavailable, using in-memory fallback");
        this.useIndexedDB = false;
        resolve();
      };
    });
  }
```

#### `store(key, nodes)` — method, line 47

* **Purpose:** Stores an array of nodes associated with a given key by compressing them and saving the resulting block metadata to persistent storage.
* **Inputs:**
    * `key`: `string` - The unique identifier used for storing the data block.
    * `nodes`: `Array<any>` - An array of node objects that need to be stored.
* **Outputs:** `Promise<void>` or `void` - Returns a Promise that resolves when the storage transaction is complete if IndexedDB is used; otherwise, it returns nothing (void).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The input `nodes` array is first serialized into a JSON string and then compressed.
    * A metadata block containing the key, compressed data, creation timestamp, node count, and size is constructed.
    * If `this.useIndexedDB` is true and `this.db` exists, the method performs an asynchronous write operation to the IndexedDB "blocks" object store within a transaction.
    * Otherwise (fallback), it synchronously sets the block in `this.fallbackStore`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async store(key, nodes) {
    const json = JSON.stringify(nodes);
    const compressed = await compress(json);
    const block = {
      key,
      data: compressed,
      createdAt: Date.now(),
      nodeCount: nodes.length,
      sizeBytes: compressed.byteLength || compressed.length
    };

    if (this.useIndexedDB && this.db) {
      return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
        const tx = this.db.transaction("blocks", "readwrite");
        tx.objectStore("blocks").put(block);
        tx.oncomplete = () => resolve();
        tx.onerror = () => reject(tx.error);
      });
    } else {
      this.fallbackStore.set(key, block);
    }
  }
```

</details>

#### `retrieve(key)` — method, line 75

* **Purpose:** Retrieves and parses archived data associated with a given key from either an IndexedDB store or a fallback storage mechanism.
* **Inputs:**
    * `key` (string): The unique identifier used to locate the stored block of data.
* **Outputs:** Promise<any> - A promise that resolves to the parsed JavaScript object contained within the archived data, or `null` if no block is found for the given key.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If `this.useIndexedDB` is true and `this.db` exists, it uses IndexedDB's transaction system to fetch the record from the "blocks" object store.
    * Otherwise, it falls back to using `this.fallbackStore.get(key)`.
    * The retrieved block data (`block.data`) is assumed to be compressed and must first be passed through an external `decompress` function before being parsed as JSON.
    * If the key does not exist in either storage mechanism, it returns `null`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async retrieve(key) {
    let block;

    if (this.useIndexedDB && this.db) {
      block = await new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
        const tx = this.db.transaction("blocks", "readonly");
        const request = tx.objectStore("blocks").get(key);
        request.onsuccess = () => resolve(request.result);
        request.onerror = () => reject(request.error);
      });
    } else {
      block = this.fallbackStore.get(key);
    }

    if (!block) return null;

    const json = await decompress(block.data);
    return JSON.parse(json);
  }
```

</details>

#### `importAll(blocks)` — method, line 150

* **Purpose:** Imports an array of block objects into storage, utilizing either IndexedDB or a fallback store based on configuration.
* **Inputs:**

* **Outputs:** Promise<void>: Resolves when all blocks have been successfully written to the configured storage mechanism.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If IndexedDB is enabled (`this.useIndexedDB` and `this.db` exist), it performs multiple write transactions on the "blocks" object store.
    * If IndexedDB is not available, it uses `this.fallbackStore.set(block.key, block)` for storage.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async importAll(blocks) {
    for (const block of blocks) {
      if (this.useIndexedDB && this.db) {
        await new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
          const tx = this.db.transaction("blocks", "readwrite");
          tx.objectStore("blocks").put(block);
          tx.oncomplete = () => resolve();
          tx.onerror = () => reject(tx.error);
        });
      } else {
        this.fallbackStore.set(block.key, block);
      }
    }
  }
```

#### `compress(text)` — function, line 171

* **Purpose:** Compresses a given string using GZIP compression if the browser environment supports `CompressionStream`, otherwise it returns the original uncompressed text.
* **Inputs:**
    * `text` (string): The plain text content to be compressed.
* **Outputs:** Promise resolving to either an `ArrayBuffer` (if compressed) or a `string` (if compression fails/is unsupported).
* **Behavioral Notes:** If `CompressionStream` is unavailable, the function acts as a no-op and returns the original string, which may result in larger storage usage. The process involves streaming the encoded text through the compression pipeline before converting it to an ArrayBuffer.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
async function compress(text) {
  if (typeof CompressionStream !== "undefined") {
    const encoder = new TextEncoder();
    const stream = new Blob([encoder.encode(text)])
      .stream()
      .pipeThrough(new CompressionStream("gzip"));
    const blob = await new Response(stream).blob();
    return await blob.arrayBuffer();
  }
  // Fallback: store uncompressed (still works, just bigger)
  return text;
}
```

</details>

#### `decompress(data)` — function, line 189

* **Purpose:** Decompresses binary or gzipped data into a readable text string, handling different input formats based on environment availability.
* **Inputs:**
    * `data` (any): The data to be decompressed. Can be a string (if already decoded), an ArrayBuffer/Uint8Array (for fallback decoding), or a Blob/Stream-compatible object (for streaming decompression).
* **Outputs:** Promise<string>: A promise that resolves to the fully decoded text content.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If `data` is already a string, it is returned immediately without processing.
    * When running in an environment supporting `DecompressionStream`, it treats the input as binary data, streams it through gzip decompression, and returns the resulting text.
    * As a fallback (if `DecompressionStream` is undefined), it assumes the input `data` is a byte array (like Uint8Array) and decodes it directly using `TextDecoder`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
async function decompress(data) {
  if (typeof data === "string") return data;

  if (typeof DecompressionStream !== "undefined") {
    const stream = new Blob([data])
      .stream()
      .pipeThrough(new DecompressionStream("gzip"));
    const blob = await new Response(stream).blob();
    return await blob.text();
  }

  // Fallback: try to decode as raw text
  const decoder = new TextDecoder();
  return decoder.decode(data);
}
```

</details>

</details>

## `src/storage/memory.js`

**96 lines** | 6 functions/classes

The `MemoryManager` class is responsible for monitoring and reporting the real-time memory consumption of a persistent node chain within the application's memory space.

**Purpose:** Its primary purpose is to provide visibility into how much Random Access Memory (RAM) the system is utilizing relative to a defined operational limit. It ensures that the application can proactively manage resource constraints, especially since the entire data structure resides in volatile memory.

**What it Does:**
1. **Tracks Usage:** Calculates and exposes current memory usage in megabytes (`usageMB`) and the utilization ratio (percentage of the configured limit).
2. **Checks Limits:** The `check()` method evaluates if the utilization exceeds predefined warning or critical thresholds, emitting a specific event if limits are approached or breached.
3. **Reports Status:** Provides a comprehensive status report detailing total nodes, total tokens, memory usage, and current utilization percentage.
4. **Event Handling:** Implements an internal event system (`on`, `_emit`) to notify external parts of the project whenever critical memory warnings occur.

**Role in the Project:** It acts as a crucial resource monitoring layer. By providing continuous feedback on memory pressure, it allows other components of the project to take corrective actions (such as triggering data trimming or branching) before the system runs out of available RAM.

### Exports

- `MemoryManager` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `MemoryManager()` — class, line 6

* **Purpose:** Manages and monitors memory usage statistics, providing current utilization levels and checking if limits or warnings thresholds have been exceeded.
* **Inputs:**
    * `chain` (Object): An object containing a `memorySizeBytes` property representing the total allocated memory.
    * `config` (Object): Configuration settings including `memoryLimitMB` (the hard limit) and `warnThreshold` (the utilization ratio that triggers a warning).
* **Outputs:**
    * `usageMB`: Number representing the current memory usage in megabytes.
    * `utilization`: Number representing the current utilization ratio (0 to 1).
    * `check()`: Object containing boolean flags (`ok`, `warning`) and numerical values for current usage, utilization, and limit details.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The class calculates memory metrics based on external state provided by `this.chain` and `this.config`.
    * The `check()` method emits a `"memory-warning"` event if the calculated utilization meets or exceeds the configured `warnThreshold`.
    * It determines if memory is "blocked" (fully utilized, $\ge 1.0$) and whether a warning should be issued based on configuration thresholds.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class MemoryManager {
  constructor(chain, config) {
    this.chain = chain;
    this.config = config;
    this.listeners = new Map();
  }

  /**
   * Current memory usage in MB.
   * @returns {number}
   */
  get usageMB() {
    return this.chain.memorySizeBytes / (1024 * 1024);
  }

  /**
   * Current utilization ratio (0 to 1).
   * @returns {number}
   */
  get utilization() {
    return this.usageMB / this.config.memoryLimitMB;
  }

  /**
   * Check if memory is within limits. Emits warning if threshold exceeded.
   * @returns {{ ok: boolean, usageMB: number, utilization: number, warning: boolean }}
   */
  check() {
    const usageMB = this.usageMB;
    const utilization = this.utilization;
    const warning = utilization >= this.config.warnThreshold;
    const blocked = utilization >= 1.0;

    if (warning) {
      this._emit("memory-warning", {
        usageMB,
        utilization,
        limitMB: this.config.memoryLimitMB,
        blocked
      });
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `check()` — method, line 33

* **Purpose:** Checks the current memory usage against configured thresholds and emits a warning event if utilization is high.
* **Inputs:** None (Relies on `this` context containing `usageMB`, `utilization`, and `config`).
* **Outputs:** Object `{ ok: boolean, usageMB: number, utilization: number, warning: boolean }` representing the current memory status.
* **Behavioral Notes:** Emits a `"memory-warning"` event if the calculated utilization meets or exceeds `this.config.warnThreshold`. The method returns `ok: false` (meaning blocked) if utilization is 1.0 or greater.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  check() {
    const usageMB = this.usageMB;
    const utilization = this.utilization;
    const warning = utilization >= this.config.warnThreshold;
    const blocked = utilization >= 1.0;

    if (warning) {
      this._emit("memory-warning", {
        usageMB,
        utilization,
        limitMB: this.config.memoryLimitMB,
        blocked
      });
    }

    return { ok: !blocked, usageMB, utilization, warning };
  }
```

</details>

#### `status()` — method, line 55

* **Purpose:** Calculates and returns a comprehensive object detailing the current operational status and resource usage of the memory storage system.
* **Inputs:** None (relies on `this` context: `this.chain`, `this.usageMB`, `this.utilization`, `this.config`).
* **Outputs:** Object containing various metrics about the system's state, including total nodes, token count, memory usage, utilization percentage, configured limits, and a warning flag.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Calculates `totalTokens` by summing the `tokenCount` property of every node within `this.chain`.
    * Rounds `memoryUsageMB` to two decimal places.
    * Rounds `utilizationPercent` to the nearest whole number.
    * Sets the `warning` flag to `true` if the current utilization (`this.utilization`) meets or exceeds the configured warning threshold (`this.config.warnThreshold`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  status() {
    // Calculate total tokens across all nodes in the chain
    let totalTokens = 0;
    for (const node of this.chain.all()) {
      totalTokens += node.tokenCount || 0;
    }

    return {
      totalNodes: this.chain.length,
      totalTokens: totalTokens,
      memoryUsageMB: Math.round(this.usageMB * 100) / 100,
      utilizationPercent: Math.round(this.utilization * 100),
      limitMB: this.config.memoryLimitMB,
      warning: this.utilization >= this.config.warnThreshold
    };
  }
```

</details>

#### `on(event, callback)` — method, line 77

* **Purpose:** Registers a callback function to be executed when a specific named event occurs.
* **Inputs:**
    * event (string): The name of the event to listen for.
    * callback (Function): The function that will be executed when the event is emitted.
* **Behavioral Notes:** If no listeners exist for the given `event`, an empty array is initialized in internal storage before adding the new callback. This method mutates the internal state (`this.listeners`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  on(event, callback) {
    if (!this.listeners.has(event)) {
      this.listeners.set(event, []);
    }
    this.listeners.get(event).push(callback);
  }
```

</details>

#### `_emit(event, data)` — method, line 89

* **Purpose:** Triggers all registered callbacks associated with a specific event, passing provided data to each listener.
* **Inputs:**
    * `event` (string): The name of the event to emit.
    * `data` (any): The payload data passed to every listening callback.
* **Outputs:** None
* **Behavioral Notes:** If any individual listener throws an error, that error is caught and swallowed, allowing subsequent listeners for the same event to execute normally.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  _emit(event, data) {
    const callbacks = this.listeners.get(event) || [];
    for (const cb of callbacks) {
      try { cb(data); } catch (e) { /* swallow listener errors */ }
    }
  }
```

</details>

## `src/tools/base.js`

**93 lines** | 5 functions/classes

This source file defines the abstract base class `Tool`, which serves as the foundational structure for all capabilities or extensions that an AI system can utilize.

**Purpose:**
The primary purpose of the `Tool` class is to standardize how external functions are integrated into a retrieval pipeline, allowing the AI model to invoke specific actions (tools) when necessary based on a user's query.

**What it Does:**
1.  **Initialization:** It requires defining a unique name, a natural language description (used for search), parameter schema, and an asynchronous execution function (`execute`).
2.  **Discovery Management:** It manages how the tool is surfaced:
    *   `bm25`: The tool is discovered by relevance matching against its description during retrieval.
    *   `llm`: The tool is always presented to the Large Language Model (LLM) in its function-calling schema, allowing the model to decide if it should use it regardless of search results.
3.  **Interface Generation:** It provides methods (`toNode()` and `toSchema()`) to convert the internal tool definition into formats required by the system: a searchable node for indexing (BM25) and a structured function-calling schema for the LLM prompt.
4.  **Execution:** The `run()` method handles the execution of the underlying logic, managing parameters, context passing, and robustly handling success or failure states.

**Role in the Project:**
The `Tool` class acts as the central abstraction layer between the AI's reasoning process (the LLM) and external business logic. It ensures that all capabilities are uniformly structured, discoverable, callable, and executable within the overall retrieval and response generation pipeline.

### Exports

* **Inputs:**

    * `context`: Object (Optional) - An optional context object that can be passed alongside the parameters during execution.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async run(params, context = {}) {
    console.log(`[Tool:${this.name}] Executing with params:`, JSON.stringify(params).slice(0, 200));
    const startTime = Date.now();

    try {
      const result = await this.execute(params, context);
      const elapsed = Date.now() - startTime;
      console.log(`[Tool:${this.name}] Success in ${elapsed}ms`);
      return { success: true, result, elapsed };
    } catch (err) {
      const elapsed = Date.now() - startTime;
      console.error(`[Tool:${this.name}] Failed in ${elapsed}ms:`, err.message);
      return { success: false, result: null, error: err.message, elapsed };
    }
  }
```

</details>

## `src/tools/registry.js`

**129 lines** | 8 functions/classes

The `ToolRegistry` class serves as the central management hub for all external tools (functions or capabilities) available to the system.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to discover, store, index, and facilitate the execution of these tools, allowing the system to intelligently determine which tool is relevant to a given user query.

**Functionality:**
1. **Registration:** Tools are registered with the registry. When registering, the tool's description is indexed using BM25 for search-based discovery (`bm25`).
2. **Query Retrieval (Search):** It uses the BM25 algorithm to search through the descriptions of registered tools based on a natural language query. It returns a list of highly relevant tools whose schemas can then be injected into the prompt for the Language Model (LLM).
3. **LLM Discovery:** It maintains a separate mechanism to collect all tools marked for "llm" discovery, ensuring these capabilities are always available to the model regardless of search relevance.
4. **Execution:** It provides an asynchronous method to execute any registered tool by name and parameters, handling potential errors or missing tools.

**Role in the Project:**
The `ToolRegistry` acts as the core dispatcher layer. It abstracts away the complexity of managing multiple external functions, providing a unified interface for both search-based (retrieval) and model-native (LLM) tool selection, ensuring that the LLM receives only the most relevant tools and their schemas at the appropriate time.

### Exports

- `ToolRegistry` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/search/bm25.js`

**Imported by:** `src/index.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `ToolRegistry()` — class, line 10

* **Purpose:** Manages the collection of available tools, providing mechanisms to register them and retrieve relevant tools based on natural language queries using BM25 scoring.
* **Inputs:**
    * `config` (object): Configuration object used during initialization.
        * `bm25` (object, optional): Parameters for the BM25 search algorithm (e.g., `{ k1: 1.2, b: 0.4 }`). Defaults to `{ k1: 1.2, b: 0.4 }`.
        * `toolMatchThreshold` (number, optional): The minimum score required for a tool match to be considered relevant. Defaults to `0.3`.
    * `tool` (object): An instance of a Tool class with a defined name and discovery method.
    * `query` (string): The natural language query used to search for relevant tools.
* **Outputs:**
    * `ToolRegistry`: A class instance managing the tool collection.
    * Array of `{ tool: import('./base.js').Tool, score: number }[]`: An array containing up to 5 tools that are most relevant to the given query, ranked by their BM25 score.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * Tools are stored internally in a `Map` keyed by their name.
    * The registry initializes an internal BM25 index using provided configuration.
    * When registering a tool, if its discovery method is not `'llm'`, the tool's description is indexed into the BM25 system.
    * If a tool with the same name is registered multiple times, it will overwrite the previous entry and issue a warning.
    * The `retrieve` method only searches tools that were explicitly indexed (i.e., those not marked as `'llm'` discovery).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class ToolRegistry {
  constructor(config = {}) {
    this.tools = new Map();         // name → Tool instance
    this.bm25 = new BM25(config.bm25 || { k1: 1.2, b: 0.4 });
    this.matchThreshold = config.toolMatchThreshold || 0.3;

    console.log("[ToolRegistry] Initialized | threshold:", this.matchThreshold);
  }

  /**
   * Register a tool. Indexes its description for BM25 retrieval
   * unless it uses 'llm' discovery (always available, model decides).
   * @param {import('./base.js').Tool} tool
   */
  register(tool) {
    if (this.tools.has(tool.name)) {
      console.warn(`[ToolRegistry] Tool "${tool.name}" already registered, overwriting`);
    }

    this.tools.set(tool.name, tool);

    // Only index in BM25 if discovery is 'bm25' (default)
    if (tool.discovery !== 'llm') {
      this.bm25.add(tool.toNode());
    }
    tool._registered = true;

    console.log(`[ToolRegistry] Registered "${tool.name}" (discovery: ${tool.discovery}) | total tools: ${this.tools.size}`);
  }

  /**
   * Retrieve tools relevant to a query via BM25.
   * Only returns tools with discovery:'bm25'. LLM-discovery tools are excluded.
   * @param {string} query
   * @returns {{ tool: import('./base.js').Tool, score: number }[]}
   */
  retrieve(query) {
    if (this.tools.size === 0) return [];

    const results = this.bm25.search(query, 5);
  // ... (truncated)
```

#### `retrieve(query)` — method, line 46

* **Purpose:** Searches a registered collection of tools using BM25 scoring and returns a list of matching tools that exceed a defined match threshold.
* **Inputs:**
    * `query`: string - The search query used to find relevant tools.
* **Outputs:** Array<Object> - An array of objects, where each object contains the matched tool instance (`tool`) and its normalized relevance score (`score`).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * If the internal tool registry is empty, it immediately returns an empty array.
    * It uses BM25 scoring to find potential matches based on the `query`.
    * The raw scores are normalized linearly (divided by the highest score found) to fit within a 0-1 range.
    * Tools with a normalized score below `this.matchThreshold` are filtered out.
    * Only tools that have been explicitly registered in `this.tools` and whose `discovery` property is *not* 'llm' are included in the results.
    * The method logs detailed messages to the console regarding successful matches, no matches found (due to threshold), or general search failures.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  retrieve(query) {
    if (this.tools.size === 0) return [];

    const results = this.bm25.search(query, 5);
    if (results.length === 0) {
      console.log(`[ToolRegistry] No tools matched for: "${query.slice(0, 50)}"`);
      return [];
    }

    // Normalize scores to 0-1 range (linear)
    const maxScore = results[0].score;
    if (maxScore === 0) return [];

    const matched = [];

    for (const r of results) {
      const normalized = r.score / maxScore; // 0-1 linear scale
      if (normalized < this.matchThreshold) continue;

      const toolName = r.nodeId;
      const tool = this.tools.get(toolName);

      if (tool && tool.discovery !== 'llm') {
        matched.push({ tool, score: normalized });
        console.log(`[ToolRegistry] Match: "${tool.name}" score=${normalized.toFixed(3)} (raw: ${r.score.toFixed(3)}) for query: "${query.slice(0, 50)}"`);
      }
    }

    if (matched.length === 0) {
      console.log(`[ToolRegistry] No tools above threshold ${this.matchThreshold} for: "${query.slice(0, 50)}" (best raw: ${maxScore.toFixed(3)})`);
    }

    return matched;
  }
```

</details>

```javascript
  getLLMTools() {

    for (const tool of this.tools.values()) {
      if (tool.discovery === 'llm') {
        llmTools.push(tool);
      }
    }
    return llmTools;
```

```javascript
  async execute(name, params, context = {}) {

      console.error(`[ToolRegistry] Tool not found: "${name}"`);
      return { success: false, result: null, error: `Tool "${name}" not registered` };
    }
    return tool.run(params, context);
  }
```

## `src/transport/llm.js`

**477 lines** | 14 functions/classes

The `LLMTransport` class serves as a model-agnostic abstraction layer for communicating with various Large Language Model (LLM) APIs. Its primary purpose is to standardize the process of sending prompts and receiving completions from diverse endpoints—including OpenAI, Ollama, Llama.cpp, and custom services—without requiring changes in the calling code.

**What it Does:**
1.  **API Routing:** It intelligently routes requests based on configuration (`apiFormat`), supporting dedicated logic for specific providers (e.g., `ollama`) or falling back to a standard OpenAI-compatible format.
2.  **Completion Handling:** It provides methods for both synchronous completion (`complete`) and advanced streaming responses (`_openaiStream`).
3.  **Data Normalization:** The class handles the complexities of different model outputs by:
    *   Extracting structured data like token usage (prompt, completion, total).
    *   Cleaning up proprietary formatting tags (e.g., stripping inline thinking blocks) to return clean text.
    *   Supporting advanced features like capturing separate "reasoning" or "thinking" content during the response stream.

**Role in the Project:**
The `LLMTransport` acts as a crucial service layer, decoupling the core application logic from the specifics of external LLM APIs. This makes the project highly flexible and maintainable, allowing developers to switch between different underlying models or services simply by updating configuration parameters rather than rewriting API interaction code.

### Exports

- `LLMTransport` (class)

### Dependencies

**Imported by:** `src/extensions/docgen.js`, `src/index.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `LLMTransport()` — class, line 6

* **Purpose:** Provides a unified interface for sending completion requests to various Large Language Models (LLMs) via different API formats (OpenAI-compatible, Ollama, or custom).
* **Inputs:**
    * `config` (object): Configuration object required upon instantiation, containing details like `endpoint`, `completionPath`, `model`, and `apiFormat`.
    * `messages` (array of { role: string, content: string }): The conversation history or prompt messages to send to the LLM.
* **Outputs:**
    * `Promise<{ text: string, usage: { promptTokens: number, completionTokens: number, totalTokens: number }|null }>`: A promise that resolves with an object containing the generated text and token usage statistics from the model.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The `complete` method acts as a router, determining which internal API call (`_openaiComplete`, `_ollamaComplete`, or `_customComplete`) to use based on `this.config.apiFormat`.
    * If `apiFormat` is "custom", it uses the dedicated custom completion logic.
    * If `apiFormat` is "ollama", it routes to the Ollama-specific endpoint.
    * Otherwise, it defaults to the OpenAI-compatible API structure.
    * The class relies heavily on internal state (`this.config`) to construct the correct request body and URL for the target LLM provider.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
export class LLMTransport {
  constructor(config) {
    this.config = config;
  }

  /**
   * Send a completion request to the LLM.
   * @param {object[]} messages - array of { role, content } message objects
   * @returns {{ text: string, usage: { promptTokens: number, completionTokens: number, totalTokens: number }|null }}
   */
  async complete(messages) {
    const { apiFormat } = this.config;

    if (apiFormat === "custom" && this.config.formatRequest) {
      return this._customComplete(messages);
    }

    if (apiFormat === "ollama") {
      return this._ollamaComplete(messages);
    }

    // Default: OpenAI-compatible
    return this._openaiComplete(messages);
  }

  /**
   * OpenAI-compatible completion (llama.cpp, LM Studio, vLLM, OpenAI, etc.)
   */
  async _openaiComplete(messages) {
    const url = `${this.config.endpoint}${this.config.completionPath}`;
    const body = {
      model: this.config.model,
      messages,
      stream: false,
      ...this._samplingParams()
    };

    // Enable thinking/reasoning if configured
    if (this.config.thinking) {
      body.thinking = true;
  // ... (truncated)
```

#### `findBoundary(text, markers)` — function, line 130

* **Purpose:** Finds the first occurrence of any specified marker within a given text string and returns its location details.
* **Inputs:**
    * `text`: The string in which to search for markers. (string)
    * `markers`: An array of strings representing potential boundary markers. (Array<string>)
* **Outputs:** Returns an object `{ idx: number, marker: string, len: number }` containing the index, the matched marker, and its length if a match is found; otherwise, returns `null`.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function returns the result based on the order of markers provided in the input array (`markers`), not necessarily the marker that appears earliest in the text.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
    function findBoundary(text, markers) {
      for (const m of markers) {
        const idx = text.indexOf(m);
        if (idx !== -1) return { idx, marker: m, len: m.length };
      }
      return null;
    }
```

</details>

#### `flushAccumulator()` — function, line 138

* **Purpose:** Processes the accumulated raw text (`rawAccum`) by detecting structural boundaries (like thinking markers) and flushing content into appropriate internal buffers (`fullText` or `thinkText`) while triggering callbacks.
* **Inputs:**
    * `rawAccum`: String array/buffer containing unprocessed raw text segments.
    * `mode`: String ('pending', 'content', or 'thinking') indicating the current processing state.
    * `callbacks`: Object containing event handlers (`onToken`, `onThink`).
    * `fullText` (State): Accumulator for final content output.
    * `thinkText` (State): Accumulator for thinking process text.
* **Outputs:** None (It modifies internal state variables: `rawAccum`, `mode`, `fullText`, and `thinkText`).
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function operates in a loop, consuming `rawAccum` until it is empty or no further boundaries are found.
    * **Mode Transitions:** It manages transitions between modes by detecting specific markers (`THINK_START` and `THINK_END`).
        * If in 'pending'/'content' mode, it searches for `THINK_START`. Content before the marker is flushed as content; state switches to 'thinking'.
        * If in 'thinking' mode, it searches for `THINK_END`. Thinking text before the marker is flushed as thinking; state switches to 'content'.
    * **Flushing:** If a boundary marker is not found (e.g., no `THINK_START` when expecting content), the entire remaining `rawAccum` is treated as raw content and flushed immediately, resetting `rawAccum`.
    * **Side Effects:** It updates `fullText` and `thinkText`, and calls `callbacks.onToken` or `callbacks.onThink` upon flushing segments of text.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
    function flushAccumulator() {
      // Process rawAccum: detect boundaries, route sections
      while (rawAccum.length > 0) {
        if (mode === 'pending' || mode === 'content') {
          // Look for think-start
          const start = findBoundary(rawAccum, THINK_START);
          if (start) {
            // Everything before the marker is content
            const before = rawAccum.slice(0, start.idx);
            if (before) {
              fullText += before;
              if (callbacks.onToken) callbacks.onToken(before);
            }
            rawAccum = rawAccum.slice(start.idx + start.len);
            mode = 'thinking';
            continue;
          }
          // No boundary found — flush all as content
          fullText += rawAccum;
          if (callbacks.onToken) callbacks.onToken(rawAccum);
          rawAccum = '';
        } else if (mode === 'thinking') {
          // Look for think-end
          const end = findBoundary(rawAccum, THINK_END);
          if (end) {
            // Everything before the marker is thinking
            const before = rawAccum.slice(0, end.idx);
            if (before) {
              thinkText += before;
              if (callbacks.onThink) callbacks.onThink(before);
            }
            rawAccum = rawAccum.slice(end.idx + end.len);
            mode = 'content';
            continue;
          }
          // No end found yet — flush all as thinking (more coming)
          thinkText += rawAccum;
          if (callbacks.onThink) callbacks.onThink(rawAccum);
          rawAccum = '';
        }
  // ... (truncated)
```

</details>

#### `stream(messages, callbacks, signal)` — method, line 244

* **Purpose:** Streams a conversation history (`messages`) to an LLM, routing the request internally based on the configured API format ("openai" or "ollama").
* **Inputs:**
    * `messages` (Array): The list of messages representing the conversation context.
    * `callbacks` (Object): Functions used to handle incoming streamed chunks and events from the LLM.
    * `signal` (AbortSignal): A signal object used to cancel the streaming operation.
* **Outputs:** Promise<void>: Resolves when the streaming process completes or rejects if an error occurs during communication with the LLM API.
* **Behavioral Notes:** The function implements conditional logic based on `this.config.apiFormat`. If the format is "openai" or undefined, it calls `_openaiStream`; if it's "ollama", it calls `_ollamaStream`; otherwise, it falls back to a non-streaming completion using `this.complete(messages)`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async stream(messages, callbacks, signal) {
    const { apiFormat } = this.config;
    if (apiFormat === "openai" || !apiFormat) {
      return this._openaiStream(messages, callbacks, signal);
    }
    if (apiFormat === "ollama") {
      return this._ollamaStream(messages, callbacks, signal);
    }
    // Fallback: non-streaming
    return this.complete(messages);
  }
```

</details>

#### `_ollamaComplete(messages)` — method, line 259

* **Purpose:** Sends a request to an Ollama endpoint to generate text completion based on provided messages and configuration parameters.
* **Inputs:**
    * `messages` (Array<Object>): The conversation history or prompt structure sent to the LLM API.
* **Outputs:** Object containing `{ text: string, usage: { promptTokens: number, completionTokens: number, totalTokens: number } | null }`, representing the generated content and token usage statistics.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The method relies on `this.config` for the API endpoint (`endpoint`) and the model name (`model`).
    * It uses an internal helper method, `this._samplingParamsOllama()`, to include sampling parameters in the request body.
    * If `this.config.thinking` is true, it adds a special `think: true` flag to the API call.
    * The function handles network errors by throwing an error if the HTTP response status is not OK.
    * It calculates and returns token usage statistics based on `prompt_eval_count` and `eval_count` provided in the Ollama response body.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async _ollamaComplete(messages) {
    const url = `${this.config.endpoint}/api/chat`;
    const body = {
      model: this.config.model,
      messages,
      stream: false,
      options: this._samplingParamsOllama()
    };

    if (this.config.thinking) {
      body.think = true;
    }

    const response = await fetch(url, {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
      body: JSON.stringify(body)
    });

    if (!response.ok) {
      throw new Error(`Ollama request failed: ${response.status} ${response.statusText}`);
    }

    const data = await response.json();
    const text = data.message?.content || "";

    // Ollama uses prompt_eval_count / eval_count
    const usage = (data.prompt_eval_count != null || data.eval_count != null) ? {
      promptTokens: data.prompt_eval_count || 0,
      completionTokens: data.eval_count || 0,
      totalTokens: (data.prompt_eval_count || 0) + (data.eval_count || 0)
    } : null;

    return { text, usage };
  }
```

</details>

#### `_ollamaStream(messages, callbacks = {}, signal = null)` — method, line 302

* **Purpose:** Streams chat completion responses from a local Ollama instance using the streaming API endpoint.

    * `callbacks` (Object): Optional object containing callback functions for handling streamed events (e.g., usage updates).
    * `signal` (AbortSignal): An optional signal used to abort the fetch request if necessary.
* **Outputs:** Async Generator/Stream that yields chunks of text representing the model's response as it is generated.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function constructs a POST request body including the specified model, messages, and sampling parameters (via `this._samplingParamsOllama()`).
    * If `this.config.thinking` is true, an additional `think: true` flag is added to the payload.
    * It handles streaming by reading chunks from the response body, decoding them, and managing a buffer to correctly split lines across chunk boundaries.
    * Throws an error if the HTTP response status code is not successful (`!response.ok`).

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async _ollamaStream(messages, callbacks = {}, signal = null) {
    const url = `${this.config.endpoint}/api/chat`;
    const body = {
      model: this.config.model,
      messages,
      stream: true,
      options: this._samplingParamsOllama()
    };

    if (this.config.thinking) {
      body.think = true;
    }

    const fetchOpts = {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
      body: JSON.stringify(body)
    };
    if (signal) fetchOpts.signal = signal;

    const response = await fetch(url, fetchOpts);

    if (!response.ok) {
      throw new Error(`Ollama stream failed: ${response.status} ${response.statusText}`);
    }

    const reader = response.body.getReader();
    const decoder = new TextDecoder();
    let fullText = '';
    let buffer = '';
    let usage = null;

    while (true) {
      const { done, value } = await reader.read();
      if (done) break;

      buffer += decoder.decode(value, { stream: true });
      const lines = buffer.split('\n');
      buffer = lines.pop() || '';

  // ... (truncated)
```
