#### `_customComplete(messages)` — method, line 380

* **Purpose:** Sends a custom request to an LLM endpoint using the provided messages and processes the resulting text response.
* **Inputs:**
    * `messages` (Array): The conversation history or prompt array to be sent to the language model.
* **Outputs:** Object containing `{ text: string, usage: null }`, representing the extracted completion text and a placeholder for token usage.
* **Behavioral Notes:** Throws an error if the HTTP response status is not successful (`response.ok` is false). It relies on `this.config.formatRequest` to prepare the request details (URL, headers, body) and `this.config.parseResponse` to extract the final text from the raw JSON data.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async _customComplete(messages) {
    const { url, headers, body } = this.config.formatRequest(messages, this.config);

    const response = await fetch(url, {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", ...headers },
      body: JSON.stringify(body)
    });

    if (!response.ok) {
      throw new Error(`Custom LLM request failed: ${response.status} ${response.statusText}`);
    }

    const data = await response.json();
    const text = this.config.parseResponse(data);
    return { text, usage: null };
  }
```

</details>

#### `generateSummary(nodes)` — method, line 403

* **Purpose:** Generates a structured summary of a conversation section by sending the dialogue nodes to an LLM and parsing the resulting JSON output.
* **Inputs:**
    * `nodes` (Array<Object>): An array of objects representing conversational turns, where each object must contain at least `role` (string) and `content` (string).
* **Outputs:** Object: A structured summary containing `startTopic` (string), `keyDecisions` (Array<string>), and `openThreads` (Array<string>).
* **Behavioral Notes:** If the LLM response cannot be parsed as valid JSON, the method falls back to returning a default structure where `startTopic` is truncated from the raw response text, and arrays are empty. The function relies on an internal `this.complete(messages)` method to communicate with the language model API.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  async generateSummary(nodes) {
    const conversationText = nodes
      .map(n => `[${n.role}]: ${n.content}`)
      .join("\n");

    const messages = [
      {
        role: "system",
        content: `You are summarizing a conversation section for future reference. 
Output ONLY valid JSON with this exact structure:
{
  "startTopic": "what was being discussed at the start",
  "keyDecisions": ["decision 1", "decision 2"],
  "openThreads": ["unresolved topic 1"]
}
Be concise. Max 2 sentences per field. Max 5 items per array.`
      },
      {
        role: "user",
        content: `Summarize this conversation section:\n\n${conversationText}`
      }
    ];

    const { text: response } = await this.complete(messages);

    try {
      return JSON.parse(response);
    } catch {
      // Fallback if LLM doesn't return valid JSON
      return {
        startTopic: response.slice(0, 100),
        keyDecisions: [],
        openThreads: []
      };
    }
  }
```

</details>

#### `_samplingParams()` — method, line 445

* **Purpose:** Collects and returns an object containing various optional sampling parameters configured for LLM generation, filtering out undefined or invalid values.
* **Inputs:** None (Reads configuration from `this.config`)
* **Outputs:** Object representing the active sampling parameters (e.g., `{ temperature: 0.7, top_p: 1.0 }`).
* **Behavioral Notes:** The method selectively includes parameters only if they are defined in `this.config`. Specifically, it ignores a `seed` value of `-1`.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  _samplingParams() {
    const params = {};
    const c = this.config;
    if (c.temperature !== undefined) params.temperature = c.temperature;
    if (c.top_p !== undefined) params.top_p = c.top_p;
    if (c.top_k !== undefined) params.top_k = c.top_k;
    if (c.min_p !== undefined) params.min_p = c.min_p;
    if (c.repeat_penalty !== undefined) params.repeat_penalty = c.repeat_penalty;
    if (c.presence_penalty !== undefined) params.presence_penalty = c.presence_penalty;
    if (c.frequency_penalty !== undefined) params.frequency_penalty = c.frequency_penalty;
    if (c.max_tokens !== undefined) params.max_tokens = c.max_tokens;
    if (c.seed !== undefined && c.seed !== -1) params.seed = c.seed;
    return params;
  }
```

</details>

#### `_samplingParamsOllama()` — method, line 464

* **Purpose:** Constructs an object containing sampling parameters, translating internal configuration settings into the specific format expected by Ollama API calls.
* **Inputs:** None (Relies on `this.config` for all parameter values)
* **Outputs:** Object representing the structured sampling options for Ollama
* **Behavioral Notes:** Parameters are only included in the returned object if they are explicitly defined in `this.config`. Note that `max_tokens` from the configuration is mapped to `num_predict` in the output object. The `seed` parameter is only included if it is defined and not equal to -1.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  _samplingParamsOllama() {
    const opts = {};
    const c = this.config;
    if (c.temperature !== undefined) opts.temperature = c.temperature;
    if (c.top_p !== undefined) opts.top_p = c.top_p;
    if (c.top_k !== undefined) opts.top_k = c.top_k;
    if (c.min_p !== undefined) opts.min_p = c.min_p;
    if (c.repeat_penalty !== undefined) opts.repeat_penalty = c.repeat_penalty;
    if (c.max_tokens !== undefined) opts.num_predict = c.max_tokens;
    if (c.seed !== undefined && c.seed !== -1) opts.seed = c.seed;
    return opts;
  }
```

</details>

</details>

This source file is a comprehensive unit test suite for the `BloomFilter` class.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to validate that the `BloomFilter` correctly implements its core functionality—efficiently testing for the presence of items while minimizing false positives.

**What it Does:**
The tests cover all major functionalities of the Bloom Filter, including:
*   Adding and verifying single terms.
*   Testing for non-existent terms (handling potential false positives).
*   Implementing item removal/deletion.
*   Processing and adding multiple terms from a full text block.
*   Querying multi-word phrases against the filter.
*   Clearing all stored data.
*   Serializing the filter's state (`export`) and restoring it to a new instance (`import`).

**Role in the Project:**
This file serves as critical quality assurance code, ensuring that the `BloomFilter` component is robust, reliable, and behaves correctly across various operational scenarios (addition, deletion, querying, and persistence).

## `tests/compaction.test.js`

**79 lines** | 1 functions/classes

This source file is a comprehensive **unit test suite** for the `Compaction` module within a knowledge graph or message history management system.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to validate the core functionality of data lifecycle management—specifically, how old or unused segments of data are efficiently removed, archived, and later restored without losing associated metadata or structural integrity.

**What it Does:**
The tests simulate various scenarios involving compaction:
1.  **Archiving/Trimming:** It verifies that when a range of nodes is "trimmed" (archived), the main data structure (`Chain`) correctly shrinks, and auxiliary indexes (like `BloomFilter` and `BM25`) are updated to reflect the removal while retaining necessary information about the archived content.
2.  **Restoration:** It confirms that previously archived data can be successfully retrieved and reintegrated into the active data set.
3.  **Metadata Tracking:** It validates that the system correctly tracks metadata (vectors) associated with each archived block, enabling targeted retrieval.
4.  **Persistence:** It ensures that the entire state of the compaction process—including markers for archived blocks—can be reliably exported and imported.

**Role in the Project:**
This file acts as a critical quality assurance layer. By rigorously testing `Compaction`, it guarantees that the system can manage large volumes of data efficiently by performing garbage collection (archiving) while maintaining transactional consistency across all dependent components (search indexes, storage layers, and the main data chain).

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/core/chain.js`, `src/search/bm25.js`, `src/search/bloom.js`, `src/search/tfidf.js`, `src/storage/archive.js`, `src/core/compaction.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `setup()` — function, line 11

* **Purpose:** Initializes a comprehensive test environment for compaction logic by setting up interconnected data structures and populating them with sample nodes.
* **Inputs:** None (It is a setup hook).
* **Outputs:** An object containing initialized instances of `chain`, `bm25`, `bloom`, `tfidf`, `archive`, and the primary `compaction` utility, along with pre-populated data.
* **Behavioral Notes:** This function performs side effects by creating and populating multiple in-memory data structures (`Chain`, `BM25`) to simulate a working system state before running compaction tests.

```javascript
  function setup() {
    const chain = new Chain();
    const bm25 = new BM25({ k1: 1.2, b: 0.4 });
    const bloom = new BloomFilter({ expectedItems: 1000, falsePositiveRate: 0.01 });
    const tfidf = new TfIdf();
    const archive = new Archive(); // uses in-memory fallback (no IndexedDB in Node)
    const config = { archiveBlockSize: 1000 };
    const compaction = new Compaction(chain, bm25, bloom, tfidf, archive, config);

    // Add some nodes
    for (let i = 0; i < 20; i++) {
      const node = chain.append(i % 2 === 0 ? "user" : "assistant", `message number ${i} about topic ${i % 3}`);
      bm25.add(node);
    }

    return { chain, bm25, bloom, tfidf, archive, compaction };
  }
```

This file is a comprehensive **unit test suite** designed to validate the functionality of a complex conversational memory system. Its primary purpose is to ensure that various components—which together manage context from long chat histories—work correctly and interact reliably.

**What it does:**
The tests simulate processing a lengthy, multi-turn conversation (35 messages) and verify how different memory management techniques handle the resulting data structure:

1.  **Data Structuring:** It confirms that raw messages are correctly grouped into "turn nodes" within a `Chain`.
2.  **Context Windowing:** It verifies that the system accurately selects only the most recent turns, respecting a defined window size (e.g., 10 nodes).
3.  **Retrieval Augmentation:** It tests the **BM25** search mechanism to ensure that relevant information (like specific facts or code snippets) can be retrieved from deep within the conversation history, even if they fall outside the immediate context window.
4.  **Memory Management:** It validates specialized buffers (`SlidingBuffer` and `RecallBuffer`) to ensure that the total token count remains under specified limits while preserving critical conversational context (recent turns and relevant memories).
5.  **Orchestration:** Finally, it tests the `ConversationManager`, which acts as the central coordinator, demonstrating its ability to combine system prompts, recent conversation history, and retrieved external memories into a single, coherent prompt structure that respects all token budgets.

**Role in the Project:**
This test suite serves as critical quality assurance for the core conversational AI logic. By rigorously testing context windowing, retrieval mechanisms, and token budgeting, it ensures that the system can maintain coherence and recall specific details even when processing extremely long user interactions.

### Dependencies

**Imports from:** `src/core/chain.js`, `src/core/window.js`, `src/search/bm25.js`, `src/core/buffer.js`, `src/core/conversation-manager.js`, `tests/fixtures/conversation-35.js`

### Functions & Methods

#### `loadConversation()` — function, line 19

* **Purpose:** Processes a sequence of messages, pairing them into turns, and populating both a structured conversational chain (`Chain`) and an indexed search structure (`BM25`).
* **Inputs:**
    * `conversation35` (Array<Message>): The source array containing the conversation history. It is assumed to be accessible in the scope and must contain message objects with a `content` property.
* **Outputs:** Object: An object containing two initialized structures: `{ chain: Chain, bm25: BM25 }`.
* **Behavioral Notes:**
    * The function processes messages strictly in pairs (User followed by Assistant).
    * It iterates over the input array with a step of 2 (`i += 2`).
    * If an odd number of messages exists at the end, the last message will be ignored because it cannot form a complete pair.
    * The `chain` and `bm25` instances are mutated by appending/adding nodes for every successful turn pair.

<details>
<summary>Source</summary>

```javascript
  function loadConversation() {
    const chain = new Chain();
    const bm25 = new BM25({ k1: 1.2, b: 0.4 });

    // Pair messages into turns
    for (let i = 0; i < conversation35.length - 1; i += 2) {
      const user = conversation35[i];
      const assistant = conversation35[i + 1];
      if (user && assistant) {
        const node = chain.appendTurn(user.content, assistant.content);
        bm25.add(node);
      }
    }

    return { chain, bm25 };
  }
```

</details>

</details>

## `tests/retrieval.test.js`

**86 lines** | 0 functions/classes

This source file is a comprehensive unit test suite designed to validate the functionality of core retrieval and indexing components within a knowledge graph or search system.

**Purpose:**
Its primary purpose is to ensure that the implemented algorithms—specifically BM25 scoring, Bloom Filter membership testing, and TF-IDF vectorization/similarity calculation—work correctly and interact reliably with the `Chain` data structure.

**What it Does:**
The file executes several tests:
1.  **BM25 Testing:** Verifies that the BM25 algorithm can accurately rank relevant nodes within a sequence of chained data (e.g., user/assistant dialogue).
2.  **Bloom Filter Testing:** Confirms that the Bloom Filter correctly determines membership for specific terms, allowing it to simulate access control or indexing checks on archived content.
3.  **TF-IDF Testing:** Validates the ability of TF-IDF to compute vector representations and rank multiple blocks of text based on semantic similarity relative to a query. It also tests the mathematical accuracy of cosine similarity and the serialization/deserialization process for vectors.

**Role in the Project:**
This file serves as critical quality assurance (QA) code. By rigorously testing these components, it ensures that the underlying search and retrieval mechanisms are robust, accurate, and ready to be integrated into a functional application layer.

## `tmp/fix-corruption.cjs`

**34 lines** | 0 functions/classes

This script serves as a specialized patch utility designed to correct structural corruption within a JavaScript file (`demo/ui/js/internal/app.js`).

**Purpose:** Its sole purpose is to fix a specific type of data duplication and syntax error that occurred in the target file, where a section was incorrectly duplicated (indicated by the pattern `}**`).

**What it does:** The script reads the entire content of the JavaScript file into memory. It locates the corruption point, then meticulously reconstructs the file's content by:
1.  Keeping all code that precedes the error.
2.  Manually inserting the necessary closing syntax (e.g., `}\n      }\n\n      `) to close the corrupted blocks correctly.
3.  Extracting and appending the correct continuation code from the second, redundant copy of the file's content.

**Role in the Project:** It acts as a build or maintenance step, ensuring that the core application logic within `app.js` remains syntactically valid and functional after an unexpected data corruption event has occurred.
